## Summary

Horror Entities is the simulation of the one real, hostile thing in the ship: a single segment-rig creature (one type, one instance, one lurk / stalk / reach / strike / retreat cycle) that turns Attention's abstract "the web has noticed you" into a body at a true position, gated by Attention's told-breach permission, that unfolds a limb onto a locked point and snaps, leaves honest marks and Graph footprints wherever it has been, and can be outwalked, hidden from or locked out but never fought or farmed. Read it if you touch any system that deals harm, writes a footprint, dips a lamp, places a sound on a body or draws a mark, because this document owns the tell composition (additive to Attention's 10-30 s window), the walk-out arithmetic that makes death fair, the truth-channel footprints a hallucination cannot hide, and the acceptance criteria that discharge the Horror half of Attention's, the Graph's, the Emitter's, Lighting's and Suit's must-repeats.

> **Quick reference** — Layer: `Feature` · Priority: `MVP` · Key deps: `Command & Simulation Layer (designed)`, `Life-Support Graph (designed)`, `Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query (designed in this run)`, `Attention System (designed in this run)`, `Suit & Equipment (designed in this run)`, `Ship Body & Interior Physics (designed in this run)`, `Ship Section & Layout Data Model (designed; `hull_segments_near` and `door_passage` published, HE-19; amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)`, `Save/Load & Versioned Format (designed in this run)`; downstream: `Adaptive Audio & Tells (designed)`, `Perception Filter (designed)`, `Sanity & Hallucination Rules (designed in this run)`

## Overview

Horror Entities is the simulation of the one kind of thing in the ship that is real, hostile and not a machine: a scene-tree-free, integer-only `SIM` block, `horror.state`, advanced by one divisor-5 step hook, `horror.step`, that holds the horror's true position, its behaviour state and the trail it leaves, and that turns Attention's abstract promise ("the web has noticed you") into a body in a room that the player can read, avoid and, if they do not, die to. In one sentence: **it is the web's physical consequence, a single segment-rig creature that lurks in the dark in CALM, stalks toward the light in a TELL, reaches at a point it has locked in a BREACH and then snaps, and that leaves honest marks wherever it has been.**

**The data layer.** The block holds one entity record at MVP (`HORROR_MAX_MVP` 1, type `horror_t01`, a placeholder renamed never aliased): body, section, integer base-px position with sub-pixel residue, behaviour state (`LURK`, `STALK`, `REACH`, `STRIKE`, `RETREAT`, plus `TRANSIT` while it passes through a hull), the locked strike point and the tick its telegraph ends, per-epoch strike, gouge and rupture counts, and a persisted counter for its one random stream, `"horror.tell"`. Beside the record sits a bounded, persisted **mark ring** (`MAX_MARKS`, wet iridescent smear, branching rime, radial hairlines, and the STRUCK burst that records a hit) and the published state of its **light disturbance** (L3) on nearby fixtures. Everything is derived from three reads and nothing else: Attention's committed `attention.view` (phase, locus, the permission predicate), the Emitter's `lux(ref)` for the seats it might prefer, and Layout's room graph for its movement. It writes the world in exactly three ways and no others: Class B footprint deposits through the Graph's `deposit_trace` (M17: CHILL, GOUGE and RUPTURE; never STRESS, which is Attention's alone), Class B `apply_suit_damage` on a body inside a telegraphed strike disc, and its own published state (marks, disturbances, `horror.view`) that Lighting and the presenters read. It is a registered Emitter subject (it can be lit and therefore seen) and it emits nothing, occludes nothing and casts no shadow. Its position is its own, moved by Body's pure `LocomotionStep` against Layout's solids; it is never a Body fact, a `CharacterBody2D` or a physics query. It holds no hit points, no aggro, threat, alert or awareness value of any kind, and no meter: the only thing it remembers is where it has been (marks) and what it has already done this epoch (counts).

**The player-facing effect.** The player does not meet a monster; they meet weather with intent. In CALM they may glimpse something long and low at the edge of their lamp, never nearer than a few tiles, and later find a wet streak or a forked white rime where it passed: the ship is not empty. A sighting in CALM is information and never a danger: the thing cannot hurt you in CALM (HE-11), but it tells you which body it lives in and that its trail and rime are fresh, and a player who reads that changes the route they take, whether the lamp is on and which job they do first. In a TELL, while Attention's creaking and filaments rise everywhere, a hairline appears on a hull face, a fixture nearby dips on its own irregular rhythm, and the thing comes in through the wall toward whoever is carrying light. When the TELL becomes a BREACH it closes at a pace the player can outwalk, plants its body, and unfolds one long jointed limb across the floor until the tip rests, motionless, on the spot where it has decided to strike. The player has one to three seconds to see the limb and step off that spot. If they do, nothing happens to them and the ship pays in scratched hull; if they do not, the suit takes a hit and the floor keeps a hairline burst where it landed (a miss leaves only the smear); the second hit opens a leak and staggers the body to a walk, and three such hits take the body down. The horror then retreats, and the cycle repeats until the breach subsides. Nothing the player carries can kill it, nothing it does can be farmed, and it leaves nothing behind but its trail.

**The contract this GDD owns.** Horror is the only gameplay system whose lethal acts are neither player-originated nor environmental, so it is where the Graph's exported requirement ("every non-player exogenous source must be preceded by that system's own Pillar 2 tell of 10-30 s", LSG:144) meets a body. Attention satisfies the 10-30 s at the breach level (AT-15); Horror **repeats** the permission predicate `exogenous_permitted(section)` at its three check points (REACH begin, STRIKE entry, every M17 RUPTURE or GOUGE call), adds two layers that are *additive and never a substitute* (the visible stalk and the REACH telegraph at a locked point), and publishes the facts a `TellAudit` replay needs. It discharges the Horror half of LSG:1063 and the crew-body scope of AT-15 R15. It is also the second GDD to land on the Emitter's "the beam reveals, never repels" rail (EM:89) and adopts it verbatim.

**What the game loses without it.** Without Horror, a breach on a clean ship is "nearly a non-event" and *commit* dominates *abort* (AT-11): restraint becomes decoration, Pillar 3 is hollow and the "chaos" half of Pillar 2 is empty. The Graph's exogenous footprint kinds have no caller, so a hidden real entity has no trail and Pillar 4's class (a) has no substance. Death-is-a-bitch (the user's decision) would arrive only through plumbing (decompression, cold), never through something that moves. Adaptive Audio would have no body to place a creak on, and Lighting's L3 disturbance would remain a stub. The core hypothesis (the boarding-and-breach loop is tense and absorbing for 30 minutes solo) needs a consequence that is felt at body scale.

**Index wording, reconciled.** The index bullet reads: "Segment-rig horrors with creep/unfold/snap behaviours, real occlusion and shadow; writes hidden-entity footprints (branching rime, radial hairlines, light disturbance) into the Graph footprint channel and submits light-disturbance modifiers to Lighting. MVP: one behaviour." This GDD honours every clause but one, and says so. *Segment-rig, creep / unfold / snap, one behaviour*: HE-1, HE-14, HE-15 (one cycle of one type; creep is LURK/STALK/RETREAT, unfold is REACH, snap is STRIKE). *Footprints into the Graph footprint channel*: the **physical half** (CHILL, hull-only GOUGE, RUPTURE) goes through M17; the **web-grammar half** (smear, branching rime, radial hairlines) is Horror's own persisted mark ring, because Failure & Trail draws only Graph descriptors (FT-31) and the Graph has no mark record (HE-27). *Light-disturbance modifiers to Lighting*: published state that Lighting reads, never a call (KRN:117; HE-35). *"Real occlusion and shadow"* is **read as visual opacity by draw order plus locomotion against Layout solids, and is not a `LightOccluder2D`, a shadow or an emission**: three designed GDDs (EM-20 (c), LO rail 10, FT-31) and the art bible's own recommendation (ART:296) rule the opposite, and a horror shadow would destroy the free stage-2 falsifier "a shadow with no occluder" (HE-2; orchestrator ruling R1). The index bullet, the routed thread, the art bible's "Physical honesty" bullet (ART:542) and PIPE:140 are listed as owed wording refreshes in Cross-References and are not edited here. **Until those rows land the art bible and this GDD contradict each other on the horror shadow, so the refresh rows (ART:542, ART 5.5, PIPE:140, IDX:35) are a BLOCKING precondition of `/asset-spec system:horror-entities` for the rig, exactly as C-HE-21 is for rime and hairlines (AC-HE-123); they are not advisory.** The creative-director affirmed the ruling at the CD-GDD-ALIGN gate (R1).

## Player Fantasy

**The fantasy in one sentence.** *"The light we need to keep the ship alive is the light that brings it, and we survive by reading the dark it walks through, never by fighting it."* This is not a scary-monster fantasy. It is a **prey-with-knowledge** fantasy that grows out of two locked facts that collide on purpose: every channelled repair verb is lit-only (Interaction `NOT_LIT`, IA-6) and the chest lamp is hands-free (user decision), so to fix the scrubber you must be lit; and the horror's one preference among living seats is the highest `lux_pm`, recomputed from physical light on every run with no stored value (HE-13). The core Horror decision of every BREACH is therefore the game's core fantasy compressed: *keep the ship breathing (light on, channel the repair) or keep the body breathing (lamp off, close a door, step out of the reach, pod-hop).* The Core Fantasy line "the ship is alive because we keep it alive, minute by minute" becomes literal: **the minute you spend keeping it alive is a minute you are visible.**

**Framing (orchestrator ruling: creative-director framing A, with B as tonal layer).** Direct framing, **"Read the Dark"**: *it comes to the light; I read its trail and choose when to be seen.* Every rule in this document can be checked against that sentence, it puts the player's competence in reading rather than in reflexes, and it turns the lit-only repair rule into the central dilemma instead of a friction. Framing **B, "The Web's Hand"** (the horror is the web reaching into the ship through this limb because the crew has earned its attention) is adopted as the fiction behind A only: it justifies why the thing is "of the web" and why it casts no shadow (see the fiction gloss below). Framing **C, "The Hunter"** (something is tracking us) is rejected: a hunter remembers, which requires a stored pursuit or last-seen value, which is a hidden aggro number and fails Pillar 1's own design test. *(Framing A with B as fiction, and the rejection of C, were ratified without change at the CD-GDD-ALIGN gate; later edits must not drift from "the minute you spend keeping it alive is a minute you are visible".)*

**The anchor moment: a limb entering the cone before the body.** The player is crouched at a derelict's failing scrubber, channel bar half full, chest lamp lit because the verb demands it. At the edge of the cone, something thin and segmented arrives first: one long jointed limb, iridescent only where the beam touches it, laid along the floor toward them. The body is still in the dark. The player has a choice that is legible, not a reflex test: finish the channel (the bar shows the ticks left) or abandon it and walk out of the reach. If they walk, nothing touches them; the scrubber is still broken. The feeling is *I lived, and the ship paid.*

| Tick (from TELL entry) | Attention (owned there, read-only here) | Horror (this GDD) | What the player can perceive |
|---|---|---|---|
| T = 0 | TELL begins; `tell_epoch` +1; locus recorded (AT-14) | The entity leaves LURK; if the locus body is not its body it leaves through the hull (egress mark) and enters `TRANSIT`, even when its own body is unloaded (HE-20) | Attention's global tell (beat on every lit region, filaments, dust turning): "it noticed" |
| T >= `INGRESS_DELAY_TICKS` − `INGRESS_MARK_LEAD_TICKS` (mark), `INGRESS_DELAY_TICKS` (body) (240 / 300 [PH]) | TELL continues | Ingress through a hull segment one room hop from the brightest living seat's room, never into a seat's room, never inside `LURK_STANDOFF_PX` (HE-20) | The first local sign: a radial hairline on a hull face, rime branching from it, a fixture nearby dipping on its own rhythm |
| `INGRESS_DELAY_TICKS` to tell end | The tell length was drawn in [`TELL_DRAW_LO`, `TELL_DRAW_HI`], never below `TELL_MIN_TICKS` | STALK at `V_CREEP_MPX`; never within `LURK_STANDOFF_PX`; no RUPTURE, no GOUGE, no harm (HE-12) | A visible stalk of at least `TELL_MIN_TICKS − INGRESS_DELAY_TICKS` = 300 ticks at the placeholders, typically 600 to 1500 |
| BREACH entry | `exogenous_permitted(section)` true in `locus_body` | Approach at `V_HUNT_MPX` toward the highest-`lux_pm` living seat | "Controlled panic, more things moving, not the screen fighting the player" (ART 2.8) |
| REACH start | n/a | The strike point is LOCKED (non-tracking); `UNFOLD_TICKS` drawn in [`UNFOLD_LO`, `UNFOLD_HI`] = [95, 180] ticks [PH] (HE-14, HE-F5) | **The limb enters the cone.** About 1.6 to 3 seconds of unfold, then a held stillness |
| REACH end | The predicate is re-checked | STRIKE resolves only if `exogenous_permitted` still holds; every living seat in the disc takes `STRIKE_BP` (HE-15) | A snap, the touch-down smear and Audio's positional strike cue. Strike one is witnessed by the strike itself, by Suit's integrity reading (the STRAINED rung is inert and carries no witness, by Suit's design) and, if a seat was hit, by the STRUCK hairline burst laid at P that a miss never leaves; the first physical consequence is strike two (leak, seam jet, CRITICAL, stagger, walk-only) |

**Worked moment (single player, derelict B, placeholders).** The player is at 40 % of a scrubber channel with the lamp on; `lux_pm` reads 1000 because the owner stands in their own light (EM-21). A TELL begins at T = 0 with a drawn length of 1200. Ingress is at T = 300, one room away; the hairline and rime are visible through the doorway; the body crosses the door frame near T = 700. BREACH begins at T = 1200 and the horror closes at `V_HUNT_MPX` 1500 (1.5 px per tick). It closes from the standoff (192 px) to `REACH_RANGE_PX` (96 px) in 64 ticks, so its REACH starts on the run grid at T = 1265; `UNFOLD_TICKS` draws 135. The player sees the limb at T = 1265, abandons the channel at T = 1319 (54 ticks of reaction, `REACT_ALLOW_TICKS_HORROR`) and walks at `V_WALK` 2: 72 px in 36 ticks on a cardinal octant, so (with the kernel's one-tick command application, KRN D.4: the command issued at T = 1319 first moves them in tick 1320, and the 36th step lands in tick 1355) they stand outside the disc at T = 1355, 45 ticks before the snap at T = 1400 (T = 1356 and 44 ticks on a diagonal octant) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: recomputed under HE-F5's tick-indexing convention, G4)*. No harm. One wet smear sits where the tip rested; if the tip was within `HULL_CONTACT_PX` of a hull face, radial hairlines and a hull GOUGE mark the wall. The scrubber is still broken.

**Explicitly not a tell length.** The kernel's illustrative `rng_int(600, 1800)` on the stream name `"horror.tell"` (KRN D.10) is an example, not Horror's tell and not Horror's REACH range. The Pillar 2 requirement of 10 to 30 seconds (LSG:144) is met by Attention's window (AT-15); Horror's stalk and REACH are **additive layers and never a substitute**, and nothing in this GDD shortens Attention's tell (HE-9). The stream `"attention.tell"` is Attention's.

**Pillar text served (verbatim) and the design tests as they bite Horror.**

| Pillar | Text (game-concept) | How this GDD serves it |
|---|---|---|
| **P2 Dread Before Chaos** (primary) | "Every breach is preceded by a tell a careful player could have perceived (sound, light, gauge drift, hull stress)." Design test: *breach fires at a hidden threshold vs. breach preceded by a 10-30 second escalating tell -> the tell.* | Every lethal act lands inside Attention's told breach (AT-15) and is further preceded by the visible stalk and by the REACH telegraph at a locked point. Three told layers, never one. |
| **P1 The World Never Lies** (primary) | "Pipes leak, frost forms, air hisses; the physical world is ground truth." Design test: *a hidden stat vs. a visibly leaking pipe -> the pipe.* | The horror is a SIM entity at a true position that leaves true marks, true Graph footprints and true light disturbance. No aggro or threat number exists, stored or derived. |
| **P3 The Web Watches** (serves) | "Attention is shown in the world (creaking, flicker, filament movement), never as a hidden meter. Restraint and stealth are real strategies." | The horror is Attention's consequence, never a second meter (AT-2). Its CALM, TELL and BREACH behaviour is gated by `attention.view.phase` only; going dark is real counterplay. |
| **P4 Sanity Rewrites Reality** (serves) | "A hidden real entity still leaves its P1/P2 footprint (sound, frost, hull stress, light disturbance) — the hallucination hides the body, never the trail." | Horror owns the web-grammar half of that guarantee: marks, disturbance state and Graph footprints are bit-identical whether or not any viewer's body-hide is active (HE-32). |
| **P5 Deeper Is Richer, Loss Is Not Total** (light) | "Losing the home-ship or a crewmate costs ship and body, never knowledge." | Death by horror restores from the newest autosave (SL-7); what the player learned about reach, rime and light survives in their head and, at Vertical Slice, in the Codex through stable `type_id` and `tell_id` enumerations. |

*Design test (this system):* **a horror that reacts to a number nobody can see vs. a horror that reacts to light the player chose to show -> the light.** *Secondary test (Pillar 2 at body scale):* **a strike that lands from the dark vs. a limb that enters the cone before the body at a point that no longer moves -> the limb.** The tests decide four recurring debates: keep a "last target" so the horror follows the player who switched the lamp off (no: turning the lamp off removes the seat from preference on the next run; memory would be a hidden number); let the strike track a moving player for "fairness of difficulty" (no: the point locks at REACH start); let it charge in CALM (no: CALM is non-lethal by construction and it never comes inside `LURK_STANDOFF_PX`); make the beam repel it, or make it shrug the beam off to stay scary (neither: the beam only reveals, EM:89 adopted verbatim).

**How the fantasy deepens under hallucination.** The horror's truth is its trail. Because a real entity always writes marks, Graph footprints and disturbance state and a phantom never does, low sanity turns "is it there?" into "is the floor marked?". The player stops reading the body and starts reading the ship, which is exactly the Pillar 1 and 4 tension the concept calls "the game". Horror's obligations in each class are small and absolute.

| Class | What the player is shown | The channel of truth that remains | Horror's obligation |
|---|---|---|---|
| (a) phantom entity (invented) | The Filter draws a horror rig from a phantom pose stream; it may lunge | No told breach precedes it (TellAudit records nothing, AT E-53); no rime, smear or hairline; no disturbance; no Graph footprint; the suit rung does not move | Keep `horror.view` real-only; the rig presenter accepts any pose stream (HE-40) |
| (a) hidden real entity (Vertical Slice) | The Filter undraws the body for one seat | Marks, disturbance, M17 writes and suit damage are bit-identical with or without the hide; a saner crewmate may still see the body | Keep every real entity registered with a true anchor; read no perception state (H5, CR-12) |
| (b) false system state | Gauges and sprites lie | Horror touches nothing in (b); its RUPTURE and GOUGE surface as true Graph descriptors that Failure & Trail draws (FT-31) | None |
| (c) crewmate as horror (Vertical Slice) | The Filter substitutes the horror rig for a crewmate's body, driven by their true velocity | The true beam: "a horror with a player's beam is your friend" (ART:292); a real horror emits nothing, so the beam stays a perfect discriminator | Real horrors never carry or emit light (HE-2) |
| (d) phantom salvage | Not Horror's | n/a | None |

Three falsifiers cost the player something, as Pillar 4 demands (checking costs time, risk or trust): **look at the floor** (time: in the dark you must light it, which draws the real one), **check the phase** (risk: is Attention's tell running?), **ask the crewmate** (trust; co-op). The no-shadow rule adds a fourth, free falsifier at stage 2: *a shadow with no occluder is never the web: it is you.*

**Fiction gloss for the no-shadow rule (creative-director; adopted at the CD-GDD-ALIGN gate; to be written into ART:542 as part of the owed refresh, which gates the rig's asset-spec).** *"The web does not stop light; it only takes it."* Web-matter is real: it stands on the deck, scars the hull, leaves rime where it passed and moves dust. But light goes through it as through a filament: the beam shows its surface as a thin iridescent band and continues to whatever is behind it. Nothing that belongs to the web casts a shadow, so a shadow with no occluder is never the web.

**Reference techniques, adopt and reject.**

| Reference | Technique | Verdict | Where it lands |
|---|---|---|---|
| *Alien: Isolation* | One persistent, physically present entity, never despawned | ADOPT | HE-1, HE-20 |
| *Alien: Isolation* | A director's "menace gauge" steering the alien | REJECT: guard rail 1 (no threat value); Attention is the only director (AT-2) | HE-7 |
| *Alien: Isolation* | Grab-instakill on contact | REJECT: three told strikes | HE-15 |
| *Amnesia: The Dark Descent* | "You cannot fight it, only hide" | ADOPT in spirit | HE-38 |
| *Amnesia* | Sanity drains from looking at the monster | REJECT here: Sanity owns exposure; Horror computes none | HE-40 |
| *Amnesia* | The monster vanishes when out of sight | REJECT: a SIM entity at a true position; dormancy freezes, never despawns | HE-21 |
| *Darkwood* | Light attracts; the world carries marks of what passed | ADOPT | HE-13, HE-27 |
| *Darkwood* | Melee combat, crafted weapons | REJECT: anti-pillar; HORROR takes no verb | HE-38 |
| *Barotrauma* | Creatures that hole the hull; crew patch it | ADOPT | HE-16 |
| *Barotrauma* | Turrets, weapons, creature loot | REJECT | HE-38 |
| *Project Zomboid* | Death is final and common ("it happens") | ADOPT (user decision) | HE-15 |
| *Project Zomboid* | A killable horde as content | REJECT: "horrors are avoided and survived, not farmed" | HE-38 |
| *Pandorum* (film) | A hands-free chest lamp in a dark derelict; hunters in the margins of the light | ADOPT (user decision) | HE-13 |
| *Pandorum* | Fighting the hunters hand to hand | REJECT: no attack input (PC-12) | HE-38 |
| *Beyond the Aquila Rift* | The kindness of the lie: the wrongness is in what you are shown | ADOPT, routed to Sanity and the Filter | HE-32 |
| *Annihilation* (film) | The shimmer: iridescence as the signature of something not of our world | ADOPT: a band only under light, never a fill | Visual/Audio Requirements |
| *The Thing* | The body as wrongness, imitation of crew | REJECT for Horror: class (c) is a render substitution owned by the Filter | HE-40 |
| Souls-like games | A long, readable wind-up at a fixed point | ADOPT | HE-14 |
| Action games | Hit-stop, screen shake, damage numbers | REJECT: the camera refuses horror callers (PIPE C1, C9) | HE-38 |

**Tone and vocabulary.** Patient, physical, quiet; closer to weather with intent than to a monster with a personality. It never roars at the camera, never poses, never "spots" anyone; it arrives in increments the player could have seen. Used in this document and in any player-facing text: reach, unfold, snap, strike, lurk, stalk, retreat, ingress, egress, "it noticed" (Attention's mood), "it comes to the light", smear, branching rime, radial hairline, "the trail", "the floor remembers", dip (for a lamp). Never used, outside a quotation or a negation: `threat`, `aggro`, `alert`, `awareness`, `suspicion`, `hate`, `detect` as horror state; `enemy`, `mob`, `boss`, `spawn` (use ingress), `kill`, `damage` (use `strike_bp`); `flicker` of a fixture (use dip); `meter`, `bar`, `gauge`, `indicator` near a horror or proximity; `real occlusion`, `cast shadow`, `occlude the beam`. These double as the stale-token lint (Acceptance Criteria).

**Emotional arc across a breach cycle (mapped to Attention's phases).**

| Phase | Intended emotion | Horror's contribution | Flow note |
|---|---|---|---|
| CALM | Unease, then orientation: "it lives in this body, and its trail is fresh" (information, never danger: CALM is non-lethal by construction, HE-11) | LURK: may be seen at a distance and cannot harm; marks age; disturbance dips near it | Flow of work with a background tax of glances at the floor, paid in choices (route, lamp, which job first), not in fear of a hit |
| TELL | Dread, rising | STALK visible; ingress hairline and rime; disturbance near it | An intentional flow break: the player reprioritises |
| BREACH | Controlled panic | Approach, REACH telegraph, STRIKE at a locked point | Choice under time, never a reflex test (the walk-out bound) |
| SUBSIDING | Relief with residue | RETREAT; no new REACH starts | Recovery |
| After | Reading the aftermath | Marks: where it came from, where it touched the hull | Discovery: "it came in through there" |

**Anti-pillar guard rails (restated as lints in Acceptance Criteria).**
1. **Not a combat game.** No horror HP, no kill state, no loot, no XP, no kill counter, no hit feedback for tool use, no player damage output; HORROR subjects take no verb at MVP (`INVALID_TARGET`, IA-39); the beam only reveals.
2. **No reward path.** Nothing drops, nothing unlocks, no codex entry is earned by approaching a horror at MVP. Surviving costs the ship (footprints), never pays.
3. **Not intentional PvP.** A horror's lethal act on a crew body is inside the told breach and audited by TellAudit (AT-15 R15); friendly harm stays Interaction plus Sanity; Horror never routes harm between players.
4. **Not a freeform builder, no authored story.** Horror places nothing and narrates nothing; Derelict Authoring "places no entity" (DA:101); ingress is derived from Attention's locus and Emitter reads, never a trigger volume ("monster closets: REJECT").
5. **No win state.** The horror cannot be removed from the world; it only retreats.
6. **Death is lethal (user), and is not Horror's.** Horror owns the act and its tells; DOWNED and DEAD are Suit's (SE-24); restore from the newest autosave is Save/Load's (SL-7). No death screen, no beat.

**Validation targets (creative-director, `[PLACEHOLDER]`, measured in MVP playtests of the concept's 30-minute solo session).** (1) *Fair death:* at least 80 % of horror-caused deaths are described unprompted, or on one neutral question, with a cause the player saw ("I saw the arm", "I stayed in the light too long"); below 60 % the telegraph or stalk is failing. (2) *Not an alarm monster:* in at least 70 % of sessions the player sees the horror or its fresh marks during CALM at least once. (3) *The dilemma exists:* at least half of playtesters, asked "what did you do when it came?", describe turning the lamp off, abandoning a repair or closing a door; if the dominant answer is "I ran", target preference by light is not legible. (4) *No hidden-number belief:* zero playtesters describe the horror "remembering" or "locking on" across a lamp-off. (5) *Marks read as web, not ship:* a blind classification of rime against coolant frost is correct at least 90 % at k = 1, `k_auto` and `k_auto + 2`. (6) *Teeth:* in a replay of a breach on a clean ship, the player loses at least one repair's worth of progress or one suit rung in at least 60 % of breaches they choose to commit through lit. (7) *A CALM sighting changes behaviour:* at least 60 % of testers who saw the horror or a fresh mark in CALM report, unprompted or on one neutral question ("did seeing it change what you did next?"), that it changed what they did (the route, the lamp, or which job they took first). If this fails, the first lever is `RETURN_HOME` 0 (OQ-1); HE-11 is not relaxed, because CALM stays non-lethal to protect Pillar 2.

## Detailed Design

### Core Rules

Rule ids are `HE-n`; formulas `HE-F n` (Formulas section, ids reserved in the index at the end of this section); edge cases `E-HE-n`; acceptance criteria `AC-HE-n`; conflicts `C-HE-n`. Every Horror-owned number is `[PLACEHOLDER]` and a registry candidate unless marked CITED (a locked name quoted verbatim with its registry value) or DERIVED. The Graph's `RUPTURE_HP`, `BREACH_HP`, `FOOTPRINT_MAX_Q`, `FOOTPRINT_MAX_GOUGE_BP`, `STRESS_HOLD_STEPS` and `C_TILE` are Life-Support Graph registry constants (registered 2026-10-27), cited with the registry value and their LSG line and never restated as Horror's own. Rules state observable behaviour and contracts; implementation patterns are marked "-> ADR candidate". "Run" means one execution of the `horror.step` hook (every 5th tick).

#### Group A — Identity, scope and ownership

**HE-1. One type, one instance, one behaviour cycle.** *Rule.* MVP ships exactly one type row, `horror_t01` (a placeholder name, renamed never aliased: floor-attached, three-branch, about 1.5 times crewmate height, one extensible reach limb; the art bible's outer-ring working target, ART:546), at most `HORROR_MAX_MVP` 1 resident record, and one behaviour cycle: **LURK (CALM) / STALK (TELL) → REACH (telegraph) → STRIKE → RETREAT**, plus `TRANSIT` while it passes through a hull. The motion vocabulary of the art matrix maps onto it as *creep* (LURK, STALK, RETREAT), *unfold* (REACH) and *snap* (STRIKE). "One behaviour" is **one cycle of one type, never three monsters.** More types, per-ring rosters, filament-riders, hidden-entity wiring, `repel`, flares and decoys, and co-op replication are Vertical Slice and are named in Open Questions and in the block shape only. *Why.* The concept's MVP item 4 is "Attention with one threshold and one horror behaviour"; AT-11 makes a told breach without teeth a named design failure (cry-wolf; commit dominates abort), and the art bible names the reach as the signature image (ART 5.4). *Cites.* AT:197, AT:373, CON MVP definition, ART:546.

**HE-2. A real horror is a true SIM entity that touches nothing optical.** *Rule.* A horror has a true integer position in its own block, is solid to Layout solids and to nothing else (it passes through crewmates and they through it, BDY-17), is drawn in the single Y-sorted layer so its sprite hides what stands behind it, is hidden by walls and by darkness, and is revealed only by light. It **contributes no occluder, casts no shadow, emits nothing, and never changes the beam, any subject's `lux`, `lit` or any emitter field.** "Real occlusion" in the index row and the routed thread is read as *visual opacity by draw order plus locomotion against Layout solids*, never as a `LightOccluder2D`. Nothing a horror does is a light event except the disturbance state of HE-35, which Lighting reads and executes. *Why.* Three designed GDDs decide it (EM-20 (c) and AC-24; Lighting rail 10 "Horrors cast no shadow and emit nothing" and AC-LO-16; Failure & Trail FT-31) and the art bible's own recommendation agrees (ART:296); a horror shadow would destroy the free stage-2 sanity falsifier "a shadow with no occluder" and with it Pillar 4's "no lie is unfalsifiable". "Displace dust" from the art bible is retained in spirit through the marks of HE-30, not through the Attention Presenter's shared dust process. *(orchestrator ruling: option A, affirmed by the creative-director at the CD-GDD-ALIGN gate as R1; the fiction gloss "the web does not stop light; it only takes it" is adopted; the wording refreshes are owed rows, never edits here.)* **Gate.** Until the owed ART:542, ART 5.5, PIPE:140 and IDX:35 refresh rows land, the art bible and this GDD contradict each other; those rows are a BLOCKING precondition of `/asset-spec` for the rig (AC-HE-123), as C-HE-21 is for rime. *Cites.* EM:208, LO:151, FT:199, ART:296, ART:542.

**HE-3. Block and hook identity.** *Rule.* Block `horror.state`: class `SIM`, `persist` true, `persist_scope` WORLD, scope SHARED, `repl_hint` SHARED_SUMMARY, `schema_version` 1, `supported_versions` [1], `restore_after` `[layout.state, graph.state, emitter.state, attention.state]`. Hook `horror.step`: divisor 5, offset 0, `after: [attention.step, emitter.place]` (the must-repeats of Attention (AT:373), the Emitter (EM AC-60) and the kernel (KRN:201), exactly as ruled; C-HE-20 closed). A hook id that is unknown at boot fails boot (CR-3, no alias concept); grep of `horror.step`, `horror.state` and `horror.view` across the neighbours before closing found no collision. The read view is `horror.view` (HE-37). *Why.* `body.integrate` precedes `horror.step` on every Horror tick through `emitter.place`'s own `after` list (EM:104: `after: [body.integrate, graph.propagate.failure]`) and the CR-3 total order, so the same-tick seat read that the exact walk-out arithmetic of HE-F2 needs holds without extending the ruled list; Horror writes nothing Body reads, so no cycle can form. A one-tick-stale seat read would let an escaping seat read up to 2 px closer than it is. *Cites.* KRN:71, KRN:201, EM:104, AT:125-131, BDY-8.

**HE-4. Cadence: one run is one rig pose, and what Horror reads between Attention's runs.** *Rule.* `horror.step` runs on ticks that are multiples of 5 (12 Hz), so one run is exactly one 12 fps rig pose (PIPE row 14). Attention and the Graph run at divisor 6; LCM(5, 6) = 30, so the two hooks coincide only when `T mod 30 = 0`, and CR-3's `after` orders the pair only on those ticks. On every other run Horror reads `attention.view` **as committed by Attention's most recent run**; this is a reading of committed state, not a new kernel rule. Presentation interpolates `pos_prev` to `pos_curr` across the five ticks of a run; the rig pose is stepped, never tweened. Every Horror duration is authored as an integer count of runs and published in ticks as `runs × 5` (`HORROR_RUN_TICKS` 5, DERIVED from the divisor); a duration that is not a multiple of 5 is a data-load lint failure (it would silently round up to the next run, a 1 to 4 tick bias that matters at the `UNFOLD_LO` bound). **The lint covers every SIM-clock duration** (`UNFOLD_LO`, `UNFOLD_HI`, `STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS`, `INGRESS_DELAY_TICKS`, `RETURN_DELAY_TICKS`, `LURK_DWELL_TICKS` and the rest of the type row); it **exempts the L3 disturbance lengths `L3_DUR_LO` and `L3_DUR_HI`** (18 and 30), which are published to Lighting in ticks and are presentation-timed (LO-19, the gutter grammar), not run-quantised: a disturbance *starts* on a run, its length does not round to one (C-HE-29). *Cites.* PIPE:303, KRN:71, HE-F5.

**HE-5. Data-driven type row.** *Rule.* Every per-type value lives in a data file keyed by `type_id`; behaviour code is generic over the row and contains no behavioural constant. A row carries: `v_creep_mpx`, `v_hunt_mpx`, `r_snap_px`, `reach_range_px`, `hull_contact_px`, `strike_bp`, `strike_cooldown_ticks`, `strike_hold_runs`, `unfold_lo`, `unfold_hi`, `lurk_standoff_px`, `lurk_dwell_ticks`, the L3 constants, the mark and rig asset ids. The data lint refuses any field named `damage`, `accuracy`, `armour`, `dps`, `xp`, `unlock`, `health`, `hp`, `kill`, `loot`, `threat`, `aggro` or `hate` (Horror's own harm field is `strike_bp`, a Suit-scale amount, never `damage`). *Why.* Coding standards (gameplay values are data-driven) and the Item DB's lint AP-2; the field list is also the anti-combat guard (HE-38). *Cites.* IDB:97.

**HE-6. Boundaries.** *Rule.* Horror does not read or write Attention's level, does not call `set_beacon`, does not schedule a breach, does not add a Graph footprint kind, does not read any `PERCEPTION_OUT` block, does not move, push or stagger a player (the stagger is the Controller's response to the Suit rung), places no content, narrates nothing, calls no physics query, owns no sound, mix or cue name, owns no HUD element, and holds no stimulus tracker, accumulator, "awareness", "alert", "threat" or "aggro" value, persisted or derived. *Why.* Guard rail 1: absorbing Attention is the most likely way this GDD drifts; the grep of this document for those words outside a negation returns zero (Acceptance Criteria lint).

#### Group B — Permission, tell composition and the audit

**HE-7. Phase-sourced behaviour, no stored drive.** *Rule.* Every transition between LURK and STALK, and every harm gate, is a function of `attention.view` fields only: `phase`, `tell_epoch`, `tell_outcome`, `locus_section_id`, `exogenous_permitted(section)`, `breach_enter_T`, `phase_enter_T`. The target (HE-13) is recomputed from physical `lux` on every run and is **not persisted**. The only things the record remembers are where it is, what state it is in since when, what it has already done this `tell_epoch` (counts), the locked point of a REACH in flight, and the stream counter. **Debug surface** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: a lethal AI cannot be playtested blind)*: each run also publishes a **non-persisted, non-hashed record `horror.view.debug`** on Horror's registered diagnostics channel (Debug Overlay DO-12, dev builds only), holding the current target ref or ANCHOR with each candidate's `lux_pm`, the goal room and the surrogate goal, the next hop, the transition guard that fired (1 to 7) and the HE-14 condition (a) to (f) that failed last, `hold_runs`, `blocked_runs`, `horror_wedged`, and the `linked` and `connected` results for the nearest seat *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. It is derived from the run, never read back: **no SIM rule, guard, formula or hash input may read `horror.view.debug`** (a lint, AC-HE-135), it is absent from `horror.state`, and it is never an accessibility or player surface (UI Requirements). *Why.* AT-2 (one level for the world), Pillar 1 (no hidden stat), guard rail 1. *Cites.* AT:139, AT:214-232.

**HE-8. The permission predicate, repeated at three check points.** *Rule.* Every web-originated harm call (M17 RUPTURE, M17 GOUGE, `apply_suit_damage`) is made only if `exogenous_permitted(section)` holds **at the call tick**, where for a bodily hit `section` is Body's `actor_section(seat)` (crew-body scope, AT-15 R15; not the Graph's `section_of`) and for a hull deposit it is the section of the hull segment. The three check points are: (1) **REACH begin** (HE-14 condition c), (2) **STRIKE entry** (HE-15), (3) **every M17 RUPTURE or GOUGE call** (HE-16, HE-29). **CHILL is a non-harm footprint, permitted in any phase, bounded by HE-F4 and HE-31** (C-HE-28). A REACH may *begin* only in BREACH (not SUBSIDING); an in-flight REACH resolves only if the predicate still holds at its last tick, otherwise it fizzles with no harm, no hairlines, no GOUGE or RUPTURE (a touch-down smear already laid at the hold stage stays: it is true; a fizzle before the hold stage lays none) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. Horror issues no STRESS (Attention's alone, AT-11). The registered formula `tell_window_contract` is the audit: for every harm event `e` there is an epoch `k` with `tell_start_k + tell_len_k <= t_e < subside_end_k`, `tell_len_k >= TELL_MIN_TICKS` and `section_e` in `locus_body_k` (the epoch's locus body in force at `t_e`: recorded at TELL entry, replaced only by an AT-14 fallback that TellAudit records as LOCUS_FALLBACK); zero violations over a replay. This GDD **repeats** AT-15; it does not restate a different rule. *Cites.* AT:211, REG `tell_window_contract`, AT:794-810.

**HE-9. Tell composition: additive layers, never a substitute.** *Rule.* The Graph's 10-30 s requirement (LSG:144, LSG:1063) is met by **Attention's window** (AT-15); nothing in Horror shortens it, defers it or replaces it. Horror adds layers on top, and a harm path that skips one of them is a defect:

| Layer | Owner | Duration | What the player can perceive | Present for |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| L0 Attention tell | Attention | `TELL_MIN_TICKS` 600 to `TELL_MAX_TICKS` 1800 | beats, filaments, dust, lamp gutter, audio (Attention's) | every harm path |
| L1 Presence and stalk | Horror | from ingress (at least `INGRESS_DELAY_TICKS` after TELL entry) or from TELL entry if already resident, to the first REACH | the entity in light, marks, disturbance dips, audio facts | bodily and hull paths |
| L2 REACH telegraph | Horror | `UNFOLD_TICKS` per strike, 95 to 180 ticks | the limb entering the cone before the body, tip resting on the locked point | every strike |
| L3 Footprint trail | Horror | continuous, any phase | smear, rime, hairlines | all |
| L4 Hull "scrape" | Horror | at least `min_gap` (> `STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS`, HE-F15) before a RUPTURE, because the RUPTURE needs the segment in `gouged_seg_ids` from an earlier strike *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | a GOUGE with radial hairlines on the same segment (HE-16) | RUPTURE only |

The kernel's illustrative `rng_int(600, 1800)` on the stream name `"horror.tell"` (KRN D.10) is **not** Horror's tell length and not Horror's REACH range; the stream `"attention.tell"` is Attention's. Horror draws from `"horror.tell"` only the REACH length (HE-14). *Cites.* LSG:144, AT:211, KRN D.10, reflexion entry on examples read as contracts.

**HE-10. TellAudit and the crew-body scope.** *Rule.* Horror publishes in `horror.view` the facts a `TellAudit` replay needs for every harm event (HE-37: `strike_T`, the seats hit, the hull segments gouged or ruptured, the `tell_epoch` it ran in) and submits no harm that the predicate would reject. A lethal act on a crew body outside a told breach is exactly the ambush Pillar 2 forbids, and Horror's stalk is additive and never a substitute. A restored CALM save never contains a lethal horror adjacent to a seat (CALM is non-lethal by construction, HE-11), so autosave needs no Horror-specific gate (SL-5 unchanged); if HE-11 is ever relaxed, SL-5 must be revisited. *Cites.* AT:211, SL:190.

#### Group C — The behaviour cycle

**HE-11. LURK: the non-lethal CALM presence.** *Rule.* In CALM a resident record is in LURK in its current body. It loiters at `V_CREEP_MPX` between waypoints drawn from Layout `anchor_geometry`: it dwells `LURK_DWELL_TICKS` at a waypoint, then the next waypoint is the lowest-hash candidate (`he_hash(entity_id, dwell_index, anchor_local_key, SALT_WP)`, HE-42; the chosen waypoint is held in the persisted `goal_wp` and the dwell start in `dwell_start_T`, HE-41) among waypoints in loaded sections of its body that are at least `LURK_STANDOFF_PX` from every living seat. It **never starts a move that brings it, or leaves it, nearer than `LURK_STANDOFF_PX` to a living seat** (the invariant is on the horror's own motion: a seat that walks toward it closes the gap itself, and the horror then steps away along the room graph; if cornered it holds still and does nothing). **Every `LURK_STANDOFF_PX` comparison in this rule (the standoff, the step-away and the waypoint filter) applies only to a seat for which `linked(horror, seat)` holds (HE-19)**: a room-level test that does not change while both stand in their rooms, so a horror at a doorway cannot oscillate between the standoff and step-away rules on a jamb graze, and a seat two short rooms away in open line of sight is linked; a seat in an unlinked room behind a wall does not push it away and is not a reason to hold still *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. It deposits no RUPTURE and no GOUGE and applies no suit damage; it may be seen, lays marks (HE-30), makes CHILL deposits (HE-27) and disturbance dips (HE-35). A seat may walk through it (no collision, BDY-17); that costs nothing and is a free moment of inspection. Its transitions out of LURK come **only from `attention.view.phase`**, never from the player's light, noise or distance. *Why.* Attention's "CALM is never known-safe" (AT:92, AT:373) is a claim about the phase (a TELL can begin at any time), not a claim that this presence can hurt, and this rule does not imply one: the CALM presence is provably harmless (no harm call, the standoff, the step-away) and **what a CALM sighting is worth is information**: which body it lives in (`RETURN_HOME`), and that its trail and rime are fresh (stage-0 marks), so the player may change the route, the lamp or the first job. It is not made dangerous to keep the dread up, because a harm in CALM would arrive with no tell and break Pillar 2; the standoff and the phase-only transitions also keep it from becoming a timer or a second meter (AT-2). Validation target (7) measures whether the information is used (AC-HE-122); if it is not, the first lever is `RETURN_HOME` 0 (OQ-1), never relaxing this rule. *Cites.* AT:92, AT:373.

**HE-12. STALK: creep in a TELL, hunt in a BREACH.** *Rule.* On a TELL entry (a `tell_epoch` different from the record's `last_seen_tell_epoch`; **this comparison is the first evaluation for every record in every run, ahead of the dormancy guard**, States and Transitions guard 2) a record whose body holds the locus goes LURK → STALK; a record in another body goes LURK → TRANSIT (HE-20), **even when its own body is unloaded** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. In a TELL, STALK moves toward the target (HE-13) at `V_CREEP_MPX` and stops at `LURK_STANDOFF_PX` from every living seat that is `linked` to it (HE-19) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; it deposits no RUPTURE and no GOUGE and calls no `apply_suit_damage`. In BREACH it moves at `V_HUNT_MPX` and closes to `REACH_RANGE_PX` of the target point. STALK is one state; the speed is a function of `attention.view.phase`. **A TELL abort** (the phase leaves TELL with an aborted `tell_outcome`) returns STALK to LURK with no strike and no mark burst, and **cancels an outbound TRANSIT** (HE-20) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. Entering SUBSIDING sends STALK to RETREAT. *Why.* Pillar 2 (the stalk is the additive earlier layer) and Pillar 3. *Cites.* AT:373, AT-16.

**HE-13. Target from physical light.** *Rule.* Candidates are living seats (not DOWNED, DEAD or IN_POD per the Controller's `loco_state`) whose `actor_section` is in `locus_body` (TELL, BREACH, SUBSIDING) or in the record's own body (CALM). One `lux(ref)` read per candidate per run, zero probes. **The target rule, stated plainly** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: a candidate is *lit* when its total light `lux_pm(s) >= LIT_ON_PM` (150, CITED), a stateless comparison on the same quantity the argmax uses (the comparison is made fresh every run and nothing is stored; there is no off-threshold and no hysteresis, as for the Emitter's `probe` `lit_on`). **Horror does not gate on the Emitter's `lit` bit**: that bit is a hysteresis on `act_lux_pm`, which excludes the owner's own beam (EM-2, EM-21, EM AC-16a), so a lone player in the dark with the lamp on reads `lux_pm` 1000 but `act_lux_pm` 0 and would never be a candidate; the Emitter's own wording ("Horror sees a lit player", EM line 685) is the contract. Any light that raises `lux_pm` counts, including a fixture (the self-powered status glow of a docked pod's dock-room prop is an ordinary lit fixture by user ruling X2, so a seat at the dock console is a lit candidate and Horror adds no exception for it *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*), a node lantern, a Chemlight pool, another crewmate's beam and the BEACON's sweep (the BEACON does not gate actions but does count in `lux_pm`; a seat swept by it is lit for as long as `lux_pm` stays at or above the threshold, and because P locks at REACH start a flapping lit state cannot steer a strike). **A seat standing in fixture light is lit and can be targeted with its own lamp off**; going dark means leaving the light, not only switching the lamp off (HE-26). The target is the lit candidate with the highest `lux_pm`, ties lowest seat id. **The argmax is therefore degenerate among seats whose own lamp is on**: each reads 1000 (the owner stands in their own light, EM-21), so the lowest seat id wins; the argmax discriminates only among seats lit by *other* light (fixtures, pools, a teammate's beam), and it is the presence of light, not its amount, that draws the horror. If no candidate is lit, the target is the locus section's anchor (the anchor REACH of HE-16). **The preference is recomputed every run and never stored.** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* The *walk* it leads to is committed, the target is not: the chosen goal waypoint is held in the persisted `goal_wp` for `GOAL_COMMIT_RUNS` runs (12, 60 ticks) unless it is reached, its seat leaves the candidate set, the phase changes, the layout changes or the goal becomes unreachable, so a lone seat on a lux boundary or under an L3 dip cannot make the stalk dither at 12 Hz (the layer players read as "it was hunting me"); REACH begin (HE-14) always reads the fresh target. The horror moves identically lit or unlit and is never repelled, stunned, slowed or harmed by light. This GDD **adopts the Emitter's text verbatim**, as the second GDD to land: "No beam stuns, burns, repels or damages a horror or a crewmate ... being revealed is the beam's whole effect" (EM:89). Objects are never targets at MVP: a dropped glowstick is a Chemlight pool that counts as exposure to whoever stands in it, but it is not a seat, so it does not pull the horror toward itself; flares, torches and decoys that repel or distract are Vertical Slice (EM:89). *Why.* Pillar 3 made legible ("you lit up, it came") with no meter. With two or more lit seats a stateless argmax can alternate; a hysteresis would need a stored value, which is a Vertical Slice question (Open Questions). *Cites.* EM:89, EM-21.

**HE-14. REACH: the telegraph (unfold).** *Rule.* STALK → REACH begins at a run when **all** of the following hold: (a) `phase` is BREACH; (b) the target is a lit seat, or the anchor under HE-16; (c) `exogenous_permitted(actor_section(target seat))` holds for a seat REACH, and `exogenous_permitted(anchor section)` holds for an anchor REACH; (d) the distance from the horror's foot point to the point is at most `REACH_RANGE_PX`; (e) `T >= cooldown_until_T`; **(f) `connected(horror foot, P)`** (HE-19): the horror's foot point and P are in the same room (Layout `room_at`), or the two rooms are adjacent through an open, passable leaf (Layout `door_passage`, HE-19) whose hinge-aware `gap_rect` the straight segment from the foot point to P crosses *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. A Euclidean distance through a wall is not a reach: a horror one tile behind a wall from a lit seat begins no REACH, and the same pair across an open sill does *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: condition (d) alone let a REACH start through solid wall and strike nothing, a telegraph drawn through a wall for a lethal mechanic, which breaks P2's "a careful player could have perceived and had somewhere to run")*. The same condition (f) gates the anchor REACH (HE-16 (3)). At that run it **locks** the point P (the target seat's foot point; non-tracking) and draws `UNFOLD_TICKS = 5 × ceil_div(rng_int(UNFOLD_LO/5, UNFOLD_HI/5) × UNFOLD_SCALE_PCT, 100)` (integer order of operations: draw, multiply by the scale, `ceil_div`, then × 5) from `rng_derive_epoch(world_seed, "horror.tell", counter)` (the kernel's one restore-epoch derivation, HE-42) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R1)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: kernel name `rng_derive_epoch`)* (`UNFOLD_SCALE_PCT` is the accessibility lengthening of HE-F5, default 100, persisted in `horror.state`; it is applied after the draw, never shortens, and does not change the draw count) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; the counter increments in the same block write, is persisted and restored exactly, and a restore never rerolls a REACH already in flight (its length is the stored `reach_end_T`; a REACH that later fizzles has still consumed its draw); the next REACH after a restore draws at the restored counter with the restore's new `restore_epoch` inside `rng_derive_epoch` (HE-42) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R1)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: kernel name `rng_derive_epoch`)* The body is rooted for the whole REACH; the limb extends toward P; `reach_end_T = reach_start_T + UNFOLD_TICKS` is a multiple of 5, so the strike resolves exactly on a run. During REACH the record publishes `state`, `locked_pt` and `reach_end_T` in `horror.view` (the rig and Audio read them). `UNFOLD_LO` and `UNFOLD_HI` must be multiples of 5 (data lint). The picture is the warning: the rig's reach-limb tip rests on P for the last part of the unfold (Visual/Audio Requirements), and the telegraph is **the limb entering the cone before the body**; no icon, ring, arc, disc, radius or ground decal marks the strike point. **The tip rests on P, which is the victim's own foot point, inside the victim's own light (EM-21: the owner stands in their own light), so the tip is lit for the victim wherever the body stands**; a touch-down SMEAR is laid at P at the start of the **hold stage** (`hold_start_T = reach_end_T − 5 × floor(3 × (UNFOLD_TICKS / 5) / 10)`, the last 25 to 50 ticks, when the tip rests on P; a true mark and a ring entry, not a marker) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: it is laid late on purpose, because a ground cue at REACH start would be a second, unacknowledged warning beside the limb and would weaken the blind-legibility claim (the limb alone carries the telegraph); a REACH that fizzles before the hold stage lays no smear, and `reach_start_T` and `locked_pt` stay observable in `horror.view` and the debug record so replay and TellAudit still see when the REACH began; Audio renders a positional REACH cue from `locked_pt`: a non-percussive lead-in started `REACH_RESOLVE_PRE_TICKS` 90 before `reach_end_T` (registry `reach_resolve_start`, `REACH_RESOLVE_PRE_TICKS <= UNFOLD_LO`) and ending at `reach_end_T` with no impact transient (Audio AA-28 b). There is **no lit-self gate**: the horror moves identically lit or unlit (HE-13). The point is locked at the start and never updated, so moving out of the strike disc (HE-15) always works. If the blind-legibility acceptance criterion fails, the fallback is a larger `UNFOLD_LO` or a smaller `R_SNAP_PX`, never a victim-side marker (a marker is a HUD by another name, Pillar 3). *(orchestrator ruling: checkpoint CP-1; C-HE-9 closed.)* *Why.* ART 5.4 ("a limb entering the cone before the body"), Pillar 2; a tracking strike is learnable never. *Cites.* HE-F2, HE-F5, ART:540.

**HE-15. STRIKE: bodily resolution.** *Rule.* At the first run with `T >= reach_end_T` and the predicate satisfied, the state becomes STRIKE for `STRIKE_HOLD_RUNS` runs (the snap pose; no travel). On the entry run, for **every** living, non-DOWNED, non-DEAD seat whose `actor_section` is the section of P, whose room (Layout `room_at`) equals the room of P, whose foot point is **strictly within** `R_SNAP_PX` of P (`dist < R_SNAP_PX`), and for which `exogenous_permitted(actor_section(seat))` holds at that tick, Horror calls `apply_suit_damage(player_id, STRIKE_BP, source_ref = the horror's ref)` once. `apply_suit_damage` is the 3-argument Suit mutator; the class (EXOGENOUS) travels on `source_ref`, classed EXOGENOUS once at record creation (boot or ingress arrival), persisted with the record, and valid across restore independently of E6 (SE:322, SE-23; persisted as `exo_source_class`, HE-41); an unclassed ref is a programmer error. The room test stops a strike through a wall. A seat standing on a door sill belongs to the sill's room (Layout `room_at`). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* The strike's wall test is the **strict same-room clause only**: the second branch of `connected` (an adjacent room across an open, passable sill, HE-19) applies to REACH begin and nowhere in the strike, so a seat in the adjacent room across an open sill is spared even though a REACH could begin toward it (AC-HE-27). The strike is target-agnostic *within a body REACH*: any living seat in the disc is hit, whether or not it was the target. `STRIKE_BP` 3200 satisfies HE-F3 (strike one: 10000 → 6800, below `SUIT_HP_OK`, rung STRAINED; strike two → 3600, below `SUIT_LEAK_HP`, CRITICAL, stagger, walk-only; strike three → 400, below `SUIT_HP_FAIL`, FAILED → DOWNED → `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` 1800 → DEAD, Suit's ladder, Save/Load's restore). Each exogenous harm lands through `apply_suit_damage(..., EXOGENOUS)` only after the horror's own 10-30 s tell (HE-9). Horror assumes `SUIT_EXO_BYPASS_LIMITER` 1 (an EXOGENOUS hit sets the integrity rung from `integrity_bp` with no limiter hold; Interaction's IA-31 now quotes the default 1, matching the registry: C-HE-5 closed 2026-10-27). Horror never moves, pushes or staggers a seat. A STRIKE publishes `strike_T` and the seats hit (HE-37) as the hit cue, because Suit's STRAINED rung is inert and carries no witness of its own (SE:83, SE:129). **The hit mark.** On the same entry run, if `apply_suit_damage` was actually called at least once, Horror lays exactly one **STRUCK** mark at P (HE-27, HE-30); if it was called for no seat, no STRUCK mark is laid. A touch-down SMEAR laid at the hold stage is on the floor whether or not the strike lands (HE-30), so without the STRUCK mark the floor would look the same after a hit and after a miss; with it the floor records a hit, and a death has a post-hoc cause the player can read (validation target 1). The mark is a true ring entry written by the same run that struck, never a marker and never read back as a hidden value. *Why.* The user's decision ("death is a bitch. It happens.") bounded to three told strikes. **Strike one is not a visible rung:** Suit's integrity STRAINED (`SUIT_LEAK_HP <= integrity_bp < SUIT_HP_OK`) is inert and has no witness by design (Suit's creative-director review chose none; reversal path SE OQ-19). Strike one is therefore witnessed by the strike itself (snap pose, Audio's positional strike cue from `strike_T`), by Suit's integrity reading, and by the STRUCK mark that stays on the floor after the moment has passed (a miss leaves only the touch-down smear); the first physical consequence is strike two (leak, seam jet, CRITICAL, stagger, walk-only). A hit that left no trace anywhere would be a hidden stat (Pillar 1), which is why the strike itself carries the witness and the floor keeps it: the pipe, not the number. *Cites.* SE:322, SE:221, SE:223, HE-F2, HE-F3.

**HE-16. Hull acts: GOUGE, RUPTURE and the anchor REACH.** *Rule.* (1) *Hull GOUGE with a strike.* If P lies within `HULL_CONTACT_PX` of a hull-segment face, the STRIKE entry run also deposits GOUGE (`deposit_trace(GOUGE, hull_segment_id, magnitude <= FOOTPRINT_MAX_GOUGE_BP)`) on that segment, at most `GOUGES_PER_EPOCH_MAX` per `tell_epoch`, appends the segment id to `gouged_seg_ids` if absent (HE-41) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, and lays radial hairlines at the contact point. **GOUGE subjects are hull segments only** (binding on this GDD by the creative-director's CD-GDD-ALIGN ruling, FT-31 and FT:1412): a duct, cable, pipe or door is never a GOUGE subject (lint `GOUGE_NON_HULL`). (2) *RUPTURE.* A RUPTURE (`deposit_trace(RUPTURE, hull_segment_id, ...)`, hull only) is issued only on a STRIKE whose P is within `HULL_CONTACT_PX` of a hull segment that is **in `gouged_seg_ids`, that is, already carries a Horror GOUGE from an earlier strike this epoch** (always at least `min_gap` > `STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS` earlier, HE-F15, so the scrape precedes the tear by a full told cycle, layer L4 of HE-9; no stored timestamp is needed, because a time clause could never decide an outcome) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, and at most `RUPTURES_PER_EPOCH_MAX` 1 per epoch, **and only when the strike's disc holds no living seat at resolution (the HE-15 hit list is empty)**: a RUPTURE never lands on a struck seat, so the compound case "a seat already CRITICAL and staggered, hit again, with a hull breach opening beside it" cannot occur; a strike that hits someone deposits a GOUGE at most, and the RUPTURE is spent only when the target walked out and the ship pays instead ("I lived, and the ship paid") *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: a RUPTURE on a victim already CRITICAL and staggered is the most lethal combination in the design and HE-F16 did not model it; modelling the Suit and Graph compound belongs to those documents, so this GDD excludes it by construction; see Open Question 2 for the rejected alternative; the miss-only rule is a user ruling, 2026-10-27)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. Each act has its own REACH and its own predicate check. (3) *Anchor REACH (no lit seat).* With no lit candidate the target is the locus section's anchor: **the lowest-id anchor (Layout `anchor_geometry`) of the locus section's outermost compartment that lies within `HULL_CONTACT_PX` of a hull-segment face; if there is none, the REACH lays hairlines only.** The horror stalks to `REACH_RANGE_PX` of it and runs a REACH whose resolution lays radial hairlines and, because `ANCHOR_REACH_GOUGES` defaults to 1, **one hull GOUGE** (hull only, under `GOUGES_PER_EPOCH_MAX`, never RUPTURE, never `apply_suit_damage`). Its `fatigued` creep is ENDOGENOUS and told by the Graph's own gate (P2 holds), so going dark now costs the ship (AT-11, Pillar 5). *(orchestrator ruling: checkpoint CP-4; C-HE-24 resolved.)* (4) *The Graph's consequence is the Graph's.* A GOUGE sets the segment `fatigued` (LSG M17 row); the creep it starts is an ENDOGENOUS clock that outlives the breach and sits under the Graph's own `W_MIN_WARNING_S` gate. Horror neither alters nor shortens it. (5) *Read published.* Mapping P to a Graph hull-segment id uses Layout's `hull_segments_near(section_id, px, r) -> [HULL_SEGMENT node id]` (node ids ascending, Chebyshev distance `r` to the plate-position face span; Layout LY-28 reads): C-HE-11 is closed 2026-10-27 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; was provisional)*. *Why.* R2: the physical half of the footprint grammar, hull-only, bounded per epoch; the "scrape then tear" ladder keeps even a hull RUPTURE preceded by a told GOUGE. *(Open Question: whether RUPTURE belongs in the anchor ladder at all; at the default it does not, so RUPTURE is reachable only when a REACH on a lit seat beside a hull face that already carries this epoch's Horror GOUGE resolves with nobody inside the disc, that is, when the seat has walked out.)* *Cites.* FT:199, FT:1412, LSG:196.

**HE-17. RETREAT, cooldown and SUBSIDING.** *Rule.* After a STRIKE the state is RETREAT: the horror moves at `V_CREEP_MPX` along the room graph to the nearest waypoint at least `LURK_STANDOFF_PX` from every living seat, and `cooldown_until_T = strike_T + STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS` (invariant `STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS > STAGGER_TICKS` 30, CITED). A **fizzle** (the predicate fails at the REACH's last tick, the phase has returned to CALM, or a restore finds the predicate false) goes to RETREAT with no harm, no hairlines, no GOUGE or RUPTURE and **no cooldown consumed**, so a fizzle cannot starve the next legitimate REACH; **a touch-down smear already laid at the hold stage stays** (it is true; a fizzle before the hold stage lays none) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. RETREAT → STALK when `T >= cooldown_until_T` and `phase` is BREACH; RETREAT → LURK when the phase is CALM or TELL and the record is not returning home. In SUBSIDING no new REACH starts and STALK goes to RETREAT; an in-flight REACH that reaches `reach_end_T` during SUBSIDING **resolves** (the predicate holds in SUBSIDING for sections in `locus_body`, so TellAudit passes) and then RETREATs, unless the knob `SUBSIDE_CANCELS_REACH` is 1 (the merciful variant, default 0). *(orchestrator ruling: this reconciles the two clauses "SUBSIDING sends the horror to RETREAT" and "an in-flight REACH resolves iff the predicate holds at its last tick" as resolve-then-RETREAT; ambiguity C-HE-10.)* Pod-hop: the departed seat leaves `locus_body`, so `exogenous_permitted(actor_section(seat))` fails and an in-flight REACH on it fizzles; on a derelict locus, the departure of the last occupant recorded at BREACH entry also ends the breach (AT-12 (iii)). *Cites.* AT:200, AT:203, SE-24.

**HE-18. Never harm what is already down.** *Rule.* DOWNED and DEAD seats are not candidates (HE-13) and are skipped at resolution (HE-15). After the third strike the seat is DOWNED and the clock `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` is the Controller's and Suit's (SE-24); death and restore belong to Save/Load (SL-7). Horror owns only the act and its tell. A seat that becomes DOWNED or DEAD during a REACH is not hit; the REACH completes harmlessly (the telegraph was already told) and the record RETREATs. *Cites.* SE:223, SL:197.

**HE-19. Strike scope is geometry, not intent.** *Rule.* Resolution reads only Layout `room_at`, `room_adjacency` (open or closed leaf), `door_passage`, the horror's and the seats' integer positions, and `solid_in` for the horror's own movement. No `probe()` and no physics query. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **The Layout read for door geometry, `door_passage(section_id, via_id)`** (published: Layout LY-28 and D.5, AC-180) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. `via_id` is the `room_adjacency` record's `via_id` (Layout's `door_index`). It returns `{orientation EW|NS, sill_rect, gap_rect, open_extent_px}`: pure, integer, section-local base px; rects are Layout's OUT convention, the occupied span (x0, y0, x1−1, y1−1); `sill_rect` is the door cell rectangle; `gap_rect` is NONE when `open_extent_px` is 0. Data path: the Graph's `opening(edge_id) = {area, area_max, door_state}` → Layout's `layout.doors` (P3, runs before `body.integrate`, which runs before `horror.step`, so a run sees same-tick state) → `open_extent_px = floor_div(96 × area, area_max)`; Horror never calls `opening()` and owns no door state. `gap_rect` is **hinge-aware** (for a partially open leaf the open part lies on the side away from the hinge, so it is not centred on the sill). A leaf is *passable* iff `open_extent_px >= foot_extent(orientation)`, with `foot_extent` = `FOOT_BOX_W` 16 across an E-W wall run and `FOOT_BOX_H` 8 across a N-S wall run (Body's foot box, cited; Layout's `passes(F)`; the foot box is 16 wide and 8 deep, so a single 16 px threshold is wrong for one of the two orientations). The result changes only when `version(section_id)` changes, so HE-25's cache key is unchanged. There is no `solid_in` sampling fallback, because the five `solid_in` queries per run (HE-43) already belong to movement. **The segment test, stated once:** the straight integer segment a → b crosses `gap_rect` iff it meets the closed occupied span [x0, x1−1] × [y0, y1−1] (integer cross-multiplication; no ray, no float, no physics query). **Two room predicates, split on purpose.** (1) `linked(a, b)`, for every Horror *movement* distance rule: true iff `room_hops(room_at(a), room_at(b)) <= LINK_ROOM_HOPS` (default 2, range 1 to 3) over the room graph whose edges are the DOOR and HATCH records of `room_adjacency` with `open_extent_px > 0` (open at all, whatever the width). It depends only on the two room ids and the open or closed state of the doors, so it is constant while both stand in their rooms: a horror at a doorway cannot flip between the standoff and the step-away rule from a one-pixel move, and a seat two short rooms away in open line of sight is linked; a door opening or closing flips it as one discrete event. (2) `connected(a, b)`, for REACH begin only (HE-14 (f)): true iff `room_at(a) == room_at(b)`, or the two rooms are adjacent through a DOOR or HATCH record whose leaf is *passable* and the straight integer segment `a → b` crosses that record's `gap_rect` (the segment test above). The strike's wall test (HE-15) is stricter than both, **strictly the same room**, so the second branch of `connected` never reaches a seat. `linked` is the test for the `LURK_STANDOFF_PX` standoff and step-away tests of HE-11, HE-12 and HE-17, the waypoint filter of HE-11, and the standoff clause of the ingress legality test of HE-20 and HE-F9; `connected` is the test for REACH condition (f) and for the anchor REACH. A door waypoint (HE-25) is the centre of `gap_rect`. The straight segment from the horror's foot point to P must not cross a closed door leaf (checked through the room-graph sill, not a ray). The same-room test (HE-15) is re-evaluated at resolution against the *current* layout, so a weld or cut during a REACH is honoured while the locked point stays the same physical point (the stored integer, translated only by HE-22's frame re-base) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*.

#### Group D — Presence: ingress, TRANSIT, return, dormancy, weld and cut

**HE-20. Instance, ingress, TRANSIT, egress and return.** *Rule.* The record has a **home body** (default: derelict B, the web-intrusion side, DA OQ-V5). At TELL entry (observed as `tell_epoch != last_seen_tell_epoch`, the **first evaluation of every record in every run, ahead of dormancy**; transition guard 2), if `body_of(locus_section_id)` is not the record's body, the record leaves its body **whether or not that body is loaded** (E7 `unregister_subject` only if the ref is still registered, because HE-34 already unregistered it when the body unloaded; an egress mark at the old position, inserted into the block-level ring with the old, possibly unloaded, `section_id` and not ageing until that section loads, HE-21; the departed-from body is stored as `from_body_id`), enters `TRANSIT` with `transit_until_T = tell_enter_T + INGRESS_DELAY_TICKS` (the destination body, `transit_to_body_id`, is the locus body), and arrives at a hull segment of the locus body chosen by HE-F9: the compartment **one room hop from the room of the highest-`lux_pm` living seat in the locus body** (none lit or none present: the locus section's outermost compartment; ties lowest room id, then lowest hull-segment id), **never a room holding a living seat and never inside `LURK_STANDOFF_PX` of a living seat that is `linked` to the entry (HE-19)**, no earlier than `INGRESS_DELAY_TICKS` after TELL entry *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. The candidate entries are composed from published Layout reads only: a *hull entry* is an `anchor_geometry` point (section-local, composed with `section_offset`) of a hull-bearing compartment of the locus body that lies within `HULL_CONTACT_PX` of a hull face, paired with the lowest id returned by `hull_segments_near(section_id, anchor_px, HULL_CONTACT_PX)`; there is no `solid_in` sampling and no unpublished room-to-segment read, and the chosen `ingress_seg`, `ingress_anchor_px` and `ingress_mark_T` are persisted (HE-41). The ingress mark (smear and radial hairlines at the segment) is laid `INGRESS_MARK_LEAD_TICKS` before the body appears, so a player looking at that wall sees the arrival coming; on arrival the record registers (E6) and enters STALK (or LURK if the phase is already CALM for a reason other than an abort, for example a return home completing; an abort cancels an outbound arrival instead, see below) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. **This departure is the one exemption from HE-21's dormancy** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the default home body is derelict B, which is usually not the body the player is on, so without the exemption a record whose own body is unloaded would be frozen by its own dormancy rule at the first TELL on the home ship or derelict A and a told breach would have no horror in it: cry-wolf, Pillar 1's "the tell is a promise")*. A record already in TRANSIT that sees a new `tell_epoch` retargets: `transit_to_body_id` becomes the new locus body and `transit_until_T` is recomputed from the new TELL entry. **TELL abort while in TRANSIT:** if the phase leaves TELL with an aborted outcome while the record is outbound (it has not yet arrived), the arrival is cancelled, nothing is injected into the player's body, and the record heads back: `transit_to_body_id` becomes `from_body_id` (with `RETURN_HOME` 1 the home body), `transit_leg` becomes RETURN (it is OUTBOUND while heading for a locus body, including after a retarget to a new locus, and RETURN while heading home), `transit_until_T = abort_T + RETURN_DELAY_TICKS`, and the arrival at that body follows HE-21 (it waits if that body is unloaded); an ingress mark already laid stays (it is a true mark) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: an abort must send a restraint signal, not inject a CALM presence into the ship that held its fire)*. If no entry is legal the record stays in TRANSIT and retries each run (diagnostic `horror_ingress_pending`; a record still unregistered after the first Horror run at or after BREACH entry is a cry-wolf defect and an acceptance criterion; a record that registers on that run, at most 4 ticks after BREACH entry by run alignment, E-HE-17, is not) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **The rule relaxes so a told breach cannot pass with nothing in it:** at `tell_enter_T + INGRESS_DELAY_TICKS + STALK_VISIBLE_FLOOR_TICKS` (540 at the placeholders) the entry rule falls back to the *nearest legal hull anchor* (HE-F9: the hop-1 and standoff conditions drop out; the entry is the hull entry nearest by room hops to the lit seat's room whose room holds no living seat), whose mark is laid at once and whose body arrives `INGRESS_MARK_LEAD_TICKS` later, at the first Horror run at or after `TELL_MIN_TICKS` (at most 4 ticks later for an unaligned `tell_enter_T`, E-HE-17) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*; only when every hull room holds a living seat does the record stay pending (AC-HE-44). **Return:** when `RETURN_HOME` is 1 (default [PH]), `RETURN_DELAY_TICKS` after SUBSIDING ends or after a TELL abort the record returns to its home body by the same rule (no seat target: the home body's outermost compartment, lowest ids; the legality tests still apply); when `RETURN_HOME` is 0 it does not return and LURKs in the body of the last breach (the creative-director's lean; an Open Question bound to validation target (2), the 70 % CALM sighting: if the target fails, flip this knob first). All choices are deterministic functions of Attention's locus, Emitter reads and ids; nothing is authored (DA:101 "authoring places no entity"). TRANSIT is unregistered, has no position, publishes `state` TRANSIT and `transit_until_T`, harms nothing and lays no marks. The fiction is that the web runs through hulls; both ends leave a mark. Whether a CALM presence aboard the home ship after a home-ship breach is acceptable is settled for MVP in Open Question 6: default `RETURN_HOME` 1, and the departure rule above makes the told breach independent of that knob (with `RETURN_HOME` 0 the record still leaves an unloaded body at every TELL elsewhere, so a TELL is never empty) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. *Why.* The first draft's "lowest-id hull segment of the outermost compartment" could place the entity rooms from the player and let it arrive late in BREACH (cry-wolf); one-hop ingress keeps teeth and keeps the approach told. *(orchestrator ruling: ingress refined to one room hop.)* *Cites.* DA:101, AT-12.

**HE-21. Dormancy.** *Rule.* A record whose body is unloaded is frozen: no step, no mark ageing, no disturbance, **no fast-forward** (LS-17, LSG:174). **The single exemption is the TELL departure of HE-20** (the new-`tell_epoch` comparison runs before this rule, so an unloaded record whose body is not the locus body leaves for TRANSIT; it does nothing else) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. A mark's `life_left` falls only while the mark's section is loaded (an egress mark laid in an unloaded section is inserted at once and starts ageing when that section loads). A record in TRANSIT whose destination body unloads waits. Answer to Web Map ASK-H1: yes. A record may enter TRANSIT toward an unloaded body (for example a return home to derelict B while it is unloaded). It stays in TRANSIT, unregistered, with no position, no marks and no harm, and arrives at its first run after the body loads with a legal HE-20 entry. The transit machine's `TRANSIT_HOLD` and pod states are Web Map's and are never read as Horror's TRANSIT. *Cites.* LSG:174, LS-17, WM:458.

**HE-22. Weld and cut.** *Rule.* The path cache is keyed by the layout (HE-25) and recomputed on any `layout.state` change (a weld or cut applying, MSA-14 / MSA-20). If a weld changes `locus_body` membership, the target body is re-derived from `locus_section_id` on the next run (`locus_body` is recorded at TELL entry and is replaced mid-epoch only by an AT-14 fallback, which TellAudit records as LOCUS_FALLBACK; Horror re-reads `locus_section_id` each run). If a cut removes the section the record stands in from its body, the record's `body_id` is re-derived from `body_of(section_id)`; if its section is gone the record goes to TRANSIT with an egress mark and re-enters its home body. **Re-basing the body-frame points** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: Layout owns the frame, G1/G6; a weld re-bases the absorbed body by `frame_shift_px`, Layout D.6, and the MVP horror's home body, derelict B, is the absorbed body)*: at the start of each run, if `section_offset(section_id)` or `body_of(section_id)` differs from the stored `offset_seen_px` / `body_id` (a weld or cut moved the record's section, Layout LY-16/LY-17), Horror re-bases every persisted body-frame point of the record (`pos_px`, `locked_pt`, `ingress_anchor_px` and any other body-frame px pair in HE-41 groups 1, 3 and 5; section-local fields such as `goal_wp` are untouched) by `delta = section_offset(section_id) − offset_seen_px`, re-derives `body_id` from `body_of(section_id)`, stores the new `offset_seen_px`, and calls E8 `set_subject_anchor(ref, pos_px)` in the same run. The Emitter derives the HORROR subject's `body_id` itself (EM-4, EM-21) and lands the new anchor at the next `emitter.place`. Horror has no cut verb and does not interrupt a cut; Modular Ship Assembly states the same ("Horror and Attention have no cut verb; the breach rhythm uses STRESS and M17, not detaches", MSA-22). *Cites.* MSA-14 / MSA-20 (weld and cut apply), MSA-22 (no cut verb).

#### Group E — Movement and pathing

**HE-23. Own position, Body's pure step.** *Rule.* Positions are Horror's, in integer base px with a two-axis sub-pixel residue and `last_octant`, inside `horror.state`. Movement calls Body's pure `LocomotionStep` (BDY-10) with `collide` bound to Layout `solid_in`, using Body's foot box (`FOOT_BOX_W` 16, `FOOT_BOX_H` 8, CITED; the 64 to 96 px sprite is visual only, because a larger box could never use an open doorway whose passage extent is exactly 16 across). Per run the horror picks one octant (the integer argmax of the eight unit vectors against the direction to the next waypoint, ties lowest octant) and steps once per tick for the five ticks of the run.
