There are no `AGENT` rows in Body at MVP (preserving `MAX_ACTORS` 8 headroom for Vertical Slice B seats), no `CharacterBody2D` and no physics query (CR-13 G3, H10). A horror's footprint is a Graph trail or a Horror mark, never a Body fact (BDY-4). `pos_prev` is the position at run start and `pos_curr` the position at run end. -> ADR candidate (1): horror position ownership versus Body `AGENT` rows. *Cites.* BDY-4, BDY-10, BDY-15.

**HE-24. Speeds and the escape invariant.** *Rule.* Registered constants, in milli-px per tick: `V_CREEP_MPX` 1000 (LURK, STALK in a TELL, RETREAT, the arrival walk), `V_HUNT_MPX` 1500 (STALK in BREACH). Each tick adds the speed to an accumulator, steps `floor(acc / 1000)` px and keeps the remainder (persisted per record). Registered invariant (HE-F6): `V_CREEP_MPX < V_HUNT_MPX < V_WALK × 1000` (2000), so **a walking seat, including a CRITICAL walk-only seat, always outpaces it**, and there is no lunge travel (the strike resolves at the locked point). A seat that is *carrying* moves at `V_CARRY` 1 (CITED, BDY-13): `V_HUNT_MPX` exceeds it, so a carrier cannot out-walk the approach, and a carrier needs 72 ticks (73 on a diagonal octant) to clear the disc against 40 available (`UNFOLD_LO` 95 less 54 for reaction less 1 for command application; conflict C-HE-8), so **a carrier who does not drop the cargo is struck on 7 of the 18 draws (38.9 %)**: the intended answer is to drop the cargo and walk (Interaction's `drop` is a TAP, lit-exempt and uninterruptible, IA-28; it can only be refused `OBSTRUCTED` when no free cell exists, which is an authoring defect to be lint-covered and a cross-GDD note to Interaction), or accept the hit; the telegraph length is **not** tied to the seat's `V_CARRY` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the 38.9 % figure is now explicit rather than implicit, and the drop is the stated counterplay)*. *(orchestrator ruling: stated as intended; hauling is where the horror bites, Pillar 5 risk; the DA-29 wording note is an owed row.)* Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions): 'drop the cargo' is confirmed as the intended price of hauling (a carrier who keeps the cargo is struck on 7 of 18 draws, 38.9 %); `R_SNAP_PX` stays 72 and 48 is the ruled lever (HE-F5, worked example) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *Cites.* DA-29, BDY-13, REG `V_CARRY`.

**HE-25. Pathing over Layout reads.** *Rule.* The horror searches an integer room graph built from Layout `room_adjacency(section_id)` over every section of its body (`sections_of`), excluding any edge whose door leaf is closed or **not passable** (a *passable leaf* is open with `open_extent_px` at least the foot-box extent across its wall run, 16 px (`FOOT_BOX_W`) across an E-W wall and 8 px (`FOOT_BOX_H`) across a N-S wall, from Layout's `door_passage` (HE-19); a leaf opened to less is solid to the horror) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. Breadth-first by hop count, ties lowest room id, at most `PATH_EXPANSIONS_MAX` node expansions per run (HE-F10); the result is cached as a next-hop table per goal room and layout key. **One graph, one goal rule (the surrogate-goal BFS)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the text had defined the pathing graph two ways, an open-leaf BFS and the full adjacency truncated at a closed leaf)*: the goal room is chosen over the full adjacency (closed and impassable leaves counted for distance, E-HE-20); the search runs on the passable-leaf graph only; when the goal room is not reachable the *surrogate goal* is the reachable room with the smallest full-adjacency hop distance to the goal room (ties lowest room id), and the horror walks there and stops. Inside a room it steers by octant toward the next waypoint (the centre of a door's hinge-aware `gap_rect`, not the sill centre or the leaf, or an `anchor_geometry` point) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; a waypoint is **reached** when the foot point is within `ARRIVE_PX` (8 px [PH], at least the largest per-run displacement, 8 px at `V_HUNT_MPX`, so a run can never step over a waypoint) of it. There is no obstacle avoidance beyond the **local slide rule**, which uses only the persisted `blocked_runs` counter: when the previous run ended `blocked`, `blocked_runs` is 1 and the run uses the adjacent octant (±1) with the larger dot product against the direction to the waypoint (ties lowest octant); at `blocked_runs` 2 it uses the other adjacent octant; from 3 it returns to the direct octant. Authoring contract: a room's walkable floor must let straight octant steps between its sills and anchors succeed with at most this slide (lint `ROOM_NONCONVEX_PATH`, owed to Layout and Derelict Authoring; an L-shaped footprint fixture is AC-HE-128). On a NONE or UNKNOWN Layout read it holds its last waypoint for at most `HOLD_MAX_RUNS` (counted in the persisted `hold_runs`), and then **the fallback depends on the phase: in CALM and TELL it goes to LURK; in BREACH it stays in STALK and holds position (LURK is n/a in BREACH, so a hold-then-LURK there would break the phase view and leave a breach with a horror that cannot do harm); in SUBSIDING it goes to RETREAT**; a record in RETREAT holds in place in any phase *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; **UNKNOWN is never read as "unlit" or as "open"**. If `step` reports no movement for `BLOCKED_RUNS_MAX` consecutive runs it discards the cached hop and re-paths once; if it is still without movement after a second `BLOCKED_RUNS_MAX` runs it is **wedged**, and the defined outcome is RETREAT (counter `horror_wedged`, `cooldown_until_T := T`; in BREACH it then returns to STALK and re-paths, so a wedge repeats visibly in the diagnostics and is an authoring signal, never a silent permanent stall) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. Zero `probe()` calls at MVP. `NavigationServer2D` and `NavigationAgent2D` are rejected (node- and server-based, outside the replay hash; H10, G3). `route_length_px` stays diagnostic (LY:310). Layout now states (ship-section-and-layout-data-model.md, LY-28 reads and LY-33) that `layout_hash()` does **not** cover door state and that a reader caching on door geometry keys on `version(section_id)`; the cache key is therefore `(goal_room, layout_hash, version(section_id) of every section of the body)`, a restore (where `version` resets) is an unconditional rebuild, and the Horror-computed closed-leaf hash is retired (C-HE-13 closed 2026-10-27, design review). -> ADR candidate (2). *Cites.* LY:296-310, LY:446, EM:723.

**HE-26. Doors and the counterplay set.** *Rule.* A closed door leaf is solid to the horror (Layout `solid_in`). It issues no `set_opening`, opens no door, and may GOUGE or RUPTURE a hull segment, never a door. The **whole MVP counterplay set** is: retreat (walk away: `V_HUNT_MPX < V_WALK × 1000`), close a door (the strike room test and solid leaf; the horror holds at the sill and marks the leaf, below), go dark (the seat leaves target preference on the next run **only if no other light reaches it: a lamp-off seat under a lit fixture or in a Chemlight pool is still lit and still targetable, HE-13** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; its price is every lit-only channelled verb for the breach and the ship under STRESS unattended), pod-hop (the departed seat leaves `locus_body`, so `exogenous_permitted(actor_section(seat))` fails and an in-flight REACH on it fizzles; on a derelict locus, the departure of the last occupant recorded at BREACH entry also ends the breach, AT-12 (iii)), drop the cargo (turns a carrier into a walker) and the **glowstick work-light** (*light the job, not yourself*: a thrown Chemlight lights a workspace so the player can step out of it dark; it does not pull the horror). The suppressant is *not* in the set (HE-39). **A horror may path and stand in derelict A's `a_spine` at the MVP** (Boarding ASK-BS-H1 / OQ-BS-33 answered by the owner) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R21: the option that needs the least new content and preserves user rulings X6 and D63)*: ingress (HE-20) lands at a hull entry one room hop from the lit seat's room and never in a living seat's room, and a stalking horror follows the lit seat through any open door, so a carrier in the 3-wide spine can meet one. No bypass is added and the spine is not widened, because the ruled exits already hold in a single corridor: drop the cargo and walk back the way the carrier came (a walking seat outpaces `V_HUNT_MPX`, HE-24), stillness and darkness, or a door shut behind; this is the intended price of hauling (D63, 7 of 18 draws for a carrier who keeps the cargo, E-HE-27). The existing guards are the dead-end lint (E-HE-26) and the drop's one-free-cell question (Open Question 7); E-HE-59. **The anti-pillar's "tools may repel or distract (flares, torches, decoys)" is deliberately unused at MVP and deferred to Vertical Slice (`repel`, flares, decoys): MVP counterplay is avoidance only, and the routed thread's "tools repel, never kill" is a boundary (no tool ever kills, HE-38) and not an MVP promise that a tool repels.** *(orchestrator ruling: the earlier phrase "glowstick lure" is reworded because HE-13 prefers seats only and a glowstick is not a seat; creative-director concurs; decoys are Vertical Slice.)* No response is free and none is dominant: abort loses the job, hiding loses the task and the ship, going dark loses the lit verbs, committing risks a rung. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G2)* **A closed door is a priced, visible refuge** (the creative-director's lean, taken because the user delegated the call). When the target is a lit seat behind a closed or impassable leaf, the horror walks to the surrogate goal (HE-25) and **camps at the sill**: on the run it becomes stationary there (edge-triggered by the persisted `held_at_leaf` flag) it lays one HAIRLINES mark on the leaf face (a true ring entry; no M17 call, no hull damage, no GOUGE, no RUPTURE; counter `horror_leaf_marks`), and it stays while that seat remains the target and the phase is BREACH, recomputing the target every run as ever. The door therefore costs what the rules already price (the task, the ship under STRESS, the lit verbs while dark) and now also shows the player that the thing is waiting on the other side, with the leaf mark as the post-hoc evidence; what it does not do is damage the ship on the player's behalf. The alternatives (an anchor GOUGE fallback; leaving the playtest band as the only price) are recorded in Open Question 33 and not adopted. **A door closed on the horror.** If a leaf closes while the horror's foot box overlaps it (Layout's close-safety counts seats' foot boxes, not the horror's), the next run ejects it as the first step of guard 4: it moves back along `−last_octant` in 8 px increments up to 32 px to the first position for which `solid_in(foot_box)` is false (at most four queries, inside the five-query budget); if none is free it is wedged (RETREAT, `horror_wedged`, HE-25). It never embeds, wedges silently or passes through a leaf; counter `horror_door_ejected`; AC-HE-137. The preferred long-term fix, Layout's close-safety including a published horror foot box (`OBSTRUCTED`), is an ask in Open Question 32 and would make the eject unreachable. *Cites.* AT-12, DA-29, EM:89.

#### Group F — Footprints

**HE-27. Footprint taxonomy: two halves, both truth channel.** *Rule.* Horror's footprints split exactly as Failure & Trail splits them: M17 kinds surface only as Graph descriptors that F&T draws, and the web-grammar marks are Horror's own.

| Kind | Half | Subject | Cap | Mark | Channel and path |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CHILL | physical | compartment | one deposit of `CHILL_STEP_DJ` per rime mark, laid per `CHILL_SPACING_PX` of travel (accumulated in `chill_travel_px`) and at each LURK dwell start; `CHILL_STEP_DJ <= FOOTPRINT_MAX_Q` (registry, LSG:912); `CHILL_DEPOSITS_PER_EPOCH_MAX`; a deposit withheld by the epoch cap or the cold margin still leaves its rime mark (ring-only, HE-F4) | branching rime | M17, Class B, path (a) |
| GOUGE | physical | **hull segment only** | `GOUGES_PER_EPOCH_MAX`; magnitude `<= FOOTPRINT_MAX_GOUGE_BP` (registry, LSG:914) | radial hairlines | M17 |
| RUPTURE | physical | **hull segment only** | `RUPTURES_PER_EPOCH_MAX` | radial hairlines | M17 |
| STRESS | none | none | Horror issues none (Attention's alone, AT-11) | none | none |
| SMEAR | web grammar | floor point | one per `MARK_SPACING_PX` of travel (accumulated in `smear_travel_px`), plus one touch-down smear at P at each REACH's hold stage *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | wet iridescent smear | `horror.state` ring |
| RIME | web grammar | CHILL deposit point | one per CHILL | branching rime | ring |
| HAIRLINES | web grammar | hull contact point | one per STRIKE, GOUGE, RUPTURE, anchor REACH, ingress and egress | radial hairlines | ring |
| STRUCK | web grammar | strike point P | one per STRIKE entry run on which `apply_suit_damage` was called at least once; none on a miss | a full radial hairline burst centred on P (HAIRLINES class: same palette, same sheet family) | ring |

**STRUCK is the hit-only mark** (CD-GDD-ALIGN): the touch-down SMEAR is laid at the hold stage on a hit and on a miss alike (a REACH that fizzles earlier lays none), so STRUCK is what makes the floor differ after the two. It is a HAIRLINES-class mark for priority, protection from smear eviction, lifetime and ageing, and it is laid whether or not P is near a hull face (a hull contact adds its own HAIRLINES at the contact point, so a hit at a wall leaves both). Dust direction and filament twitch are Vertical Slice A: the dust process is the Attention Presenter's, and `web_strand_*`, `vfx_tell_*` and the band-slide are exclusive to it (AV-18, AC-AT-113). The Graph has no mark record, so a persisted Horror ring is the only way a reload cannot lie by omission (Pillar 1). *CHILL is bookkeeping, and this document says so:* at `CHILL_STEP_DJ` 3000 a mark cools a 20-tile room by 0.05 degC (`C_TILE` 3000, registry, LSG:458), below the Graph's own "invisible" row (LSG:912) and far below the COOL band; the rime on the floor is **a surface mark the web leaves, not a claim about air temperature**, and the physical deposit is route-choice and Debug Overlay bookkeeping. Claiming otherwise would be the hidden-stat shape Pillar 1 forbids. *(orchestrator ruling: option B of the CHILL magnitude question; dropping CHILL at MVP would also be acceptable and is the degrade path.)* *Cites.* FT:199, FT:1412, LSG:196, LSG:912, CON:243.

**HE-28. Write path (a): published mutators, no commands.** *Rule.* `deposit_trace` (M17) and `apply_suit_damage` are **path (a)** of CR-9: published unconditional Class B mutators called from Horror's own P3 hook; the kernel names "a horror writing a footprint into the Graph's footprint channel" as the path-(a) example (KRN:104). Horror submits **no `SYSTEM`-origin command at MVP**, because no target of Horror's can reject. Attention's C22 and E-29 ("SYSTEM commands in P2") and LSG:807 ("the first 16 in arrival order within P2") carry a P2 premise that path (a) does not satisfy; this GDD **asserts no drain order** and lists both as owed refresh rows (C-HE-3). *Why.* The kernel is the authority on paths, and the budget rule of HE-29 holds under any drain order. *Cites.* KRN:104, AT:197, LSG:807.

**HE-29. Per-window footprint budget and per-run cap.** *Rule.* Horror counts its accepted M17 calls per **Graph window**, defined as *the deposits the next `graph.propagate` run drains*: writes made at tick `T` in `[6k, 6k+5]` drain at the Graph run at `6(k+1)`, because `horror.step` runs after `graph.propagate.failure` on coinciding ticks (via `attention.step` and `emitter.place`) and so can never be drained by the run it follows (window index `w = floor(T / 6)`, the Graph's divisor 6, offset 0, LSG:104). Horror never exceeds `HE_FOOTPRINT_BUDGET = MAX_FOOTPRINTS_PER_STEP − STRESS_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN` (16 − 8 = 8; DERIVED) per window, regardless of drain order. Because Horror's divisor (5) differs from the Graph's (6), one Graph window in five contains **two** Horror runs (`T mod 30` of 0 and 5, both inside `[6k, 6k+5]` for `k = 5j`); a per-run cap `MAX_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN` 4 keeps two runs at or under the window budget (`2 × MAX_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN <= HE_FOOTPRINT_BUDGET`). Priority inside a run: RUPTURE, then GOUGE, then CHILL. Writes over either bound are **not attempted** (a trace is "a moment, not a queue", LSG:807) and are counted `horror_footprints_withheld`; in nominal play that counter is a defect signal and never a gameplay state. Horror's 8-call window budget is the remainder of the registered global per-run cap (`MAX_FOOTPRINTS_PER_STEP` 16, "deposit_trace writes honoured per run across all callers", LSG:807), so Horror leaves Attention's `STRESS_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN` whole. Natural worst case per run: one hull deposit (RUPTURE or GOUGE, one per STRIKE, HE-F17) and one CHILL = 2; the three-kind run of E-HE-13 is a stub fixture for the priority rule (priority RUPTURE, GOUGE, CHILL). *Cites.* AT:197, AC-AT-92, LSG:807, LSG:911, REG `MAX_FOOTPRINTS_PER_STEP`.

**HE-30. The mark ring.** *Rule.* The ring holds at most `MAX_MARKS` records `{mark_id, kind, section_id, pos_px, seed, life_left_ticks}` (`kind` in SMEAR, RIME, HAIRLINES, STRUCK); `mark_id` comes from an in-block counter and is never reused. `seed = he_hash(mark_id, 0, 0, SALT_MARK)` (HE-42), **never `rng_derive`** (the precedent of Lighting LO-19 and Attention AT-20). **Per-kind quotas** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: rime is protected from eviction and used to pile up through a long CALM LURK, and the strike, ingress and egress marks of a 3600-tick breach add up to eight more, so the unquota'd rule let `n_other` exceed the figure the smear-retention relation assumed. The ring holds at most `MARK_QUOTA_RIME` RIME marks (7), `MARK_QUOTA_HAIRLINES` HAIRLINES marks (3: strike, GOUGE, RUPTURE, anchor, ingress and egress contacts) and `MARK_QUOTA_STRUCK` STRUCK marks (3, one per landed strike), so `n_other_max = 13` (derived), and SMEAR has the rest of the ring, `MAX_MARKS − n_other_max >= 19` (HE-F7). Inserting a mark of a quota'd kind that is already at its quota evicts the **oldest mark of that kind** (lowest `mark_id`); inserting a SMEAR into a full ring evicts the oldest SMEAR, and a SMEAR is always resident in a full ring because `MAX_MARKS > n_other_max`. The STRUCK mark (at P) and the hull-contact HAIRLINES mark (at the contact point) are two marks of two kinds, each counted in its own quota; a hit beside a wall leaves both. The earlier fallback "evict the lowest `mark_id` of any kind when no SMEAR is resident" is unreachable and survives only as a defensive assertion (E-HE-8). `life_left_ticks` starts at `MARK_LIFE_TICKS` and falls by 5 per run while the mark's section is loaded; the mark is removed at zero. A SMEAR is laid per `MARK_SPACING_PX` of moved distance while the record moves and one touch-down SMEAR at P at each REACH's hold stage (a stationary horror lays nothing else, so a long LURK does not flush the ring), a RIME at each CHILL deposit, HAIRLINES at hull contact (HE-16) and at ingress and egress, and one STRUCK at P on a STRIKE that called `apply_suit_damage` (HE-15). At most `MARKS_PER_RUN_MAX` inserts per run, in the priority STRUCK, HAIRLINES, RIME, SMEAR (a hit's record is never the insert that is dropped). Marks are laid whenever the entity moves or acts, **regardless of any viewer's hallucination state** (HE-32), and are drawn by a Horror PRESENTATION subscriber in the floor-marks band within `HORROR_MARK_DRAW_GROUPS` of the shared `D_marks` budget (Failure & Trail's `TRAIL_DMARKS_SHARE` 6 of `D_marks` 4-20 "crowds Horror's footprints out", FT:816). Every mutation marks its dirty keys **explicitly**: in Godot 4.7 packed-array element writes do not call the setter, a HIGH silent-failure class (breaking-changes, CR-8). *(orchestrator ruling: checkpoint CP-5; C-HE-12 closed. A long creep can no longer evict the hairlines of a strike while any smear remains.)* *Why.* Option B of the footprint taxonomy: the sim stays the authority and the view is reconstructible after a load. *Cites.* PIPE:137, PIPE:616, FT:816.

**HE-31. Rime and mark honesty.** *Rule.* Rime is **hue-less**, rendered L\* 70 to 78, with **non-repeating** branch angles taken from the mark's `seed`, and **never at a coolant fitting**: a CHILL deposit and its rime mark are withheld when the deposit point is within `FITTING_CLEARANCE_PX` of a coolant-loop fitting node (counter `horror_chill_withheld_fitting`), so a fitting never shows Horror rime. No flesh (hue 5-30, S 20-60 %, V 45-85 %), no violet (hue 270-330, S above 25 %), no Alarm Red, Chemlight or Tungsten appears in any mark (FT:886). A runtime guard withholds a CHILL deposit that would bring a compartment closer to `COLD_DMG_ONSET_MC` than `CHILL_MARGIN_MC`, and the data lint bounds the cumulative CHILL per compartment per epoch (HE-F4): **the cold of passage is a footprint, never a killer.** The AC-79 collision fixtures (frost fan against branching rime; RUPTURE trail against footprint) are blind-classified and signed off by the art-director. The art-director's proposed palette entry is **Rime White-Grey `#B0B8C0` `[PROVISIONAL]`**; the entry itself is an art-bible decision (creative-director, art-director), and until it lands the rime has no palette candidate (C-HE-21 open; it blocks asset-spec for rime and hairlines, not this GDD). *Cites.* FT:1427, FT:886, FT:1032, REG `COLD_DMG_ONSET_MC`.

**HE-32. Footprints survive hallucination.** *Rule.* A real entity, registered under HE-34, keeps depositing M17 writes, marks, disturbance state and suit damage **whether or not any seat's Filter hides its body**; these outputs are bit-identical with and without a Filter stub (CON:243: "the hallucination hides the body, never the trail"). Horror holds no sanity reference and reads no `PERCEPTION_OUT` block (H5, CR-12). Phantom horrors are the Filter's: no Body row, no footprint, no M17 call, no record in `horror.state`, no registration with the Emitter, and Sanity's own rule matches (a phantom "exists only in `sanity.perception`", never registered, no footprint). A HIDE entry names a real HORROR ref; the entity stays registered and keeps obeying AT-15. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: Sanity & Hallucination Rules and the Perception Filter are both `Status: Designed` and repeat this; Filter truth-table row 7 and SN-22.)* *Cites.* CON:243, SN-22, H5.

**HE-33. Seat reads.** *Rule.* Horror enumerates the seats on a body with Body's `actors_on(body_id)`, reads each seat's `pos_curr(actor_id)` and `actor_section(actor_id)` (Body's SIM read view, BDY-29, BDY-30), and reads the seat's life state (`DOWNED`, `DEAD`, `IN_POD`, `STAGGERED`) from the Controller's `controller.state` `loco_state`, which Suit states is readable (SE:333; the published read is `controller.state.loco_state` through the `SIM` read view, PCI:58 and :241, C-HE-18 closed) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. The mapping from actor to `player_id` for `apply_suit_damage` is Body's and the Controller's binding *(provisional — read name owed)*. Horror reads **no** suit integrity value and no Suit rung (it never needs one: the rung ladder is Suit's); it reads no Sanity value and no `PERCEPTION_OUT` block. The predicate argument is always Body's `actor_section`, not the Graph's `section_of`. *Cites.* BDY-29, SE:333, PCI-14.

#### Group G — Light

**HE-34. Emitter subjects.** *Rule.* Each non-TRANSIT record is an Emitter HORROR subject: E6 `register_subject(ref, HORROR, anchor_px, section_id)` at arrival, E8 `set_subject_anchor(ref, anchor_px)` once per run (the E6/E8 anchor is in the record's current body frame, as re-based by HE-22 after a weld or cut *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*; E8 is called once per run and lands at the next `emitter.place`: the one-tick lag EM:154 accepts. The anchor is otherwise current, because the sim position changes only at runs (HE-23)), E7 `unregister_subject(ref)` at egress or unload; registrations are canonical in `emitter.state` and survive a restore (EM-28, EM:712); in its own `post_restore` the record calls E6 only when it is non-TRANSIT and its ref is absent from the Emitter subject table; a duplicate E6 returns false by design (EM:152) and is not a defect. HORROR is always `moving` (EM-21). An unregistered ref answers like an unlit one (`lux` 0, `lit` false). A false return from E6 on a first registration (the `MAX_EXPOSURE_SUBJECTS` overflow, `emitter_subjects_overflow` increments, EM AC-27) is a **budget defect** (the subject reads dark) and a lint or acceptance-criterion failure, never a gameplay state; a false return on an already-registered ref is not. Horror reads `lux(ref)` only (its lit test is the stateless `lux_pm >= LIT_ON_PM` of HE-13, not the Emitter's `lit` bit) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; it calls no `probe` at MVP (Emitter EM-24 anticipates Horror pathing probes; HE-25 pathing needs none, so Horror consumes none of `MAX_PROBES_PER_TICK` 4, shared with Interaction (Attention makes no `probe()` call and does not read it, AT-4), and "Horror spams probes: only `probes_allowed` are served", EM:723). `HORROR_SUBJECTS_MAX` 2 (one active plus one transient during ingress), range 1 to 12; the Emitter's "placeholder 12" (EM:229) is a ceiling Horror owns, not the plan; I-CAPS (d) holds (2 + actors 4 ≤ `MAX_MOVING_SUBJECTS` 16). Interaction's `H` (REG `subject_budget`) and Derelict Authoring's `HORRORS` now quote this registered number, `HORROR_SUBJECTS_MAX` 2 (C-HE-4, closed 2026-10-27). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **One registration rule, for body load and `post_restore` alike:** a record is an Emitter subject iff it is non-TRANSIT and its body is loaded. On every run, after guard 2 and before guard 3, the record reconciles: body loaded, non-TRANSIT and its ref absent from the Emitter subject table, then E6 at its stored `pos_px` (this is the first run after a body loads, including first boarding, and the first run after a restore, so a resident LURK in derelict B reads lit the moment the player boards, which the CALM-sighting goal needs); body unloaded and the ref still registered, then E7 once. Both calls are idempotent through the table read, and neither is a step: an unloaded record performs no other act (HE-21). AC-HE-140. *Cites.* EM-21, EM-24, EM:229.

**HE-35. L3 light disturbance: published state, never a call.** *Rule.* `horror.view.disturbances[]` lists `{emitter_id, strength_pm, start_T, dur_ticks}` for emitters of kind FIXTURE and NODE_LANTERN in the horror's body within `L3_RADIUS_PX` of its foot point, in **any phase**. **Never** a BEAM, a BEACON, a FAULT_GLOW, any DEPLOYABLE (glowsticks), and **never the EMERGENCY fixture subkind** (`fx_cryo_lamp`) **and never the DOCK_STATUS fixture subkind** (a dock room's self-powered pod status glow, Emitter EM-3: a SYSTEM-switched status light is not a disturbance carrier, since a dip on it would read as a false tell; Lighting LO-20, Attention AT-20) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix, G6; Lighting LO-20 now exempts DOCK_STATUS per Attention AT-20 / user ruling X2)*: this is Horror's answer to Lighting's open questions 15 and 22, "EMERGENCY stays exempt" (and, from 2026-10-28, DOCK_STATUS with it). A disturbance starts on an eligible lamp at a run when `he_hash(emitter_id, run_n, 0, SALT_L3_START) mod 1000 < L3_START_PM` (HE-42) and `T >= last_end_T + L3_MIN_GAP_TICKS` (the ruled `L3_MIN_GAP_FRAMES` 120 is stored in ticks because a SIM block cannot count presentation frames, H9; one tick equals one frame at the nominal 60 fps and Lighting maps it). `strength_pm` lies in `[L3_STRENGTH_LO_PM, 1000 − MOD_FLOOR_PM]` and `dur_ticks` in `[L3_DUR_LO, L3_DUR_HI]`, both from `he_hash` with their own salts (`SALT_L3_STRENGTH`, `SALT_L3_DUR`; HE-F11); at most `L3_MAX_ACTIVE` lamps at once. A disturbance changes a lamp's **output only**: no disturbance touches a beam, the beacon, a fault glow or a glowstick, or changes a real light's radius, position or colour (Lighting's submitter contract, LO OQ 15). Horror never writes an Emitter field, never feeds the query and never calls Lighting (KRN:117; LO:424 must-repeat verbatim: disturbance is never applied to a BEAM, BEACON, FAULT_GLOW or any DEPLOYABLE, never an Emitter field, never an input to the query; horrors never occlude and emit nothing). The verb "flicker" never describes a fixture (LO-20). Disturbances are persisted (save-equivalence). The lamp enumeration near a point is the Emitter's `emitters_near(body_id, px, r)` (ids of emitters on the body whose origin lies within `r` px, ascending id; a filter of the records, not a probe; Emitter read table): Horror filters the returned ids to FIXTURE and NODE_LANTERN (excluding the EMERGENCY and DOCK_STATUS subkinds) from the emitter records, so an exempt lamp never takes an `L3_MAX_ACTIVE` slot or a `last_end_T` entry *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*; C-HE-15 is closed 2026-10-27 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; was provisional)*. *Why.* The Pillar 4(a) "light disturbance" footprint: local and unsynchronised, which separates it from the tell (global, beat-locked, AT:1184); the player's test is "is it everywhere on the beat, or one place?". *Cites.* LO-18, LO-19, LO-20, LO:424, LO OQ 15 and 22, EM-3, AT-20.

**HE-36. No noise push.** *Rule.* Horror calls `push_stimulus` **never**. Audio facts stay published (HE-37). *Why.* A horror that feeds the ledger can hold BREACH open past `CLEAR_HOLD_TICKS` (the C8 spiral) and puts a lever outside the player's restraint (Pillar 3). AC-AT-95 refuses any `push_stimulus` caller other than the Controller and Interaction (a Horror double is refused), so Horror is not a declared caller (registry `push_stimulus`). *Cites.* AT:373, AC-AT-95.

#### Group H — Information, rules and reward

**HE-37. Audio and presentation facts, not sounds.** *Rule.* `horror.view` publishes, per entity: `entity_id`, `type_id`, `state` (LURK, STALK, REACH, STRIKE, RETREAT, TRANSIT), `pos_px`, `body_id`, `section_id`, `locked_pt` and `reach_end_T` (REACH), `strike_T` and the seats hit (STRIKE), `transit_until_T`, ingress and egress events with their tick, `disturbances[]`, the mark ring, and diagnostic counters (`horror_footprints_withheld`, `horror_marks_withheld`, `horror_ingress_pending`, `horror_chill_withheld_*`, E6 refusals). These `horror_*` names are **frozen** for the Debug Overlay, which reads them by channel and restates none as a fact (DO P-11). Adaptive Audio owns every sound, mix and cue name; the Horror sub-tell family is **disjoint** from Attention's tell family, the Graph loop sounds and Sanity's hallucination family (Audio AA-6 (the identity table), Sanity R28); phantom horrors use the Filter and Sanity audio classes, **never the Graph witness acoustic classes**. Horror never mutes, ducks or filters anything. A `tell_id` and `type_id` enumeration is kept stable and enumerable so the Knowledge Codex (Vertical Slice) can target one. Designed (`adaptive-audio-and-tells.md`, AA-28, AA-29). *Cites.* AT:1204, IDX:41.

**HE-38. No combat, no reward.** *Rule.* No horror HP, no kill state, no loot, no XP, no kill counter, no hit feedback for tool use, no player damage output, no hit-stop, no screen shake, no damage number, no threat bar and no proximity indicator. HORROR subjects take no verb at MVP: any forced command is `INVALID_TARGET` (IA-39, AC-IA-113). The beam only reveals (HE-13). "HORROR subjects take no verb" holds at MVP only; the Vertical Slice B `repel` declaration fields and their parity precondition (IA-31) are recorded in Open Questions row 27 only. The data-file lint of HE-5 and a repository scan for the feedback words in the vocabulary list are acceptance criteria. *Why.* The anti-pillar "not a combat game"; "horrors are avoided and survived, not farmed". *Cites.* IA:288, IA:468, PC-12, FT:98.

**HE-39. Suppressant: a data-dormant interface; `tool_suppressant` is out of the MVP start kit.** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *Rule.* **No suppressant effect ships at MVP**, per AT-19 (designed, CD-approved: "no effect verb ships at MVP ... a Vertical Slice addendum"; a suppressant that aborts a tell would "make restraint an item"). Horror declares only the dormant interface `repel(origin, T)` with `REPEL_RADIUS_PX` 192 and `REPEL_TICKS` 600 [PLACEHOLDER]: any record within the radius would go RETREAT for the window, begin no REACH or STRIKE, never die, drop nothing, never touch Attention's level or tell. When the effect verb ships, its registrant reads the suppressant's `OUT_OF_CHARGES` through Suit's `tool_charge(player_id, def_id)` (SE read table); Horror never stores or writes a charge. Its acceptance criteria read **NOT ASSESSED at MVP, never PASS**. **Conflict C-HE-6 — closed. Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions): Option A (X6).** The start kit carried `tool_suppressant` with `SUPPRESSANT_START_CHARGES` 3 and no effect, a Pillar 1 problem of its own (a tool whose use changes nothing in the world is a lie the sane game tells). `tool_suppressant` is removed from the MVP start kit (SE-11 slot 4, SE-14, SE-15), from the Item DB MVP list and from the HUD, and returns with its effect verb at Vertical Slice; the registry name `SUPPRESSANT_START_CHARGES` is retired (status deprecated). HE-39 keeps the dormant `repel` interface, marked **`[CUT-FIRST]`**: if AT-19 is reversed at Vertical Slice the effect and the item arrive together and it is a one-owner change. **Gate:** AC-HE-124 is BLOCKING; Item DB AC-109 (the start kit has no consumer-less def) passes. The edits to Suit, the Item DB, the HUD and Attention are those owners' (Open Question 3); then the consistency check is rerun. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *Cites.* AT:243, SE:201, IDB:249.

**HE-40. Rig presenter, Sanity and the Perception Filter.** *Rule.* A render-side rig presenter takes `(pose_stream, type_id)` and an optional `leak` input that **only the Filter may set**; Horror feeds it real poses with `leak` 0; the Filter (Vertical Slice) may feed phantom or crewmate-driven poses (ART:594 "0 extra frames"; class (c) is driven by the crewmate's true velocity and true beam). Conformance to the Pipeline's row 14: Y-sort with the core's foot row as sort origin, 15-degree quantised segment rotation, pose stepped every 5th tick, `present_time` is not the rig's clock (PR-9), the presenter derives a five-tick interpolation alpha from the kernel's one-tick `tick_alpha` (owed to the Pipeline, C-HE-14), and horror presentation never writes the camera (C1, C9; the jolt API refuses it). Three canonical poses (rest, reach, move) pass the silhouette test: no closed circle, no bilateral symmetry, no four-limb pairs, no emission, iridescence band only under light. A rig instance with no Horror ref in `horror.view` and `leak` 0 is a lint error. Sanity's rulings decide what Horror owes: keep every real entity registered (E6) with a true anchor and publish true positions in `horror.view`; Horror computes no exposure, owns neither `HORROR_NEAR_R` (called `HORROR_SEEN_R` in the Sanity rulings; renamed in the Sanity draft, C-HE-32) nor `EXP_HORROR`, and holds no sanity reference. -> ADR candidate (3). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: Sanity and the Perception Filter are both `Status: Designed`.)* *Cites.* PIPE:303, PCI row 16, ART:594, SN-22.

#### Group I — State, determinism and budget

**HE-41. Block contents and restore.** *Rule.* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* The field list was incomplete: a restore could not reproduce the uninterrupted run. The list below is the closed manifest, committed as a file and checked by AC-HE-76 (the old criterion checked the implementation against its own list, which is how the gaps passed review twice). Persisted per record, in groups. (1) *Identity and place:* an in-block `entity_id` counter, `type_id`, `body_id`, `home_body_id`, `section_id`, `pos_px`, `acc_mpx` (the two-axis sub-pixel residue), `last_octant`, `exo_source_class` (the EXOGENOUS class of the record's `source_ref`, set once at creation, HE-15), and `offset_seen_px` (an int2: the `section_offset(section_id)` this record last used for its body-frame points, written at creation (E6 time), at every run and at ingress; HE-22 re-bases on a mismatch) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. (2) *Behaviour and timers:* `state`, `state_enter_T`, `cooldown_until_T`, `last_seen_tell_epoch`, `epoch_id`. (3) *REACH and STRIKE:* `locked_pt`, `reach_start_T`, `reach_end_T`, `reach_smear_laid`; and, while in STRIKE only, `strike_T`, `seats_hit[]` (player ids ascending, at most `MAX_ACTORS`) and `strike_hull_seg` (the one hull segment deposited on, or none), which are the stored hit cue of E-HE-38. (4) *Navigation goal:* `goal_wp` (the LURK, RETREAT or STALK goal waypoint, a section-local px pair or none), `goal_room`, `goal_commit_runs` (runs left of the commitment, HE-13), `dwell_start_T` (the start of the current LURK dwell, or none), `hold_runs` and `blocked_runs` (the two pathing counters of HE-25; they steer the horror's position, so they must be persisted for save-equivalence) and `held_at_leaf` (HE-26). (5) *Transit and ingress:* `transit_until_T`, `from_body_id`, `transit_to_body_id`, `transit_leg` (OUTBOUND or RETURN: guard 5 cancels only an outbound leg), `ingress_seg` (the chosen HULL_SEGMENT node id), `ingress_anchor_px` (its arrival point) and `ingress_mark_T` (the tick the ingress mark was or will be laid; none until chosen). (6) *Cadence accumulators:* `smear_travel_px` (travel since the last SMEAR, `0..MARK_SPACING_PX−1`) and `chill_travel_px` (travel since the last CHILL deposit, `0..CHILL_SPACING_PX−1`). (7) *Epoch ledgers, reset by guard 2:* `strikes_epoch` (diagnostic, no rule reads it; range `0..STRIKES_EPOCH_MAX`, derived 13 = `floor((BREACH_MAX_TICKS + SUBSIDE_TICKS) / min_gap) + 1`), `gouges_epoch`, `ruptures_epoch`, `chill_epoch_dj`, `chill_deposits_epoch` and `gouged_seg_ids[]` (the hull-segment ids this epoch's GOUGEs landed on, ascending, no duplicates, at most `GOUGES_PER_EPOCH_MAX`; ids only, because the time of a GOUGE can never decide a RUPTURE, HE-F17). Block-level: the mark ring (each mark with its `life_left_ticks`) and the `mark_id` counter, the `"horror.tell"` counter, `fp_window` and `fp_count`, the active disturbance list and the `last_end_T` map (bounded by `L3_MAX_TRACKED`), `lurk_dwell_index`, and `unfold_scale_pct` (the accessibility scale of HE-14, applied after the draw; sim-visible, changed only by the host command of HE-F5, and replays with the world). **Not persisted:** the target, any threat of any kind (none exists), the path cache, `restore_epoch` (Save/Load's value in `slot_epoch.bin`, read through `kernel.clock`, HE-42; *Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R1 as amended by R13/R14*) and the non-hashed `horror.view.debug` record of HE-7. Restore is two-phase with a closed set `HORROR_RESTORE_*` (`SCHEMA`, `RANGE`, `STATE_INCOHERENT`, `SECTION_UNKNOWN`, `RNG_COUNTER`, `MARK_RING`); an out-of-range value is a hard failure, never clamped (SL-15). `RANGE` covers: `goal_commit_runs` outside `0..GOAL_COMMIT_RUNS`; `smear_travel_px` or `chill_travel_px` at or above its spacing; `gouged_seg_ids` longer than `GOUGES_PER_EPOCH_MAX` or than `gouges_epoch`, unsorted or duplicated; `strikes_epoch` above `STRIKES_EPOCH_MAX`; `seats_hit[]` longer than `MAX_ACTORS`; `exo_source_class` other than EXOGENOUS; `transit_leg` outside {OUTBOUND, RETURN}; `dwell_start_T`, `strike_T` or `reach_start_T` later than the restore tick; `ingress_mark_T` more than `INGRESS_MARK_LEAD_TICKS` after `transit_until_T`. `STATE_INCOHERENT` covers: `transit_leg` or any `ingress_*` field set outside TRANSIT; `strike_T`, `seats_hit` or `strike_hull_seg` set outside STRIKE; `locked_pt`, `reach_start_T`, `reach_end_T` or `reach_smear_laid` set outside REACH and STRIKE; `held_at_leaf` set outside STALK. `SECTION_UNKNOWN` covers a `home_body_id`, `goal_wp` or `ingress_anchor_px` in a body or section Layout does not know. `RNG_COUNTER` covers a `"horror.tell"` counter that is negative, non-integer or at or above `RESTORE_KEY_STRIDE`. A restore **into REACH** keeps the full remaining telegraph (never shortened; mirrors AT E-51) and re-checks the predicate; if it no longer holds, RETREAT with no strike. A restore into TRANSIT with `transit_until_T` in the past is legal (arrive next run); a TRANSIT record with a registered subject, or a non-TRANSIT record without a legal section, is `STATE_INCOHERENT`. All explicit dirty keys; plain data only, no `StringName` keys or `Vector2i`; restore is idempotent. -> ADR candidate (5). *Cites.* SL-15, SL:221-232, AT E-51.

**HE-42. Determinism.** *Rule.* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R1)* The two draw kernels (Attention's `restore_epoch` key and a Horror-private epoch seed) are replaced by the one below; Attention's AT-8 calls the same function. All simulation randomness is drawn through the kernel's one helper **`rng_derive_epoch(world_seed, "horror.tell", counter)` = `rng_derive(world_seed, "horror.tell", counter_eff)` with `counter_eff = counter + RESTORE_KEY_STRIDE × restore_epoch`** (KRN D.10; the kernel defines it, Horror only calls it and defines no alias), used for the REACH length only (the stream-name hash stays cached and unchanged; Attention and Horror call it and neither defines its own) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: kernel name `rng_derive_epoch`)*. `restore_epoch` is a persisted u32 **owned by Save/Load** (starts at 0), held in the per-slot side file `slot_epoch.bin` (u32 + CRC32, written atomically by temp file and rename by the Save/Load worker); it is in no generation file, so generation bytes and every trailer SHA never change, and each generation header carries only a read-only copy for recovery (R14). A death-stub restore advances it by 1; a Continue of a `QUIT` generation that is the newest non-demoted verified generation carries it unchanged (R13, user ruling D16: not steerable by quit); a Continue of any other generation (after a kill, crash or fallback), in-process or after a fresh process start, advances it. Save/Load writes an advance in a pre-restore staging step and calls `request_restore(gen, restore_epoch)` only after `EPOCH_DURABLE`; on a failed write or after `RESTORE_EPOCH_WRITE_MAX_MS` (500 ms placeholder) it proceeds with the advanced epoch in memory, logs `SL_EPOCH_NOT_DURABLE` and sets `save_status = FAILED` (a listed exposure: a kill before a later successful write may replay that epoch once). So two death restores of one autosave give two new epochs, and a Save & Quit then Continue replays the same REACH lengths: quit-and-continue is no reroll oracle *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R1 as amended by R13/R14; cross-GDD fix)*. The kernel exposes it read-only in `kernel.clock` as `(T, world_seed, restore_epoch)`, sim-visible and pure. `RESTORE_KEY_STRIDE` is 1,048,576 (2^20) and is the kernel's constant (registry request: its source moves from Attention to the kernel, with Attention, Horror and Save/Load in `referenced_by`); the lint `counter < RESTORE_KEY_STRIDE` holds. Horror reads `restore_epoch` only through `rng_derive_epoch` at the draw and at no other time *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: kernel name `rng_derive_epoch`)*, never stores it in `horror.state` and never decides when it advances; the Graph's physical failures and every persisted counter never read it. The persisted `"horror.tell"` counter is restored exactly, so no draw is reused at one epoch and the counter never moves backwards, and a REACH already in flight keeps its stored length (`reach_end_T`, E-HE-36). **Determinism (G4) is defined given the pair (save bytes, `restore_epoch`)**: the same pair replays identically (strike lengths, positions, marks, `state_hash`), tests pin the epoch, and a restore that advances the epoch changes only the lengths of REACHes not yet begun. Nothing physical is re-keyed: mark seeds, LURK waypoint choice and L3 timing are pure integer hashes of ids, the run index and `HE_HASH_SEED` through `he_hash` (below), so the trail and the positions the player can read in the world replay exactly (Pillar 1); they are not threat draws, since the REACH length is Horror's only draw. No wall clock and no global RNG (H9). **`he_hash`** *(defined 2026-10-27 — re-review: the earlier text said "pure integer hash" with no function or sign rule)*: `he_hash(k1, k2, k3, salt) = lsr(mix64( mix64(k1 ⊕ HE_HASH_SEED) ⊕ mix64(k2 ⊕ salt) ⊕ (k3 × GOLDEN) ), 1)`, on the kernel's pinned `mix64`, `lsr` and `GOLDEN` (kernel D.10) with the intended wrap on the multiplication; the final logical shift makes the result non-negative (63 bits), so every `mod` in this GDD acts on a non-negative integer and no reduction depends on a sign convention. `HE_HASH_SEED` defaults to `0x4845` (any fixed integer; changing it re-rolls every mark seed, LURK waypoint and L3 timing). The salts are fixed constants: `SALT_MARK` 1, `SALT_WP` 2, `SALT_L3_START` 3, `SALT_L3_STRENGTH` 4, `SALT_L3_DUR` 5. Callers: mark seed `he_hash(mark_id, 0, 0, SALT_MARK)`; waypoint rank `he_hash(entity_id, dwell_index, anchor_local_key, SALT_WP)`; L3 `h1`, `h2`, `h3` = `he_hash(emitter_id, run_n, 0, SALT_L3_START)`, `…SALT_L3_STRENGTH)`, `…SALT_L3_DUR)`. Golden vectors are pinned by AC-HE-139. -> ADR candidate (4): a shared pure-integer presentation hash with Lighting LO-19. *Cites.* KRN D.10, D.12, H9.

**HE-43. Budget.** *Rule.* Target cost of `horror.step` is `HORROR_STEP_BUDGET_MS` 0.15 ms P99 per run (a share of the kernel's 2.0 ms P3 target; GDScript operation cost is unsourced and is a spike parameter; the kernel's `STEP_BUDGET_MS` 4 is a logging threshold). Per run: at most the number of seats in `lux` reads (up to 4), at most 5 `solid_in` queries, at most `PATH_EXPANSIONS_MAX` expansions on a cache miss, 0 probes, at most `MARKS_PER_RUN_MAX` mark inserts, at most `MAX_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN` M17 calls, at most one `apply_suit_damage` per seat. *Cites.* REG `STEP_BUDGET_MS`.

**HE-44. Reserved formula and constant index.** The Formulas section writes these ids; rules above cite them.

### States and Transitions

The record's `state` takes one of six values: `LURK`, `STALK`, `REACH`, `STRIKE`, `RETREAT`, `TRANSIT`. The first five are the cycle; `TRANSIT` is a **sixth value, additive** to the five the audio facts need (C-HE-17): relocation through a hull needs a visible place in the machine and has no position. "Permitted act" is what the state may do to the world. Dormancy (HE-21) is not a state: a record whose body is unloaded is frozen in whatever state it holds, with no step.

#### State table

| State | Entry (from, guard) | Phases it lives in | Permitted act | Registered subject | Motion | Exit (first match wins) | Who may cause the transition |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **LURK** | boot or default; STALK on a TELL abort; RETREAT when the phase is CALM or TELL and the record is not returning; TRANSIT arrival while the phase is CALM | CALM (any other phase only transiently) | marks, CHILL, disturbance dips; no RUPTURE, no GOUGE, no suit damage; never within `LURK_STANDOFF_PX` of a living seat | yes | `V_CREEP_MPX` between waypoints; dwell `LURK_DWELL_TICKS` | `attention.view.phase` = TELL with a new `tell_epoch` → STALK (locus body is its body) or TRANSIT (another body; evaluated first, so it fires even when the record's own body is unloaded); return due → TRANSIT; body unloaded and the locus body is its own or none → frozen *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | **Attention's phase only** (HE-7); never the player's light, noise or distance |
| **STALK** | LURK on TELL entry; RETREAT when the cooldown has elapsed in BREACH; TRANSIT arrival in TELL or BREACH | TELL, BREACH | marks, CHILL, disturbance. TELL: the standoff holds, no harm. BREACH: may begin a REACH under HE-14 | yes | TELL `V_CREEP_MPX`; BREACH `V_HUNT_MPX` | TELL abort (phase back to CALM) → LURK; BREACH and HE-14 (a) to (f) true → REACH; SUBSIDING → RETREAT; no lit seat → the anchor becomes the target (still STALK) | `attention.view` fields and the stateless `lux_pm >= LIT_ON_PM` read of HE-13 (not the Emitter's `lit` bit); the abort is Attention's *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| **REACH** | STALK in BREACH when the predicate holds at begin | BREACH (resolves in BREACH or SUBSIDING) | lays the touch-down SMEAR at P at the start of the hold stage (a pre-hold fizzle lays none, HE-14); publishes `reach_start_T`, `locked_pt` and `reach_end_T`; no harm yet; rooted | yes | none | `T >= reach_end_T` and the predicate holds → STRIKE; the predicate false at that run, or the phase CALM, or a restore with the predicate false → RETREAT (fizzle: no harm, no hairlines, no GOUGE or RUPTURE, no cooldown; an already-laid touch-down smear stays); `SUBSIDE_CANCELS_REACH` 1 and SUBSIDING → RETREAT (fizzle) | the predicate (Attention's `exogenous_permitted`); the horror's own clock |
| **STRIKE** | REACH resolve | BREACH or SUBSIDING | on the entry run only: `apply_suit_damage` per HE-15; GOUGE and RUPTURE per HE-16; hairline mark; publishes `strike_T` and the seats hit | yes | none (the snap pose) | after `STRIKE_HOLD_RUNS` runs → RETREAT | the horror's own clock |
| **RETREAT** | STRIKE; a fizzle; SUBSIDING entry; (Vertical Slice) a `repel` | BREACH, SUBSIDING, then CALM | marks only | yes | `V_CREEP_MPX` away from every living seat | `T >= cooldown_until_T` and BREACH → STALK; CALM or TELL and not returning → LURK; return due → TRANSIT | Attention's phase; the horror's own clock |
| **TRANSIT** | TELL entry with `body_of(locus_section_id)` not its own (also from an unloaded body, HE-20); return due (`RETURN_DELAY_TICKS`); its section lost to a cut (HE-22) | any | none; no position, no marks (the egress and ingress marks are laid at the segments, not by the traveller), not registered | no | none | `T >= transit_until_T` and a legal entry (HE-20) → STALK (TELL or BREACH), LURK (CALM) or RETREAT (SUBSIDING), with E6 and an ingress mark; a TELL abort while outbound cancels the arrival and retargets to `from_body_id` (HE-20); a new `tell_epoch` retargets to the new locus body *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | Attention's locus; Layout for legality |

#### Phase view (state against phase: the single table a reviewer tests against)

| Phase | LURK | STALK | REACH | STRIKE | RETREAT | TRANSIT |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CALM | resident | → LURK (after an abort) | fizzle | n/a | → LURK | an abort cancels an outbound arrival (the record heads back; it never arrives as LURK from an abort) |
| TELL | → STALK | creep, standoff holds, no harm | cannot begin | n/a | → LURK | arrives as STALK |
| BREACH | n/a | hunt; may begin a REACH | the telegraph | harm (predicate) | cooldown | arrives as STALK |
| SUBSIDING | n/a | → RETREAT | resolves or fizzles | harm only if resolving | stays | arrives as RETREAT |

*(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the CALM/TRANSIT cell no longer reads "arrives as LURK" after an abort, HE-20; a TRANSIT record that reached its arrival run in CALM for any other reason, such as a return home completing, still arrives as LURK.)*

A TELL abort moves the phase from TELL to CALM; the record in STALK returns to LURK with no strike and no mark burst (HE-12). A record in RETREAT during a second TELL goes RETREAT → LURK → STALK on the new `tell_epoch`; it never skips LURK. Predicate check points (each is an acceptance criterion): REACH begin (HE-14 c), STRIKE entry (HE-15), every M17 RUPTURE or GOUGE call (HE-16, HE-29). All three read `exogenous_permitted` at their own tick. CHILL is a non-harm footprint, permitted in any phase, bounded by HE-F4 and HE-31 (C-HE-28).

#### Transition guards (ordered; the first match wins within a run)

1. **Restore-first.** A run that follows a restore re-checks the predicate for any record in REACH (HE-41) before any other guard.
2. **Tell edge (new `tell_epoch`; runs for every record in every run, ahead of dormancy)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; was guard 4, which sat behind dormancy so a record whose body was unloaded never saw the edge)*. If `tell_epoch != last_seen_tell_epoch`: zero the five epoch counters and empty `gouged_seg_ids`, set `last_seen_tell_epoch := tell_epoch`, and take a record in REACH to a fizzle (HE-17: no harm, no cooldown consumed) and a record in STRIKE out of its hold, each going to RETREAT in this run (a new `tell_epoch` means the previous breach is over) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; then LURK → STALK (the locus body is the record's body; a record found in RETREAT or STALK passes through LURK first, never skipping it, E-HE-16) or LURK / STALK / RETREAT → TRANSIT (another body; a departure from an unloaded body is allowed, HE-20, and is the only act an unloaded record performs); a record already in TRANSIT retargets to the new locus body (HE-20). Nothing else in the run may act on the record before this check.
3. **Dormancy** *(after the registration reconciliation of HE-34, which runs between guards 2 and 3)*. The record's body is not loaded and the record is not in TRANSIT → no step (HE-21). (A TRANSIT record has no body; its wait for an unloaded destination is HE-21's second rule.)
4. **Layout change.** `layout.state` changed since the last run, or `version(section_id)` changed for a section of the record's body (a door opened or closed) → first the door-eject check of HE-26 (a leaf closed on the horror's foot box), then invalidate the path cache and re-derive `body_id` from `body_of(section_id)` (HE-22) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.
5. **Phase edge (the rest).** The phase returned to CALM from TELL (an abort) → STALK → LURK, or an outbound TRANSIT (`transit_leg` OUTBOUND) is cancelled and retargeted to `from_body_id` with `transit_leg` RETURN (HE-20); SUBSIDING → STALK → RETREAT. A record in REACH or STRIKE is untouched by this guard (guard 6 resolves it).
6. **Cycle guards** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. **Independent of phase:** REACH → STRIKE or fizzle at `T >= reach_end_T` (a REACH begun in BREACH resolves in SUBSIDING, HE-17, and fizzles in CALM or when `SUBSIDE_CANCELS_REACH` is 1) and STRIKE → RETREAT after `STRIKE_HOLD_RUNS`. **In BREACH only:** STALK → REACH (HE-14) and RETREAT → STALK.
7. **Return.** `RETURN_DELAY_TICKS` after the end of SUBSIDING or a TELL abort and not in the home body → TRANSIT.

**Epoch counters.** `strikes_epoch`, `gouges_epoch`, `ruptures_epoch`, `chill_epoch_dj` and `chill_deposits_epoch` reset **lazily, keyed on the comparison `tell_epoch != last_seen_tell_epoch` and not on observing the phase edge itself**: at the first run in which a record sees that inequality (guard 2, which runs first), the five counters are zeroed, `gouged_seg_ids` is emptied and `last_seen_tell_epoch` is updated before any act in that run. A record that slept through one or more TELLs, or a save restored from an older epoch, therefore wakes with zero counters and its caps bind correctly. Caps bound each told epoch, and CALM activity after a breach counts against that epoch's remaining allowance until the next TELL. `epoch_id` (HE-41) is set to the new `tell_epoch` at the same moment *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.

#### Published fields by state (`horror.view`)

| State | Published beyond the always-present `entity_id`, `type_id`, `state`, `body_id`, `section_id`, `pos_px` |
|---|---|
| LURK, STALK, RETREAT | `state_enter_T`; the disturbance list; diagnostic counters |
| REACH | `reach_start_T`, `locked_pt`, `reach_end_T` (the rig and Audio read them; the player reads the limb; `reach_start_T` keeps replay and TellAudit able to see when the REACH began although the smear is laid later) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| STRIKE | `strike_T`, the seats hit, the hull segments gouged or ruptured, the `tell_epoch` it ran in (TellAudit) |
| TRANSIT | `transit_until_T`, `transit_leg`, the pending ingress segment once chosen; no `pos_px` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |

#### Persisted timers and counters (all in `horror.state`; see HE-41)

All fields of HE-41, and nothing else. The timers: `state_enter_T`, `reach_start_T`, `reach_end_T`, `cooldown_until_T`, `transit_until_T`, `ingress_mark_T`, `dwell_start_T`, `strike_T` and `last_seen_tell_epoch`. The counters: the `"horror.tell"` counter, `fp_count` for `fp_window`, the per-epoch ledgers (`strikes_epoch`, `gouges_epoch`, `ruptures_epoch`, `chill_epoch_dj`, `chill_deposits_epoch`, `gouged_seg_ids`), `hold_runs`, `blocked_runs`, `goal_commit_runs`, `smear_travel_px`, `chill_travel_px`, `lurk_dwell_index` and the `mark_id` counter. The mark ring with each mark's `life_left_ticks`, and the disturbance list with `last_end_T`. A restore reconstructs every state from these, never from the previous process's memory (G4 save-equivalence, given the pair (save, `restore_epoch`)); the committed field manifest (AC-HE-76) is the check that this list and HE-41 stay one list *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.

#### Cycle timing at the placeholders

Minimum interval between two strike resolutions for a seat that has not moved is `STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS + UNFOLD_LO` = 395 ticks (6.6 s) when the horror is already in reach range. `UNFOLD_TICKS` is `5 × rng_int(19, 36)`: 18 equally likely values from 95 to 180 with mean 137.5, so the mean interval is 437.5 ticks (7.3 s) and the longest is 480 ticks (8.0 s). That is a lower bound: after a strike the horror retreats (96 ticks out at `V_CREEP_MPX`, inside the cooldown), turns at `cooldown_until_T` and walks back at `V_HUNT_MPX` (64 ticks, 65 on the run grid), so the realised period of a seat that does not move is `UNFOLD_TICKS + STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS + 65` = 460 to 545 ticks (7.7 to 9.1 s), mean 502.5 (registered formula HE-F15). The time to a third strike for a seat that never moves, and the share of minimum-length breaches that contain a third REACH start (none), are derived in HE-F16 and are not carried here.

### Interactions with Other Systems

Reading guide. "Neighbour's own row" quotes the dependency GDD's text about Horror Entities verbatim (columns of a quoted table row are separated by ` ‖ `), so that bidirectional consistency can be read in one place. "Path" is the kernel's CR-9 path: (a) a published unconditional mutator called from the caller's own hook, (b) a `SYSTEM`-origin command processed at T+1 when the target may reject. **Horror uses path (a) only and submits no `SYSTEM` command at MVP.** "Class A / Class B" is the mutator class (Class B clamps, never rejects). "Status" is the neighbour's state at authoring time. Rows for undesigned neighbours list **supplies and assumptions only** and are `[PROVISIONAL]`, each registered with the owner named.

KRN:687 Dependencies: "Horror Entities ‖ Hard ‖ Ordered step, RNG streams, `SYSTEM` commands ‖ Entity block, footprint mutator calls ‖ Yes" | tick `T`; `kernel.clock.restore_epoch`; `rng_derive_epoch(world_seed, "horror.tell", counter)`, the kernel's single restore-epoch helper (HE-42, user ruling X8/D16) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R1)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: kernel name `rng_derive_epoch`)*; `read_view`s; `layout_hash` via Layout | block `horror.state`; hook `horror.step`; `horror.view` | CR-3 hook tuple (HE-3); CR-8 block registry (HE-41); CR-9 path (a) mutators (HE-28); D.10 `rng_derive` (HE-42); D.12 `state_hash`; CR-12 `PERCEPTION_OUT` isolation; H5, H9, H10 | Agrees. ## Formulas

Conventions. Every simulated quantity is an **integer** (ticks, base px, milli-px, basis points, permille, deci-joules, milli-degrees), because anything Horror hashes or replicates must be bit-identical on replay (kernel D.12 `state_hash`, H9): no float, no `sqrt`, no wall clock. Distances are compared as squared integers. Two formulas are analysis only and use real arithmetic (HE-F16's Poisson term and HE-F18's presentation alpha); neither is ever hashed or replicated. `ceil5(x)` rounds `x` up to the next multiple of `HORROR_RUN_TICKS` (5, DERIVED from the hook divisor, HE-4). Locked constants are cited by registry name with the registry value; the Graph's constants (`C_TILE`, `FOOTPRINT_MAX_Q`, `FOOTPRINT_MAX_GOUGE_BP`, `RUPTURE_HP`, `BREACH_HP`, `STRESS_HOLD_STEPS`, registered 2026-10-27) are cited with the registry value and their Life-Support Graph line and never restated as Horror's own. Each two-bound relation below is a **registry-formula candidate** (reflexion 2: register the formula, not only its constants); the registry entries are written from this final text by a later stage, never from the specialist drafts. `ceil_div(a, b) = (a + b − 1) div b` for `a >= 0`, `b > 0`; `he_hash` is defined in HE-42; every integer division in this section is on a non-negative operand *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. `[PH]` is a placeholder; ★ marks a value a neighbour quotes.

### HE-F1 Per-window footprint budget

The per-window footprint budget formula is defined as:

`n_w ≤ HE_FOOTPRINT_BUDGET = MAX_FOOTPRINTS_PER_STEP − STRESS_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN;  n_run ≤ MAX_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN;  2 × MAX_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN ≤ HE_FOOTPRINT_BUDGET`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `n_w` | int | 0..8 | block (`fp_count` for `fp_window`) | Horror's accepted M17 calls in one Graph window, where a window is *the deposits the next `graph.propagate` run drains*: writes at `T` in `[6k, 6k+5]` drain at the Graph run at `6(k+1)` (HE-29) |
| `n_run` | int | 0..4 | block | Accepted M17 calls in one `horror.step` run |
| `MAX_FOOTPRINTS_PER_STEP` | int | 16 (CITED) | Life-Support Graph, registry (LSG:807) | The Graph's honoured M17 writes per run |
| `STRESS_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN` | int | 1..16, [PH] 8 (CITED) | Attention, registry | Attention's claim on the same cap; Horror leaves it whole |
| `MAX_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN` ★ | int | 1..4, [PH] 4 | Horror | Per-run cap so that the two Horror runs that can share one window sum to the budget |
| `HE_FOOTPRINT_BUDGET` | int | DERIVED 8 | this formula | Never registered with a free value |

**Output Range:** `n_w` in 0..8 and `n_run` in 0..4. Over-budget writes are *not attempted* and are counted `horror_footprints_withheld`; in nominal play that counter is 0 (a defect signal, never a gameplay state). Natural worst case per run: one hull deposit (RUPTURE or GOUGE, one per STRIKE, HE-F17) and one CHILL = 2; the three-kind run of E-HE-13 is a stub fixture for the priority rule (priority RUPTURE, GOUGE, CHILL).

**Example:** window `k = 5` holds the Horror runs at `T = 30` and `T = 35` (one window in five holds two runs because LCM(5, 6) = 30). The run at 30 issues GOUGE + CHILL (2), the run at 35 issues CHILL (1): `n_5 = 3 ≤ 8`. *Saturating double* (AC-AT-92 pattern): a test stub requests 20 deposits in window 5; the first run is granted 4 (cap), the second 4, the other 12 are withheld and `horror_footprints_withheld += 12`; with Attention's 8 STRESS writes the Graph sees 16 and drops none. Windows 1 to 4 hold one Horror run each (`T = 10, 15, 20, 25`).

**Registry candidate:** `horror_footprint_budget`.

### HE-F2 Strike geometry and walk-out time

The strike geometry and walk-out formula is defined as:

`hit(seat) = living(seat) ∧ ¬DOWNED(seat) ∧ ¬DEAD(seat) ∧ actor_section(seat) = section(P) ∧ room_at(seat) = room_at(P) ∧ (dx² + dy²) < R_SNAP_PX² ∧ exogenous_permitted(actor_section(seat));   t_out(V, o)` is the first tick count `n` at which a straight walk in octant `o` stands outside the disc, which for a cardinal octant is `ceil(R_SNAP_PX / V)` and for a diagonal octant is the first `n` with `2 × floor(181 × V × n / 256)² >= R_SNAP_PX²` (Body's axis step, BDY-13, with `sub` reset to 0 when the walk starts, BDY-12); `t_out_max(V)` is the maximum over the 8 octants *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier `t_out(V) = ceil(R_SNAP_PX / V)` was the cardinal case only; a diagonal walk-out takes one tick longer)*

with `dx = seat.x − P.x` and `dy = seat.y − P.y`, the seat's foot point read after `body.integrate` on the resolving tick (HE-3).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `seat.x`, `seat.y` | int px | world px | Body `pos_curr(actor_id)` | The seat's foot point |
| `P` | int px pair | body-frame px (HE-22) | `horror.state` (`locked_pt`) | The locked strike point; the seat's foot point at REACH start; never updated, except the HE-22 frame re-base after a weld or cut, which moves the same physical point into the new body frame *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* |
| `R_SNAP_PX` ★ | int px | 48..96, [PH] 72 | Horror (kept equal to Interaction's `REACH_PX` 72 on purpose, one disc size teaches both reaches) | Strike radius; the disc is **strict** |
| `V` | int px/tick | `V_WALK` 2, `V_RUN` 3, `V_CARRY` 1 (CITED) | Player Controller; Body (BDY-13) | Locomotion class of the escaping seat; a CRITICAL walk-only seat moves at `V_WALK` |
| `room_at` | room id | Layout | Layout | A seat on a door sill belongs to the sill's room; the test stops a strike through a wall |
| `t_out(V, o)` | int ticks | 24..73 | this formula | Ticks of straight movement in octant `o` needed to stand on the disc boundary |

**Output Range:** `hit` is a boolean. The walk-out table, derived with Body's `LocomotionStep` (`UNIT` 256 cardinal, 181 diagonal; the fixture of AC-HE-130 recomputes it from the real stepper): `t_out(V_WALK)` = 36 on the 4 cardinal octants and **37 on the 4 diagonal octants** (diagonal cadence 1,1,2,1,2,1,1,2: after 36 ticks each axis has moved 50 px and `2 × 50² = 5000 < 5184` is still a hit; after 37 ticks 52 px and `5408 >= 5184` is clear); `t_out(V_RUN)` = 24 cardinal, 25 diagonal; `t_out(V_CARRY)` = 72 cardinal, 73 diagonal. So `t_out_max(V_WALK) = 37`, `t_out_max(V_RUN) = 25`, `t_out_max(V_CARRY) = 73`. A seat that does not move is hit; a reactive walker is not (HE-F5 carries the reaction budget) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.

**Example:** `P = (400, 300)`, `R_SNAP_PX² = 5184`. A seat standing on `P` that starts walking east at 2 px/tick has `dx = 70` after 35 ticks (`4900 < 5184`, hit) and `dx = 72` after 36 ticks (`5184 < 5184` is false, clear). The strict disc is why 36 ticks suffice on a cardinal octant (37 on a diagonal one, see the Output Range). A seat 60 px west of `P` but in the next room (different `room_at`) is not hit. Two seats at `dx = 10` and `dx = 50` in the same room are each hit once: two `apply_suit_damage` calls, one per seat. A DOWNED seat inside the disc is skipped.

**Registry candidate:** `strike_hit_test`.

### HE-F3 Integrity after k strikes (the `STRIKE_BP` two-bound)

The integrity-after-k-strikes formula is defined as:

`I_k = max(0, I_0 − k × STRIKE_BP);   k_fail(I) = floor((I − SUIT_HP_FAIL) / STRIKE_BP) + 1;   bounds: SUIT_HP_FULL − SUIT_HP_OK < STRIKE_BP  and  2 × STRIKE_BP < SUIT_HP_FULL − SUIT_HP_FAIL  and  SUIT_PATCH_CAP_BP − STRIKE_BP ≥ SUIT_HP_FAIL` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `I_0`, `I` | int bp | 0..`SUIT_HP_FULL` | Suit (Horror never reads it) | Suit integrity before the first strike |
| `STRIKE_BP` ★ | int bp | 3001..3500, [PH] 3200 | Horror (Suit-scale amount) | One strike's integrity cost; the interval is derived from the three bounds (the third is new, *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*) |
| `SUIT_HP_FULL` / `SUIT_HP_OK` / `SUIT_LEAK_HP` / `SUIT_HP_FAIL` | int bp | 10000 / 7000 / 5000 / 2500 (CITED) | Suit, registry | Rung thresholds |
| `SUIT_PATCH_CAP_BP` | int bp | 6000 default, range 5100..6900 (CITED) | Suit, registry | Cap of `patch_integrity`; no passive regeneration exists. The third bound needs `cap >= STRIKE_BP + SUIT_HP_FAIL`: 5700 at the default `STRIKE_BP`, 6000 over the whole `STRIKE_BP` range. The registered floor of 5100 does not satisfy it, so the data load rejects that pairing rather than letting one strike kill a patched suit; a cross-GDD note to Suit asks for the range floor to follow (Open Question 34) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |

**Output Range:** `I_k` in 0..10000; `k_fail(10000) = 3` for **every** `STRIKE_BP` in 3001..3500 (`7500/3001 = 2.499`, `7500/3500 = 2.14`, so the floor plus one is 3 across the whole range). The third bound means **a single strike never kills a patched suit**: the patch cap is a ceiling and `cap − STRIKE_BP >= SUIT_HP_FAIL` (2800 left at the default, exactly 2500 at the corner `STRIKE_BP` 3500 with cap 6000, which is not below `SUIT_HP_FAIL`); without it the registered ranges allowed a one-strike death (cap 5100..5699 at `STRIKE_BP` 3200, or `STRIKE_BP` 3501..3749 at cap 6000), which would break the told-strikes promise of Pillar 2 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. **`STRIKE_BP` is not a lethality dial inside its registered range**; the dials are attempts per breach, inattention (HE-F16's `p`), breaches per session and repair availability. The first bound takes strike one below `SUIT_HP_OK` (STRAINED, inert by Suit's design; the strike itself is the witness, HE-15); the second means two strikes never kill a full suit.

**Example:** `STRIKE_BP` 3200 from 10000: `I_1 = 6800` (below `SUIT_HP_OK`, STRAINED, inert), `I_2 = 3600` (below `SUIT_LEAK_HP`, CRITICAL, stagger, walk-only), `I_3 = 400` (below `SUIT_HP_FAIL`, FAILED → DOWNED → `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` 1800 → DEAD, Suit's ladder and Save/Load's restore). *One-way ratchet:* a worn suit dies sooner. `k_fail(7000) = floor(4500/3200) + 1 = 2`; a patched suit starts from at most `SUIT_PATCH_CAP_BP` 6000, `k_fail(6000) = floor(3500/3200) + 1 = 2` (first strike → 2800, CRITICAL; second → 0, FAILED). The three strikes need not occur in one breach, but after the first landed strike and any patch, two strikes kill. Edges of the range: `STRIKE_BP` 3500 leaves 3000 after two strikes from 10000 (survives) and exactly 2500 for a patched suit at cap 6000 after one (survives: 2500 is not below `SUIT_HP_FAIL`); 3501 would leave 2499 and kill a patched suit in one strike, so it is outside the range (the earlier ceiling of 3749 allowed it) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. AC-HE-86 sweeps these corners.

**Registry candidate:** `strike_integrity_bounds`.

### HE-F4 CHILL cumulative bound

The CHILL cumulative bound formula is defined as:

`ΔT_mC(Q) = floor(1000 × Q / (C_TILE × n_tiles));   CHILL_EPOCH_MAX_DJ = CHILL_DEPOSITS_PER_EPOCH_MAX × CHILL_STEP_DJ;   lint: ΔT_mC(CHILL_EPOCH_MAX_DJ) at n_tiles_min ≤ CHILL_EPOCH_MAX_MC;   runtime: deposit iff T_c − ΔT_mC(CHILL_STEP_DJ) ≥ COLD_DMG_ONSET_MC + CHILL_MARGIN_MC;   CHILL_STEP_DJ ≤ FOOTPRINT_MAX_Q`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Q` | int dJ | 0..`CHILL_EPOCH_MAX_DJ` | this formula | Heat removed by one deposit or one epoch of deposits |
| `C_TILE` | int dJ per °C per tile | 3000 | Life-Support Graph, registry (LSG:458, :937; CITED) | Heat capacity per tile |
| `n_tiles` | int | compartment tile count | Layout | Tiles in the deposited compartment |
| `n_tiles_min` | int | smallest authored compartment | Layout, Derelict Authoring *(provisional — not published; 8 assumed in the example)* | Worst case for the lint |
| `T_c` | int mC | −40000..70000+ | Graph `compartment(cid)` | The compartment's current temperature |
| `COLD_DMG_ONSET_MC` | int mC | −15000, range −25000..−5000 (CITED) | Life-Support Graph, registry | Where cold begins to harm a body |
| `CHILL_MARGIN_MC` | int mC | 2000..10000, [PH] 5000 | Horror | Runtime margin kept above the onset |
| `CHILL_STEP_DJ` ★ | int dJ | 1000..`FOOTPRINT_MAX_Q`, [PH] 3000 | Horror | One deposit; `FOOTPRINT_MAX_Q` 20000 is the Graph's cap (registry, LSG:912, CITED) |
| `CHILL_DEPOSITS_PER_EPOCH_MAX` | int | 1..48, [PH] 24 | Horror | Deposits per `tell_epoch` |
| `CHILL_EPOCH_MAX_MC` | int mC | 2000..8000, [PH] 5000 | Horror | Lint ceiling on the epoch total |

**Output Range:** one deposit in a 20-tile room is 50 mC; one epoch's maximum is 1200 mC (20 tiles), 2000 mC (12 tiles), 3000 mC (8 tiles). A deposit whose runtime check fails, or whose epoch cap is spent, is **not made** (counters `horror_chill_withheld_cold`, `horror_chill_withheld_cap`); the rime mark is **still laid** in those two cases (ring-only: the mark is a true surface record that the web passed, not a thermal claim), and withheld in the third case only, a coolant fitting within `FITTING_CLEARANCE_PX` (HE-31), where neither deposit nor mark is made.

**Example:** `CHILL_EPOCH_MAX_DJ = 24 × 3000 = 72000`. In a 20-tile room `ΔT = 1000 × 72000 / (3000 × 20) = 1200` mC (1.2 °C); the lint at `n_tiles_min` 8 gives 3000 mC ≤ 5000, pass. Runtime: the threshold is `−15000 + 5000 = −10000` mC. A room at −9000 mC deposits (`−9000 − 50 = −9050 ≥ −10000`); a room at −9990 mC withholds (`−10040 < −10000`). *Why this is bookkeeping:* taking a 20 °C, 20-tile room below the Graph's COOL band (under 10 °C) needs `10000 mC × 3000 × 20 / 1000 = 600000` dJ, which is 200 deposits at 3000, and Horror is capped at 24 per epoch; the rime on the floor is a surface mark, and no instrument or body cue disagrees with it (HE-27).

**Registry candidate:** `chill_cumulative_bound`.

### HE-F5 REACH reaction bound

The REACH reaction bound formula is defined as:

`UNFOLD_LO ≥ ceil5( t_out_max(V_WALK) + REACT_ALLOW_TICKS_HORROR + INPUT_LATENCY_TICKS + 1 );   UNFOLD_LO mod HORROR_RUN_TICKS = 0;   UNFOLD_HI ≥ UNFOLD_LO;   UNFOLD_TICKS = HORROR_RUN_TICKS × ceil_div( rng_int(UNFOLD_LO / 5, UNFOLD_HI / 5) × UNFOLD_SCALE_PCT, 100 )`  *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the bound now uses `t_out_max`, the slowest of Body's 8 octants (37 ticks, diagonal), carries an explicit input-latency term, and the drawn length can be lengthened, never shortened, by the accessibility scale)*

The `+ 1` is the kernel's command application at `apply_tick ≥ T + 1` (KRN D.4): a walk command issued on the last reaction tick moves the seat one tick later. **Tick-indexing convention** (stated once; the slack below is derived under it) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: the REACH begins on the Horror run at tick `R` (`reach_start_T`), which executes after `body.integrate` of tick `R` (HE-3); a command the player issues during tick `R + a` is applied at `apply_tick >= R + a + 1` and first moves the seat in the `body.integrate` of tick `R + a + 1`; the strike resolves on the Horror run at `R + U` after that tick's `body.integrate`, so the seat has taken `U − a` steps and is clear iff `U − a >= t_out_max`, that is `U >= a + t_out_max` (91 for `a` = 54 on a diagonal octant). The bound above carries the `+ 1` on top of that count as a deliberate margin, so under this convention the exact slack at `U` = 95 is 4 ticks (5 cardinal), and the 3 (4) quoted in this document is the conservative figure that also spends the margin; AC-HE-130 pins the real kernel order, and a kernel change that applied commands later than `T + 1` would consume the margin first. `rng_int` is drawn from `rng_derive_epoch(world_seed, "horror.tell", counter)`, through the kernel's one restore-epoch helper (`counter_eff = c + RESTORE_KEY_STRIDE × restore_epoch`; kernel D.10; HE-42; user ruling X8/D16) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R1)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: kernel name `rng_derive_epoch`)*; the stream is Horror's REACH length only and is **not** a tell length (HE-9). The scale is applied after the draw in integer order, `UNFOLD_TICKS = 5 × ceil_div(draw × UNFOLD_SCALE_PCT, 100)` (draw 21 at scale 105: `ceil_div(2205, 100) = 23`, so 115); the rounding order is fixed, so there is no sign or rounding ambiguity *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `R_SNAP_PX` | int px | 72 | HE-F2 | Disc radius |
| `t_out_max(V_WALK)` | int ticks | 37 | HE-F2 (Body's stepper, 8 octants) | The slowest walk-out: a diagonal octant |
| `INPUT_LATENCY_TICKS` | int ticks | 0..3 fit the current `UNFOLD_LO`; [PH] 0, **unmeasured** | Horror (a spike row, AC-HE-131) | Input-to-motion and display latency (controller poll, render, display). The 3 ticks of slack at `UNFOLD_LO` 95 are its allowance (50 ms); a measured value of 4 or more raises the bound to 100 and `UNFOLD_LO` must move with it (re-derive HE-F13, HE-F15, HE-F16, AC-HE-12, AC-HE-88, AC-HE-91, AC-HE-92, Game Feel; Open Question 17) *(new 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `UNFOLD_SCALE_PCT` | int percent | 100..200, default 100 | Horror (accessibility setting, persisted in `horror.state`) | Lengthens each drawn `UNFOLD_TICKS` after the draw (`ceil_div`, then × 5), never shortens, so the bound above still holds at every value; it is a world option that takes effect at the next REACH begin and shows nothing in the world (AT-24: no caption, no indicator); replays and saves carry it (HE-41); it changes only through the host command `horror_set_unfold_scale(pct)` *(name provisional)*, a command with `HOST` origin that is hashed in the replay log like any command, applied at its `apply_tick`, range-checked 100..200 and refused otherwise (a lint refuses any other writer), so the field is never written outside the command stream *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; the F13 and F16 figures are stated at scale 100 *(new 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `V_WALK` | int px/tick | 2 (CITED) | Player Controller, registry | Escaping speed |
| `REACT_ALLOW_TICKS_HORROR` | int ticks | 30..90, [PH] 54 | Horror (**unsourced**, C-HE-27; never Attention's `REACT_ALLOW_TICKS` 180) | Reaction allowance: the ticks from the limb entering the cone to the walk command; validated by the blind-legibility playtest |
| `UNFOLD_LO`, `UNFOLD_HI` ★ | int ticks | `UNFOLD_LO` 95, `UNFOLD_HI` 180, [PH]; both multiples of 5 | Horror | Telegraph length range |
| `counter` | int | ≥ 0 | block (`"horror.tell"` counter) | Incremented in the same block write as the draw; persisted and restored exactly; a restore never rerolls a REACH in flight (only the stream key, through `restore_epoch` inside `rng_derive_epoch`, differs after a restore, HE-42) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R1)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: kernel name `rng_derive_epoch`)* |

**Output Range:** `UNFOLD_TICKS` takes 18 equally likely values, 95, 100, …, 180, mean 137.5 ticks (2.29 s). Bound at the placeholders: `t_out_max(V_WALK) 37 + 54 + 0 + 1 = 92`, rounded up to 95; **3 ticks of unclaimed slack** (4 on the cardinal octants, where `36 + 54 + 1 = 91`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier 4 ticks used the cardinal 36; Body's octant stepper makes a diagonal walk-out 37)*. The 3 ticks (50 ms) are the whole allowance for `INPUT_LATENCY_TICKS`; the term is unmeasured, so Open Question 17 and AC-HE-131 carry the measurement. The carrier line is informative: `t_out_max(V_CARRY) 73 + 54 + 1 = 128` (127 on a cardinal octant) against 95 leaves 40 ticks available for 72 (73 diagonal) needed; rounded to the draw grid it is 130, so a carrier who does not drop the cargo is clear on the 11 draws 130..180 and **is struck on the other 7 of 18 draws (38.9 %)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the figure is now explicit)*.

**Example:** counter 7 draws `rng_int(19, 36) = 22`, so `UNFOLD_TICKS = 110` and `reach_end_T = reach_start_T + 110`. A walker who reacts in 54 ticks is clear with at least 3 ticks to spare (4 on a cardinal octant) at every legal draw. A carrier who does not drop the cargo can walk out only when `UNFOLD_TICKS ≥ 130` (`128` rounded up to the next draw value), which is 11 of the 18 draws (61.1 %), so it is struck on 7 of 18 (38.9 %); a carrier who drops the cargo inside the reaction budget becomes a walker.
