Interaction's `drop` (IA-28) is a TAP verb, exempt from the lit rule, allowed in CARRY_IDLE and CARRY_WALK, with no channel to interrupt; its only rejects are `INVALID_TARGET` and an immediate `OBSTRUCTED` when no free cell exists among the nine candidate cells, so the guarantee is "instant and uninterruptible except when there is nowhere to put the cargo" (Open Question 7; cross-GDD note to Interaction on the foot-origin fallback). Timeline for the drop counterplay: drop and walk issued at tick 54, first step in tick 55, the cargo-free walker is on the disc boundary after the integrate of tick 91 (diagonal; 90 cardinal), clear at every legal draw if the Controller restores the walk gait on the tick `clear_carry_ref` lands (a one-tick lag gives tick 92, still inside 95); AC-HE-132 asserts it *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: recomputed under the tick-indexing convention above, G4)*. C-HE-8 states the carrier case as intended, and the user ruled it so on 2026-10-27.

**The ruled lever, `R_SNAP_PX` 48 (derivation; Open Question 7).** *Constraint:* keep the price legible and bounded, and keep the chain `R_SNAP_PX < REACH_RANGE_PX < LURK_STANDOFF_PX` (HE-F14) and the knob range 48..96. The disc is strict, so a walker at `V_WALK` 2 needs `R_SNAP_PX / 2` ticks to clear it and a carrier at `V_CARRY` 1 needs `R_SNAP_PX` ticks (one more on a diagonal octant). A carrier who does not drop the cargo is clear when `UNFOLD_TICKS ≥ R_SNAP_PX + REACT_ALLOW_TICKS_HORROR + 1`, rounded up to the 5-tick draw grid. *Worked example at the default 72:* `72 + 54 + 1 = 127`, rounded up 130; struck draws are 95..125, which is 7 of 18 (38.9 %), the confirmed price. *At the lever 48:* `48 + 54 + 1 = 103` (diagonal `49 + 54 + 1 = 104`), rounded up 105; struck draws are 95 and 100, which is 2 of 18 (11.1 %). The walker bound falls to `24 + 54 + 1 = 79` (about 80 on the diagonal octant; Body's stepper decides at the spike), so `UNFOLD_LO` 95 would carry 15 ticks of slack; this ruling does **not** lower `UNFOLD_LO` (that would re-derive HE-F13, HE-F15 and HE-F16). Moving to 48 also ends the deliberate equality with Interaction's `REACH_PX` 72 (one disc size teaches both reaches). The ruling therefore confirms the price at 72 and fixes 48 as the lever; the default is not changed. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

**Registry candidate:** `strike_walkout_bound`.

### HE-F6 Speed cadence and the escape invariant

The speed cadence and escape invariant formula is defined as:

`acc ← acc + V_MPX;  v_eff = floor(acc / 1000);  acc ← acc − 1000 × v_eff   (once per tick of a run);   V_CREEP_MPX < V_HUNT_MPX < V_WALK × 1000;   ceil(V_MPX / 1000) ≤ 3`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `V_MPX` | int milli-px/tick | `V_CREEP_MPX` or `V_HUNT_MPX` | Horror | Speed of the current state: LURK, STALK in a TELL, RETREAT and the arrival walk use `V_CREEP_MPX`; STALK in BREACH uses `V_HUNT_MPX` |
| `V_CREEP_MPX` ★ | int | 1000..1400, [PH] 1000 | Horror | Below 1000 a tick can have `v_eff` 0, and Body resets its sub-pixel residue on a zero-speed tick (BDY-12), so the floor stays 1000 until that is verified |
| `V_HUNT_MPX` ★ | int | `V_CREEP_MPX`+1..1999, [PH] 1500 | Horror | Approach speed in BREACH |
| `V_WALK` | int px/tick | 2 (CITED) | Player Controller | A walking seat, including a CRITICAL walk-only seat |
| `acc` | int | 0..999 | block, per record | Persisted remainder |
| `v_eff` | int px/tick | 1..3 | this formula | The per-tick step passed to Body's `LocomotionStep` with the run's octant |

**Output Range:** `v_eff` in 1..2 at the placeholders; the sweep box of Body's step is sized for 3 (BDY-15). The invariant means a walker gains `V_WALK × 1000 − V_HUNT_MPX = 500` milli-px per tick on a hunter. A **carrier** (`V_CARRY` 1, 1000 milli-px) cannot out-walk `V_HUNT_MPX` (HE-24, C-HE-8).

**Example:** `V_HUNT_MPX` 1500, `acc = 0`: run one steps 1, 2, 1, 2, 1 = 7 px (`acc` 500); run two steps 2, 1, 2, 1, 2 = 8 px; mean 7.5 px per run, 1.5 px/tick, 90 px/s. `V_CREEP_MPX` 1000: always 1, 5 px per run, `acc` stays 0. A walker at 2 px/tick gains 0.5 px/tick on the hunter: 30 px/s.

**Registry candidate:** `horror_speed_invariant`.

### HE-F7 Mark ring eviction, lifetime and ageing

The mark ring eviction, lifetime and ageing formula is defined as:

`insert of kind K at its quota evicts argmin(mark_id) over kind = K;   insert of a SMEAR into a full ring evicts argmin(mark_id) over kind = SMEAR;   n_other_max = MARK_QUOTA_RIME + MARK_QUOTA_HAIRLINES + MARK_QUOTA_STRUCK;   life_left ← life_left − HORROR_RUN_TICKS per run while the mark's section is loaded, removed at life_left ≤ 0;   stage = min(2, floor(3 × (MARK_LIFE_TICKS − life_left) / MARK_LIFE_TICKS));   smear_retention_ticks = (MAX_MARKS − n_other_max) × MARK_SPACING_PX × 1000 / V_CREEP_MPX;   MARK_LIFE_TICKS ≥ smear_retention_ticks ≥ TRAIL_TARGET_TICKS;   MAX_MARKS − n_other_max ≥ ceil(TRAIL_TARGET_TICKS × V_CREEP_MPX / (MARK_SPACING_PX × 1000))` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `mark_id` | int | ≥ 1, never reused | block counter | Insertion order |
| `kind` | enum | SMEAR, RIME, HAIRLINES, STRUCK | block | Mark kind (HE-27) |
| `MAX_MARKS` | int | `n_other_max + 19`..48 (32..48 at the placeholders), [PH] 32 | Horror | Ring size. Raised from 24 to 32 in the design review (at 24 the smear history was `(24 − 9) × 96 = 1440` ticks, below the 1800 target); the range's floor now follows the quotas, so the range no longer contradicts its own relation *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; a registry request |
| `MARK_LIFE_TICKS` | int ticks | `smear_retention_ticks`..54000 (1824 is the derived floor at the placeholders; 3600 is the lowest authored value), [PH] 18000 | Horror | Lifetime of a mark in a loaded section; the lower bound is asserted at load *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| `MARK_SPACING_PX` | int px | 48..144, [PH] 96 | Horror | One SMEAR per this much travel (two tiles); plus one touch-down SMEAR per REACH start |
| `CHILL_SPACING_PX` | int px | 192..768, [PH] 384 | Horror | One CHILL deposit and rime mark per this much travel, and at each LURK dwell start |
| `n_other_max` | int | DERIVED 13 | the three quotas | `MARK_QUOTA_RIME` 7 + `MARK_QUOTA_HAIRLINES` 3 + `MARK_QUOTA_STRUCK` 3. The rime quota is `ceil(MAX_MARKS / (CHILL_SPACING_PX / MARK_SPACING_PX + 1))` = `ceil(32 / 5)` = 7 (one rime per five ring slots of creep); the other two follow the three-strike ladder. Asserted at data load as arithmetic (`n_other_max + 19 <= MAX_MARKS`), never as a runtime-derived fixture *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| `MARK_QUOTA_RIME` / `MARK_QUOTA_HAIRLINES` / `MARK_QUOTA_STRUCK` | int | 1..12 / 1..6 / 1..6, [PH] 7 / 3 / 3, and `sum + 19 <= MAX_MARKS` | Horror | Per-kind ring quotas (HE-30); an insert at quota evicts the oldest of its own kind *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| `TRAIL_TARGET_TICKS` | int ticks | 1800..3600, [PH] 1800 | game-designer target (the hidden-entity investigation window), not a runtime value | The trail an investigator arriving up to 30 s late should still find |
| `V_CREEP_MPX` | int | 1000 | HE-F6 | Creep speed |
| `MARKS_PER_RUN_MAX` | int | 1..4, [PH] 2 | Horror | Inserts per run; over the cap, priority STRUCK, then HAIRLINES, then RIME, then SMEAR; a dropped insert is counted `horror_marks_withheld` |

**Output Range:** `stage` in {0, 1, 2}: exactly three frame stages for smear and rime (and two for hairlines), so physical age is readable without a meter (Pillar 1) and no continuous value is drawn. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* `smear_retention_ticks` is now a **guaranteed minimum**, because the quotas fix `n_other_max` at 13: at the placeholders `(32 − 13) × 96 × 1000 / 1000 = 1824` ticks at creep speed, at or above `TRAIL_TARGET_TICKS` 1800 whatever the history (an 18000-tick CALM LURK fills the rime quota and no more; a 3600-tick breach fills the HAIRLINES and STRUCK quotas and no more, the oldest of a kind being replaced); more smear history, up to 2112 ticks with only ten others resident, is available but not guaranteed. At `V_HUNT_MPX` the same ring holds `19 × 96 × 1000 / 1500 = 1216` ticks, below the target: the BREACH approach trail is short-lived by construction (the horror covers distance faster than a smear can age), the hidden-entity investigation window is defined on the creep trail, and the shortfall is accepted and logged (Open Question 20). The touch-down SMEAR of each REACH is a SMEAR and competes with the travel smears, but REACHes occur only in BREACH, where that shortfall is already accepted. The ring, not the lifetime, is the binding bound on a creeping horror; the lifetime exists so a frozen loaded body does not keep stale marks forever.

**Example:** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* the ring is full at 32 with 3 hairline marks, 3 STRUCK marks, 7 rime marks and 19 smears. A new smear evicts the oldest smear and never a hairline; a fourth HAIRLINES mark evicts the oldest HAIRLINES mark and nothing else; an eighth rime evicts the oldest rime. There is always a smear to evict because `32 > 13`. The relation `MAX_MARKS − n_other_max >= ceil(TRAIL_TARGET_TICKS × V_CREEP_MPX / (MARK_SPACING_PX × 1000))` = 19 is an arithmetic assertion the data load makes: `13 + 19 = 32` passes, and a quota set summing to 14 with `MAX_MARKS` 32 fails the load. Ageing: a mark with `life_left` 12000 has age 6000 and `stage = floor(3 × 6000 / 18000) = 1`; at `life_left` 5999 the age is 12001 and the stage is 2.

**Registry candidate:** `mark_ring_retention`.

### HE-F8 Target selection from `lux_pm`

The target selection formula is defined as:

`candidates = { s : living(s) ∧ ¬DOWNED(s) ∧ ¬IN_POD(s) ∧ actor_section(s) ∈ body_scope ∧ lit(s) };   target = argmax over candidates of (lux_pm(s), −seat_id);   candidates = ∅ ⇒ target = ANCHOR (HE-16 (3))`

`body_scope` is `locus_body` in TELL, BREACH and SUBSIDING and the record's own body in CALM. Nothing is stored between runs.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `lux_pm(s)` | int permille | 0..1000 | Emitter `lux(ref)` | The seat's illumination; the owner stands in their own light (EM-21), so a seat with its lamp on reads 1000 |
| `lit(s)` | bool | — | derived here: `lux_pm(s) >= LIT_ON_PM` (150, CITED) | Stateless, recomputed every run, nothing stored. **Not the Emitter's `lit` bit** (a hysteresis on `act_lux_pm`, which excludes the owner's own beam and the BEACON; a lone lamp-on player reads `act_lux_pm` 0) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `seat_id` | int | ≥ 0 | Body | Tie-break: lowest wins |
| `ANCHOR` | position | Layout `anchor_geometry` | HE-16 (3) | The target when no candidate is lit |

**Output Range:** a seat id or ANCHOR; one `lux(ref)` read per candidate per run, zero probes (HE-43).

**Example:** seat A `lux_pm` 1000 (lamp on), seat B 0 (dark): target A. Seat A switches the lamp off: no lit candidate, target ANCHOR on the next run, and nothing remembers A (the target is not persisted). A thrown Chemlight pool reads 700 only for a seat that stands in it; the horror targets whichever *seat* is lit, never the pool (a pool is not a seat, HE-13). Two lit seats at 1000 and 1000: the lower `seat_id` wins; a stateless argmax can alternate as lamps change (C-HE-19, Vertical Slice). *Further cases (design review):* a seat with its lamp off under a lit fixture reading 400 is lit (`400 >= 150`) and is the target while a dark seat is not; a seat with its lamp off whose only light is the BEACON's sweep is lit only on the runs where `lux_pm >= 150` (it counts in `lux_pm`, not in `act_lux_pm`) and the next run's target may differ, but a REACH already begun keeps its locked P; a seat with its lamp on standing in a 400 fixture pool and a seat with its lamp off in a 700 Chemlight pool: the lamp-on seat reads 1000 and wins, so the argmax is the lamp, not the amount *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.

**Registry candidate:** `horror_target_rule`.

### HE-F9 Ingress timing and entry choice

The ingress timing and entry choice formula is defined as:

`transit_until_T = tell_enter_T + INGRESS_DELAY_TICKS;   fallback_T = transit_until_T + STALK_VISIBLE_FLOOR_TICKS;   arrive_T = min{ T ≥ transit_until_T : T mod 5 = 0 ∧ ( legal_entry(T) ∨ (T ≥ fallback_T + INGRESS_MARK_LEAD_TICKS ∧ fallback_entry(T)) ) };   stalk_visible_min = TELL_MIN_TICKS − INGRESS_DELAY_TICKS;   D_entry_max = LURK_STANDOFF_PX + floor(stalk_visible_min × V_CREEP_MPX / 1000);   INGRESS_DELAY_TICKS + STALK_VISIBLE_FLOOR_TICKS + INGRESS_MARK_LEAD_TICKS ≤ TELL_MIN_TICKS` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*

`legal_entry(T)` selects, among the hull entries of `locus_body` at room-hop distance 1 from the room of the highest-`lux_pm` living seat (none lit or present: the locus section's outermost compartment), a hull segment whose room holds no living seat and which lies at least `LURK_STANDOFF_PX` from every living seat **that is `linked` to it (HE-19)**; ties lowest hop, then lowest room id, then lowest hull-segment id. A *hull entry* is an `anchor_geometry` point of a hull-bearing compartment that lies within `HULL_CONTACT_PX` of a hull face, paired with the lowest id returned by `hull_segments_near(section_id, anchor_px, HULL_CONTACT_PX)`; there is no `solid_in` sampling and no unpublished room-to-segment read. The entry is chosen at the run at `transit_until_T − INGRESS_MARK_LEAD_TICKS` and the ingress mark is laid then (re-chosen under E-HE-34 if it becomes illegal); the body arrives at `transit_until_T`, or at the first later run whose re-chosen entry has had its full mark lead. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **Fallback.** `fallback_entry(T)` is the *nearest legal hull anchor*: if no legal entry exists at `fallback_T` (540 ticks after TELL entry at the placeholders, inside `TELL_MIN_TICKS`), the hop-1 and standoff conditions drop out and the entry is the hull entry nearest by room hops to the lit seat's room whose room holds no living seat (ties as above); its mark is laid at `fallback_T` and the body arrives `INGRESS_MARK_LEAD_TICKS` later, at the first Horror run at or after `TELL_MIN_TICKS` (at most 4 ticks later for an unaligned `tell_enter_T`, E-HE-17) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. Only when every hull room holds a living seat does the record stay in TRANSIT (`horror_ingress_pending`, E-HE-33). **Precondition of `stalk_visible_min`:** the visible-stalk bound below holds only when a legal entry exists at `INGRESS_DELAY_TICKS`; under the fallback the told breach still has a horror and an ingress mark from `fallback_T`, but the visible stalk can be as short as the mark lead before BREACH entry at the shortest tell.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `INGRESS_DELAY_TICKS` ★ | int ticks | 120..360, [PH] 300 | Horror | Earliest ingress after TELL entry |
| `TELL_MIN_TICKS` | int ticks | 600 (CITED, DERIVED from `W_MIN_WARNING_S`) | Attention, registry | Shortest tell |
| `STALK_VISIBLE_FLOOR_TICKS` | int ticks | 120..360, [PH] 240 | Horror | The least visible stalk the design accepts |
| `INGRESS_MARK_LEAD_TICKS` | int ticks | 30..120, [PH] 60 | Horror | The mark precedes the body so a player watching that wall sees the arrival coming |
| `LURK_STANDOFF_PX` | int px | 144..288, [PH] 192 | Horror | HE-F14 |
| `V_CREEP_MPX` | int milli-px/tick | 1000..1400, [PH] 1000 | Horror | HE-F6 |
| `D_entry_max` | int px | derived 492 | this formula | The farthest an entry may lie from the seat for the stalk to close to the standoff before the shortest tell ends |

**Output Range:** `stalk_visible_min = 300` ticks (5 s): the entity's presence is published for at least 300 ticks before the shortest tell ends (up to 4 ticks later for run alignment); whether the player *sees* it depends on light. An entry beyond `D_entry_max` still arrives inside the tell but reaches the standoff after BREACH entry (the approach then runs at `V_HUNT_MPX`); it is a tuning and authoring signal, never a rule violation. The relation in the formula's last clause is the dependent bound for the three knobs (their ranges are not independent): 300 + 240 + 60 = 600 at the placeholders *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.

**Example:** TELL enters at `T = 1200` with a drawn length of 900. `transit_until_T = 1500`; the ingress mark is laid at 1440 and the body registers (E6) at the run at 1500; BREACH begins at 2100, so the stalk is published for 600 ticks. Relation check: `300 + 240 + 60 = 600 ≤ 600` (pass, with no slack at the placeholders) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; `D_entry_max = 192 + 300 = 492` px, covering a one-hop entry in the 288 to 480 px room sizes of the slice. If a seat walks into the chosen room during the 60 ticks of mark lead, the entry is re-chosen by the same rule; the first hairline stays (it is a true mark of where the web touched).

**Registry candidate:** `horror_ingress_timing`.

### HE-F10 Path expansion bound

The path expansion bound formula is defined as:

`expansions_per_run ≤ PATH_EXPANSIONS_MAX;   PATH_EXPANSIONS_MAX ≥ |rooms(body)|_max;   cache hit ⇒ 0 expansions;   cache key = (goal_room, layout_hash, version(section_id) for every section of the body)`  *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: Layout's `version(section_id)` replaces the Horror-computed closed-leaf hash)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `PATH_EXPANSIONS_MAX` | int | 16..256, [PH] 64 | Horror | Node expansions per run on a cache miss |
| `|rooms(body)|_max` | int | 14 in the slice (5 + 4 + 5) | Layout | The largest welded body the slice can form |
| `layout_hash` | hash | — | Layout | Changes on any `layout.state` change (weld, cut) |
| `version(section_id)` | u32 | — | Layout (`OccluderSource.version`, LY-12 / LY-33) | Monotone per section, bumped when any opaque rectangle (a door leaf included) changes; reset to 1 on restore, which is treated as an unconditional rebuild. Replaces the retired Horror-computed `closed_leaf_hash`; `layout_hash()` does not cover door state (C-HE-13 closed) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

**Output Range:** 0 on a cache hit; at most 14 on a miss in the slice (breadth-first, ties lowest room id). `PATH_EXPANSIONS_MAX` below the room count truncates long routes; the horror then holds its waypoint up to `HOLD_MAX_RUNS` and then falls back by phase (LURK in CALM and TELL, STALK holding position in BREACH, RETREAT in SUBSIDING; HE-25) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.

**Example:** a goal on a 14-room body expands at most 14 nodes with at most 6 neighbours each, 84 edge relaxations, well under 64 expansions. A weld invalidates the cache for the welded body only; a door closing bumps `version(section_id)`, so the next run recomputes once.

**Registry candidate:** `path_expansion_bound`.

### HE-F11 L3 disturbance strength and duration bound

The L3 disturbance strength and duration formula is defined as:

`S_hi = 1000 − MOD_FLOOR_PM;   strength_pm = L3_STRENGTH_LO_PM + (h2 mod (S_hi − L3_STRENGTH_LO_PM + 1));   dur_ticks = L3_DUR_LO + (h3 mod (L3_DUR_HI − L3_DUR_LO + 1));   start iff h1 mod 1000 < L3_START_PM ∧ T ≥ last_end_T + L3_MIN_GAP_TICKS;   L3_STRENGTH_LO_PM ≤ S_hi`

`h1`, `h2`, `h3` are `he_hash(emitter_id, run_n, 0, SALT_L3_START)`, `…SALT_L3_STRENGTH)` and `…SALT_L3_DUR)` (HE-42: non-negative, golden vectors in AC-HE-139), never `rng_derive`. The result enters Lighting's registered `modifier_stack` as the L3 factor, whose floor `MOD_FLOOR_PM` already clamps the chain; Horror never restates or applies that clamp. **Sign convention** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: `strength_pm` is a dip **depth**, like Lighting's `DIP_DEPTH_PM` (`M1 = 1000 − DIP_DEPTH_PM` during a dip, LO D.15); Lighting's L3 factor is `M3 = 1000 − strength_pm` (1000 = no dip). **Worked LO-18 example:** `strength_pm` 374 gives `M3 = 626`; with a concurrent gutter `M1 = 700`, an idle tell `M2 = 1000` and `MOD_FLOOR_PM` 600, `m_mod = clamp(floor(700 × 1000 × 626 / 10^6), 600, 1000) = clamp(438, 600, 1000) = 600` (the floor binds); with `M1 = 1000` the same dip is `clamp(626, 600, 1000) = 626`. The reduced-motion cap is stated in Tuning Knobs (`L3_STRENGTH_RM_CAP_PM`).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MOD_FLOOR_PM` | int permille | 600, range 400..800 [PH] (CITED) | 2D Lighting & Occlusion, registry | The deepest any modifier chain may dim a lamp |
| `S_hi` | int permille | DERIVED (400 at the placeholder; the formula is cited, never the bare number) | this formula | Strength ceiling |
| `L3_STRENGTH_LO_PM` ★ | int permille | 50..`S_hi`, [PH] 150 | Horror | Shallowest dip (a depth: `M3 = 1000 − strength_pm`) |
| `L3_STRENGTH_RM_CAP_PM` | int permille | `L3_STRENGTH_LO_PM`..`S_hi`, [PH] 150 | Horror (a presentation-side read cap, not hashed, not in `horror.state`) | The reduced-motion ceiling: Lighting reads `min(strength_pm, L3_STRENGTH_RM_CAP_PM)` when reduced motion is on, so `M3 >= 850`; at the default it equals `L3_STRENGTH_LO_PM`, so every dip is the shallowest one *(new 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `L3_DUR_LO` / `L3_DUR_HI` ★ | int ticks | [PH] 18 / 30 | Horror (Lighting's gutter grammar, LO-19, 0.3 to 0.5 s) | Dip length |
| `L3_START_PM` | int permille | 10..200, [PH] 40 | Horror | Per eligible lamp per run start probability |
| `L3_MIN_GAP_TICKS` ★ | int ticks | ≥ 90 (raised from 60 by the flash-rate bound), [PH] 120 | Horror (ruled as `L3_MIN_GAP_FRAMES`, renamed because a SIM block cannot count frames, H9) | Minimum quiet time per lamp *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| `L3_MAX_ACTIVE` / `L3_MAX_TRACKED` | int | 1..4 (capped at 4 by the flash-rate bound) / 4..32, [PH] 4 / 16 | Horror | Simultaneous dips per horror; size of the `last_end_T` map *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |

**Output Range:** `strength_pm` in `[L3_STRENGTH_LO_PM, S_hi]`; `dur_ticks` in `[18, 30]`; duty per lamp at most `30 / (30 + 120)` = 20 %. Never below `MOD_FLOOR_PM` after the chain. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **Flash-rate bound (photosensitivity):** a dip start is a flash, a lamp starts at most once per `L3_DUR_LO + L3_MIN_GAP_TICKS` ticks and at most `L3_MAX_ACTIVE` lamps are active per horror, so the combined start rate is at most `L3_MAX_ACTIVE × TICK_RATE / (L3_DUR_LO + L3_MIN_GAP_TICKS) × HORROR_MAX_MVP`, asserted at load to be at most 3 per second: `4 × 60 / (18 + 120)` = 1.74 per second at the placeholders, 2.2 per second at the corner (`L3_MAX_ACTIVE` 4, `L3_MIN_GAP_TICKS` 90), and the corner `L3_MAX_ACTIVE` 8 with `L3_MIN_GAP_TICKS` 60 (6.2 per second) is outside the ranges.

**Example:** at the 600 placeholder `S_hi = 400`, so `strength_pm = 150 + (h2 mod 251)`; a hash of 977 gives `977 mod 251 = 224` and `strength_pm = 374`; `dur_ticks = 18 + (h3 mod 13)`. Expected cycle per lamp near the horror: duration about 24, minimum gap 120, expected wait `1000 / 40 = 25` runs (125 ticks), about 269 ticks (4.5 s), duty about 9 %. The EMERGENCY subkind (`fx_cryo_lamp`), the DOCK_STATUS subkind (the pod status glow), any BEAM, BEACON, FAULT_GLOW and any DEPLOYABLE never start a disturbance *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*.

**Registry candidate:** `l3_strength_bound`.

### HE-F12 Subject count

The subject count formula is defined as:

`S_h = count(records not in TRANSIT);   S_h ≤ HORROR_SUBJECTS_MAX;   HORROR_SUBJECTS_MAX + A_max ≤ MAX_MOVING_SUBJECTS;   E6 returns true for every first registration`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `S_h` | int | 0..2 | block | Registered Horror subjects |
| `HORROR_SUBJECTS_MAX` ★ | int | 1..12, [PH] 2 | Horror (Interaction's `H` and Derelict Authoring's `HORRORS` quote this, C-HE-4 closed 2026-10-27) | One active plus one transient during ingress |
| `A_max` | int | 4 | Body (`MAX_ACTORS` 8 headroom; seats at MVP) | Actors counted as moving subjects |
| `MAX_MOVING_SUBJECTS` | int | 16 (CITED) | Emitter, registry | Moving-subject ceiling |

**Output Range:** `S_h` in 0..2; a false return from E6 on a first registration is a **budget defect** (a duplicate E6 on an already-registered ref returns false by design, EM:152, and is not) (the subject reads dark) and a failing criterion, never a gameplay state.

**Example:** at ingress the old record is unregistered (E7) before the new arrival registers (E6), so `S_h` stays 1; the transient 2 is a ceiling the order never reaches at MVP. `2 + 4 = 6 ≤ 16`.

**Registry candidate:** `horror_subject_count`.

### HE-F13 Tell composition timeline

The tell composition timeline formula is defined as:

`t_arrive = ceil5( (LURK_STANDOFF_PX − REACH_RANGE_PX) × 1000 / V_HUNT_MPX );   first_strike_T = tell_len + t_arrive + UNFOLD_TICKS;   layered_warning_min = TELL_MIN_TICKS + t_arrive + UNFOLD_LO ≥ W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE`

`T` is counted from TELL entry. `tell_len` is Attention's draw in `[TELL_MIN_TICKS, TELL_MAX_TICKS]`; the audit for any harm event is the registered `tell_window_contract` (HE-8), not this timeline.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `tell_len` | int ticks | 600..1800 (CITED) | Attention, registry (`TELL_MIN_TICKS`, `TELL_MAX_TICKS`) | Length of the tell; Horror never shortens it |
| `t_arrive` | int ticks | **0..70** (65 at the pinned fixture: 192 px, same room, cardinal axis, no slide) | this formula | BREACH entry to the first REACH start. 0 when the horror is already within `REACH_RANGE_PX` of the seat at BREACH entry (an adjacent seat); 65 at the standoff on a cardinal axis; up to 70 (`ceil5(96 × 1000 / (1500 × cos 22.5°))`, the octant-steering path of at most 103.9 px) when the approach is off-octant. Never negative: the chain `REACH_RANGE_PX < LURK_STANDOFF_PX` (HE-F14) is asserted at load *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| `W_MIN_WARNING_S` | int s | 10 (CITED) | Life-Support Graph, registry | The Graph's minimum warning |
| `TICK_RATE` | int Hz | 60 (CITED) | Kernel, registry | Tick rate |
| `UNFOLD_TICKS` | int ticks | 95..180 | HE-F5 | The first strike's telegraph |
| `LURK_STANDOFF_PX` | int px | 144..288, [PH] 192 | Horror (HE-F14) | The standoff the horror holds before the BREACH approach |
| `REACH_RANGE_PX` | int px | 73..159, [PH] 96 | Horror (HE-F14) | Distance at which a REACH may begin |
| `V_HUNT_MPX` | int milli-px/tick | `V_CREEP_MPX`+1..1999, [PH] 1500 | Horror (HE-F6) | Approach speed in BREACH |

**Output Range:** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* `t_arrive` is a range (0 to 70), not a constant. The first strike lands no earlier than `600 + 0 + 95 = 695` ticks and no later than `1800 + 70 + 180 = 2050` ticks after TELL entry for run-aligned edges (up to `HORROR_RUN_TICKS − 1` = 4 further ticks of run alignment for an arbitrary TELL entry, E-HE-17, so 2054). **760 and 2045 are the bounds for the pinned fixture only** (`t_arrive` 65: `600 + 65 + 95` and `1800 + 65 + 180`), which AC-HE-12 uses; AC-HE-144 gives the adjacent-seat and off-octant fixtures their own bounds. `layered_warning_min = TELL_MIN_TICKS + t_arrive + UNFOLD_LO` is 695 at `t_arrive` 0, still at or above `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE` = 600 (760 at the pinned fixture). Attention's L0 alone already meets the Graph's requirement (`TELL_MIN_TICKS` 600 = `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE`); L1 and L2 are additive. Figures are stated at `UNFOLD_SCALE_PCT` 100.

**Example:** `tell_len = 900`, the horror resident in another body. `t = 0`: TELL begins; the record goes TRANSIT (egress mark at the old site). `t = 240`: ingress mark on the hull seam. `t = 300`: the body registers and STALKs at `V_CREEP_MPX`, held at 192 px. `t = 900`: BREACH, `V_HUNT_MPX`. `t = 965`: first REACH starts, the limb enters the cone. With `UNFOLD_TICKS` 110 (HE-F5 example), `t = 1075`: the snap, `I_1 = 6800`. The player has had 900 ticks of L0, 600 ticks of L1 and 110 ticks of L2 before the first strike. A suit already at `SUIT_HP_OK` (7000) dies at the second strike, no earlier than `STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS + UNFOLD_LO` = 395 ticks after the first (HE-F15).

**Registry candidate:** `horror_tell_composition`.

### HE-F14 REACH range ordering chain

The REACH range ordering formula is defined as:

`R_SNAP_PX < REACH_RANGE_PX < LURK_STANDOFF_PX;   REACH_RANGE_PX ≤ 3 × H_crew`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `R_SNAP_PX` | int px | 72 | HE-F2 | Strike disc radius |
| `REACH_RANGE_PX` ★ | int px | `R_SNAP_PX + 1` .. `min(LURK_STANDOFF_PX − 1, 3 × H_crew)` (73..159 at the placeholders), [PH] 96 | Horror | Distance from the horror's foot point to P at which a REACH may begin; also the limb length used by the rig. The three ranges are not independent: the chain is asserted at load for every combination, so the corner `R_SNAP_PX` 96 with `REACH_RANGE_PX` 73 or `LURK_STANDOFF_PX` 144 is rejected *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| `LURK_STANDOFF_PX` ★ | int px | 144..288, [PH] 192 | Horror | The nearest the horror comes to a living seat outside a BREACH approach |
| `H_crew` | int px | 53 | Art bible (ART:1028, 1042: crewmate figure 53 px; horror height band 1× to 3× = 53 to 159 px) | Crewmate height |

**Output Range:** `REACH_RANGE_PX` in 73..159 (the art cap binds below the standoff). The first draft's relation `REACH_RANGE_PX + core_radius ≤ field_h_min / 2` fails once the camera's look-ahead `L_MAX × s` and follow lag (PIPE:157) are counted, so only the tip at the seat's foot is guaranteed on screen at `k_auto + 2` (CP-3); body and limb visibility at `k = 1`, `k_auto` and `k_auto + 2` is a manual screenshot criterion, not a derived bound.

**Example:** `72 < 96 < 192` (pass) and `96 ≤ 3 × 53 = 159` (pass). `REACH_RANGE_PX` 144 would also satisfy both, but leaves only 48 px between the REACH start and the standoff and was withdrawn (C-HE-23).

**Registry candidate:** `reach_range_chain`.

### HE-F15 Cycle period of a seat that does not move

The cycle period formula is defined as:

`P = UNFOLD_TICKS + STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS + t_back;   t_back = ceil5( (D_wp − REACH_RANGE_PX) × 1000 / V_HUNT_MPX );   t_out = ceil( (D_wp − REACH_RANGE_PX) × 1000 / V_CREEP_MPX ) ≤ STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS;   STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS > STAGGER_TICKS;   min_gap = STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS + UNFOLD_LO`

After a strike the horror RETREATs to the nearest waypoint at least `LURK_STANDOFF_PX` from every living seat (HE-17); at `cooldown_until_T` it turns to STALK and walks back at `V_HUNT_MPX` until it is within `REACH_RANGE_PX`; the next REACH may begin only then (HE-14 (e) and (d)).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS` ★ | int ticks | 180..900 and `> STAGGER_TICKS`, [PH] 300 | Horror; `STAGGER_TICKS` 30 (CITED, Player Controller) | The pause after a strike; also the floor under a RUPTURE's preceding scrape (L4) |
| `UNFOLD_TICKS` | int ticks | 95..180 | HE-F5 | The telegraph that opens each cycle |
| `REACH_RANGE_PX` | int px | 73..159, [PH] 96 | Horror (HE-F14) | Distance at which the next REACH may begin |
| `V_HUNT_MPX` | int milli-px/tick | `V_CREEP_MPX`+1..1999, [PH] 1500 | Horror (HE-F6) | The return speed in `t_back` |
| `V_CREEP_MPX` | int milli-px/tick | 1000..1400, [PH] 1000 | Horror (HE-F6) | The retreat speed in `t_out` |
| `D_wp` | int px | ≥ `LURK_STANDOFF_PX`, nominal 192 | Layout `anchor_geometry` | Distance from the seat to the retreat waypoint |
| `t_back` | int ticks | 65 at the nominal waypoint | this formula | Return trip, rounded up to the run grid |
| `t_out` | int ticks | 96 at the nominal waypoint | this formula | Retreat trip; the lint asserts it finishes inside the cooldown so the retreat reads as a retreat, not a hover |
| `min_gap` | int ticks | 395 | this formula | The cooldown-gated lower bound between two strike resolutions when no return trip is needed (a REACH that fizzled leaves the cooldown unconsumed) |

**Output Range:** `P` in 460..545 ticks at the nominal waypoint (7.7 to 9.1 s), mean 502.5 (8.4 s). A retreat waypoint farther than nominal lengthens `P` by `(D_wp − 192) × 1000 / V_HUNT_MPX`; the cooldown below 180 turns the peak into a flat screech and above 600 into a lull (game-designer bounds).

**Example:** strike at `T = 4000`: `cooldown_until_T = 4300`; the horror reached its waypoint at about `T = 4096` (96 ticks out at 1 px/tick), waited, turned at 4300, was back within 96 px at 4365 (64 ticks, run grid), and the next REACH starts at 4365; with `UNFOLD_TICKS` 110 the next strike resolves at 4475, a period of 475 ticks (7.9 s). A single-seat player therefore gets a breathing gap of at least 365 ticks (6.1 s) between a snap and the next limb.

**Registry candidate:** `horror_cycle_period`.

### HE-F16 Stationary-seat lethality and the session ratchet

The stationary-seat lethality formula is defined as:

`s_1 = t_arrive;   s_{k+1} = s_k + U_k + STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS + t_back;   T_3 = t_arrive + U_1 + U_2 + U_3 + 2 × (STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS + t_back);   T_2 = t_arrive + U_1 + U_2 + (STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS + t_back);   third REACH starts inside a breach of length B iff s_3 < B;   P_3(λ) = 1 − e^{−λ} × (1 + λ + λ²/2) (fresh suit, I_0 ≥ 8900: three landed strikes kill);   P_2(λ) = 1 − e^{−λ} × (1 + λ) (worn or patched suit, I_0 < 8900: two kill);   λ = E_s × n × p` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*

The first four terms are integer simulation arithmetic; `P_death` is design analysis (real arithmetic, never hashed): the probability of at least three landed strikes (fresh suit) or two (worn or patched suit) in a session when strikes arrive as a Poisson process (no passive integrity regeneration exists, so hits accumulate until `patch_integrity`, HE-F3). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* The fresh-suit tail alone understated lethality and was only a lower bound; `k_fail(I) = 3` needs `I >= 8900` (`floor((I − 2500) / 3200) + 1`), so every patched suit (cap at most 6900) and every suit after one landed strike (6800) is in the two-strike state.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `U_k` | int ticks | 95..180 (18 values) | HE-F5 | The k-th telegraph length |
| `t_arrive`, `t_back` | int ticks | `t_arrive` 0..70 (65 at the pinned fixture, HE-F13); `t_back` 65 | HE-F13, HE-F15 | First approach and per-cycle return; the figures below use the pinned `t_arrive` 65 unless a range is named *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| `B` | int ticks | `BREACH_MIN_TICKS` 900 .. `BREACH_MAX_TICKS` 3600 (CITED) | Attention, registry | BREACH length; a REACH may begin only while the phase is BREACH and then resolves into SUBSIDING if the predicate holds (HE-17) |
| `E_s` | real | 3..8 *(provisional — Attention's breaches per session is undesigned as a rate)* | Attention | Told breaches per session with the seat in the locus body |
| `n` | real | 0..8 | HE-F15 | REACH attempts that reach the seat per breach (a stationary seat sees 7 or 8 starts in a 3600-tick breach, so 8 is the ceiling) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| `p` | real | 0..1 | playtest | Per-attempt probability that the seat is struck (inattention, late reaction, cornered, carrying); **this is the lethality dial** |
| `λ` | real | ≥ 0 | derived | Expected landed strikes per session |

**Output Range:** `T_3` in 1080..1335 ticks after BREACH entry (18.0 to 22.3 s), mean 1207.5 (20.1 s). The earliest possible third REACH start is `s_3 = 65 + 95 + 95 + 730 = 985`, so a stationary seat **with a fresh suit (`I_0 >= 8900`, three strikes needed) cannot be killed in a minimum-length breach** (`B = 900`: two starts, rung CRITICAL, stagger, a seat that then moves lives); it can in any breach of at least 986 ticks and certainly (probability 1) in any breach of at least 1156 ticks. REACH starts for a stationary seat: 2 at `B = 900`, 4 at `B = 1800`, 7 or 8 at `B = 3600`. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **The claim is conditioned on `I_0`:** with `t_arrive` 0 the earliest third REACH start is `s_3 = 0 + 95 + 95 + 730 = 920`, still after 900, and `T_3` runs 1015..1270 at `t_arrive` 0 and 1085..1340 at 70 (1080..1335 at the pinned 65); but a **worn or patched suit that stands still is killed in a minimum-length breach with probability 1**: `T_2` is 620 to 790 ticks after BREACH entry at the pinned fixture (555..725 at `t_arrive` 0), and the second REACH starts at `s_2 = t_arrive + U_1 + 365` = 525..610, always before 900, so all 324 draw pairs end in FAILED (DOWNED then the Controller's clock). The 6800 row (one landed strike already) is therefore the same state as a patched suit: two further strikes kill. *Scope note (design review 2026-10-27):* the model needs no compound term for a RUPTURE on a struck seat, because HE-16 (2) forbids a RUPTURE on a strike that hits a seat (E-HE-56); and the figures here are stated at `UNFOLD_SCALE_PCT` 100, a larger scale stretches every `U_k` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.

**Example:** `P(s_3 < B)` over the 18 × 18 equiprobable draw pairs: 0 at 900, 0.3 % at 990, 1.9 % at 1000, 28.1 % at 1050, 75.9 % at 1100, 99.7 % at 1155, 100 % at 1156. Session ratchet: `λ = 0.5` gives 1.4 %, `λ = 1.0` gives 8.0 % (the median target), `λ = 1.5` gives 19.1 %, `λ = 2.4` gives 43.0 %, `λ = 3.0` gives 57.7 %, `λ = 6.0` gives 93.8 %. With `E_s = 4` and `n = 6`, `p = 5 %` gives `λ = 1.2` and about 12 %; `p = 10 %` gives `λ = 2.4` and 43 % (fresh suit). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **Worn or patched suit** (`P_2`): `λ = 0.5` gives 9.0 %, `λ = 1.0` gives 26.4 %, `λ = 1.5` gives 44.2 %, `λ = 2.4` gives 69.2 %, `λ = 3.0` gives 80.1 %, `λ = 6.0` gives 98.3 %; `p = 5 %` (`λ = 1.2`) gives about 33.7 % where the fresh suit gives 12 %. The session ratchet is steeper than the fresh-suit table alone suggests, which is why `patch_integrity` availability (Suit's) is one of the named lethality dials. The whole "death is a bitch" dial is the per-attempt miss rate `p`, set by telegraph legibility (HE-14), not by any number in the knob table.

**Registry candidate:** `stationary_seat_lethality` (the integer part only).

### HE-F17 Hull act ladder gate

The hull act ladder formula is defined as:

`rupture_ok = (seg ∈ gouged_seg_ids) ∧ ruptures_epoch < RUPTURES_PER_EPOCH_MAX ∧ exogenous_permitted(section(seg)) ∧ hit_list = ∅;   gouge_ok = gouges_epoch < GOUGES_PER_EPOCH_MAX ∧ exogenous_permitted(section(seg));   deposit(STRIKE at P within HULL_CONTACT_PX of seg) = RUPTURE if rupture_ok, else GOUGE if gouge_ok, else none;   on a GOUGE: gouged_seg_ids ← gouged_seg_ids ∪ {seg} (after the test above);   lint: STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS < min_gap = STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS + UNFOLD_LO` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*

One hull deposit kind per STRIKE: the RUPTURE act is a later act than the GOUGE act it requires (HE-16 (2); *orchestrator ruling: HE-16 is silent on a strike where both are eligible, and one deposit per act keeps each act preceded by its own REACH*). The anchor REACH (no lit seat) may deposit one GOUGE only (`ANCHOR_REACH_GOUGES` 1), never RUPTURE. The kind is hull only (lint `GOUGE_NON_HULL`).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `gouges_epoch`, `ruptures_epoch` | int | 0..`GOUGES_PER_EPOCH_MAX`, 0..`RUPTURES_PER_EPOCH_MAX` | block (reset lazily at the first run that sees a new `tell_epoch`, transition guard 2) | Hull acts this `tell_epoch` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `GOUGES_PER_EPOCH_MAX` | int | 0..6, [PH] 3 | Horror | Hull GOUGE ceiling per epoch |
| `RUPTURES_PER_EPOCH_MAX` | int | 0..1, [PH] 1 | Horror | A RUPTURE needs a Graph-told clock afterwards (decompression, `RUPTURE_HP` 3600, registry, LSG:102) |
| `HULL_CONTACT_PX` | int px | 12..48, [PH] 24 | Horror | P within this of a hull-segment face |
| `gouged_seg_ids` | ascending int list | length at most `GOUGES_PER_EPOCH_MAX` and at most `gouges_epoch` | block (emptied by guard 2) | The hull-segment ids this epoch's GOUGEs landed on: the whole gouge ledger the RUPTURE gate reads (ids only, no time) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| `magnitude` | int bp | ≤ `FOOTPRINT_MAX_GOUGE_BP` 2500 (registry, LSG:914, CITED) | Graph | M17 magnitude |

**Output Range:** per epoch at most 3 GOUGE and 1 RUPTURE, hence at most 4 hull-deposit M17 calls from strikes (plus the anchor GOUGE, which counts against `GOUGES_PER_EPOCH_MAX`). The earlier time clause `T ≥ gouge_T + STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS` is **deleted** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: consecutive strikes are at least `min_gap` = `STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS + UNFOLD_LO` = 395 apart (HE-F15), which exceeds `STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS` for any `UNFOLD_LO >= 1`, so the clause could never decide an outcome and a stored time would only add restore state. The ordering that matters, a GOUGE strictly earlier than the RUPTURE that needs it and never in the same strike (it forbids a same-act GOUGE-plus-RUPTURE and keeps the scrape ahead of the tear), is carried by membership in `gouged_seg_ids` tested before the append; the lint in the formula guards the one assumption it rests on.

**Example:** a lit seat stands beside a hull face. Strike one at `T = 1075`: P within 24 px of segment S, no GOUGE yet: `deposit = GOUGE` (`gouges_epoch = 1`, S appended to `gouged_seg_ids`), radial hairlines laid. Strike two at `T = 1550` on S, the seat having walked out of the disc (`hit_list = ∅`): `rupture_ok` (S ∈ `gouged_seg_ids`, `ruptures_epoch 0 < 1`, nobody in the disc): `deposit = RUPTURE`; had the seat stood still and been struck, `rupture_ok` is false and the deposit is `GOUGE` (if under 3) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. Strike three on S: `ruptures_epoch = 1`, so `GOUGE` (if under 3). A GOUGE sets the segment `fatigued`, an ENDOGENOUS creep that outlives the breach and sits under the Graph's own `W_MIN_WARNING_S` gate (HE-16 (4)); Horror neither alters nor shortens it.

**Registry candidate:** `hull_act_ladder`.

### HE-F18 Presentation interpolation alpha (render side only)

The presentation interpolation alpha formula is defined as:

`alpha5 = ((T_now − T_run) + tick_alpha) / HORROR_RUN_TICKS;   draw_pos = pos_prev + (pos_curr − pos_prev) × alpha5`

This is presentation arithmetic (real numbers, never hashed, never in a SIM block); the rig *pose* is stepped once per run and never tweened (PIPE row 14). It is owed to the Pipeline because the kernel's `tick_alpha` spans one tick, not five (C-HE-14).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `T_now`, `T_run` | int tick | — | Kernel | The presented tick and the tick of the most recent Horror run |
| `tick_alpha` | real | [0, 1] | Kernel | Fraction within the current tick |
| `pos_prev`, `pos_curr` | int px | — | `horror.view` | Positions at the previous and the latest run |

**Output Range:** `alpha5` in [0, 1]. **Example:** two ticks after the run with `tick_alpha` 0.5: `alpha5 = (2 + 0.5) / 5 = 0.5`, the draw position is the midpoint. A rig pose change on any tick that is not a multiple of 5 fails the cadence criterion.

**Registry candidate:** none (render-side; owed to the Pipeline).

## Edge Cases

Each case states the exact outcome and the rule it follows. Ids are `E-HE-n`. "Fizzle" always means the HE-17 outcome: RETREAT with no harm, no hairlines, no GOUGE or RUPTURE and no cooldown consumed, an already-laid touch-down smear stays (a fizzle before the hold stage lays none), the `"horror.tell"` draw stays consumed. Where a case settles something the Detailed Design left silent, it is marked *(orchestrator ruling)* and is repeated in the review questions. Cases that depend on an undesigned neighbour are marked *(provisional — <dep>)*.

#### Zero, maximum and out-of-range values

- **If a seat stands at exactly `R_SNAP_PX` (72 px) from P at resolution (E-HE-1)**: it is not hit. The disc is strict, `dx² + dy² < R_SNAP_PX²` with integers (`5184 < 5184` is false). The walk-out arithmetic of HE-F5 depends on the strictness, and an integer comparison keeps the outcome identical on replay.
- **If a seat that is already CRITICAL (integrity 3600) is struck (E-HE-2)**: `I = max(0, 3600 − 3200) = 400`, below `SUIT_HP_FAIL`, so FAILED → DOWNED → `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` → DEAD on Suit's ladder. The horror enters RETREAT after the hold. With no living, non-DOWNED candidate left, the target is the anchor (HE-13, HE-F8). At `cooldown_until_T` in BREACH it STALKs toward the anchor and runs anchor REACHes (hairlines and at most one GOUGE under `GOUGES_PER_EPOCH_MAX`, never RUPTURE or `apply_suit_damage`, HE-16 (3)) until SUBSIDING sends it to RETREAT. It never LURKs in BREACH (phase view). The ladder and the death are Suit's, the restore is Save/Load's (HE-18).
- **If `apply_suit_damage` would take integrity below 0 (a patched suit at 2800 struck again) (E-HE-3)**: integrity clamps at 0 (`I_k = max(0, …)`, HE-F3); the mutator is Class B, clamp-never-reject, so Horror never sees a rejection and has no error path to write.
- **If the type row has `UNFOLD_LO` or `UNFOLD_HI` that is not a multiple of 5, `UNFOLD_HI < UNFOLD_LO`, or `UNFOLD_LO` below the HE-F5 bound (E-HE-4)**: the data load fails with a lint error and the type row is refused; nothing rounds silently. A silent round-up would bias every telegraph by 1 to 4 ticks at the exact bound that sizes the walk-out.
- **If `STRIKE_BP` is outside 3001..3500, `SUIT_PATCH_CAP_BP − STRIKE_BP` is below `SUIT_HP_FAIL`, or `STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS` is at or below `STAGGER_TICKS` (E-HE-5)**: the data load fails the registered bounds (HE-F3, the third being the one-strike-never-kills-a-patched-suit bound) or the cooldown invariant (HE-F15) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. A value outside the interval changes the hits-to-FAILED count, which is Suit's rung design, not Horror's to retune.
- **If a seat walks into the horror's mouth during a BREACH and the lit seat is already within `REACH_RANGE_PX` at the run the cooldown is clear (E-HE-6)**: the REACH begins on that run (conditions (a) to (f) of HE-14 hold); the telegraph is still at least `UNFOLD_LO` ticks and P is the seat's foot point at lock. A close approach shortens the horror's walk, never its warning.
- **If no waypoint is at least `LURK_STANDOFF_PX` from every living seat (a small body, seats on all sides) (E-HE-7)**: the horror holds its position and does nothing (the cornered rule of HE-11); it deposits no harm and lays no marks while still. A cornered horror is a stationary hazard, never a pursuer.
- **If a ring insert finds no SMEAR to evict (E-HE-8)**: unreachable by construction, because the per-kind quotas (HE-30) leave at least `MAX_MARKS − n_other_max` = 19 ring slots to SMEAR; a fixture that forces it fires the defensive assertion, which evicts the lowest `mark_id` of any kind and increments `horror_ring_invariant`, a defect signal and never a gameplay state *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.
- **If more than `MARKS_PER_RUN_MAX` marks would be inserted in one run (E-HE-9)**: the first `MARKS_PER_RUN_MAX` are inserted in the order STRUCK, HAIRLINES, RIME, SMEAR and the rest are dropped and counted `horror_marks_withheld`. A run can legitimately want three (an ingress with smear, rime and hairlines); the cap bounds the dirty keys per tick (`MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` 256).

#### Simultaneous rules

- **If the predicate flips false between REACH begin and REACH end (the seat pod-hops, the breach is cleared early, the phase returns to CALM) (E-HE-10)**: the REACH fizzles at its last tick. The tip rested on P for the whole telegraph, a touch-down smear laid at the hold stage is a true mark and stays (a fizzle before the hold stage laid none), and the cooldown is not consumed, so a fizzle cannot starve the next legitimate REACH (HE-17).
- **If `reach_end_T` falls in SUBSIDING and the seat's section is still in `locus_body` (E-HE-11)**: the REACH resolves; the predicate holds in SUBSIDING for sections in `locus_body`, so `tell_window_contract` passes; the state then goes RETREAT. With `SUBSIDE_CANCELS_REACH` 1 it fizzles instead (the merciful variant, off by default; C-HE-10).
- **If a STRIKE is eligible for both GOUGE and RUPTURE on the same segment (E-HE-12)**: one hull deposit per STRIKE; RUPTURE when `rupture_ok` (which now requires an empty hit list), else GOUGE (HE-F17). The scrape (GOUGE) is always a separate, earlier, told act than the tear *(orchestrator ruling: HE-16 is silent on the tie)*.
- **If a REACH resolves on a seat that is already CRITICAL and staggered, beside a gouged hull face (E-HE-56)**: the seat is in the disc, so the hit list is not empty, `rupture_ok` is false and the deposit is a GOUGE (or none, if `GOUGES_PER_EPOCH_MAX` is spent); the strike's `apply_suit_damage` takes the seat from 3600 to 400 and FAILED as in E-HE-2, and no breach opens beside the dying seat. The compound "CRITICAL, staggered, plus a hull rupture" is excluded by construction. If the same seat is outside the disc, a RUPTURE may be issued and the Graph raises its own told breach clock (RUPTURE sets the segment to `RUPTURE_HP` 3600: decompression takes about 131 s at the initial hole, LSG:707), which a CRITICAL walk-only seat (`V_WALK` 2, about 15 000 px in that time) can outwalk or patch; AC-HE-133 asserts both halves *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. The miss-only RUPTURE rule is a user ruling (Open Question 2) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*
- **If one run wants a RUPTURE, a GOUGE and a CHILL while the window already holds 6 accepted calls (E-HE-13)**: the priority RUPTURE, GOUGE, CHILL applies within `MAX_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN`, then the window budget of 8 binds; RUPTURE and GOUGE are issued (window total 8), the CHILL is withheld (its rime mark is still laid) and `horror_footprints_withheld` increments. Harm outranks bookkeeping.
- **If a weld or cut changes the layout on the same tick a REACH resolves (E-HE-14)**: the locked P stays the stored integer; the same-room test (HE-15) and the closed-leaf test (HE-19) are re-evaluated against the layout as it is at resolution. A seat whose room now differs from P's room is not hit, so a layout change can only spare a seat, never reach one through a new wall.
- **If TELL ends in an abort on the same run a TRANSIT arrival completes, or at any earlier run while the record is outbound (E-HE-15)**: the abort is evaluated before the arrival (guard 5 precedes the TRANSIT exit), so the arrival is **cancelled**: no E6, no body in the player's ship, no new mark; the record heads back to `from_body_id` (the home body with `RETURN_HOME` 1) after `RETURN_DELAY_TICKS`, and an ingress mark already laid stays (HE-20). No strike, no mark burst *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; the earlier text had the record arrive as LURK, which injected a CALM presence and marks into the ship that had just held its fire)*.
- **If a second TELL begins while the record is in RETREAT from the previous breach (E-HE-16)**: RETREAT → LURK → STALK on the new `tell_epoch`; it never skips LURK, and the per-epoch counters (`strikes_epoch`, `gouges_epoch`, `ruptures_epoch`, `chill_epoch_dj`, `chill_deposits_epoch`) reset at the first run that sees the new `tell_epoch` (lazy reset, transition guard 2), and `gouged_seg_ids` empties with them *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.
- **If Attention's phase changes on a tick on which `horror.step` does not run (E-HE-17)**: Horror reads the view as committed by Attention's most recent run and acts at its next run, at most 4 ticks later (HE-4). A REACH can therefore begin up to 4 ticks after BREACH entry plus the approach; the delay only ever lengthens the warning.
- **If a seat is STAGGERED during a REACH (E-HE-18)**: the Controller freezes it and ignores input for `STAGGER_TICKS` 30 (PCI:170; STAGGERED is not the CRITICAL walk-only cap). A seat staggered at REACH start whose walk command is issued at tick 54 still clears at 90 (91 on a diagonal octant) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: HE-F5 tick-indexing convention, G4)*. A seat staggered mid-walk loses the frozen ticks plus a re-command, and clears only on draws long enough to absorb them. Horror does not adapt (no stored state). The stagger is itself Suit's or the Controller's told consequence (another axis reaching CRITICAL, or friendly harm). A strike-two stagger cannot overlap the next REACH because `STRIKE_COOLDOWN_TICKS` 300 > `STAGGER_TICKS`.

#### Degenerate strategies

- **If a seat closes a door leaf between itself and the horror (E-HE-19)**: the leaf is solid to the horror (`solid_in`), it opens no door, and the straight segment from its foot point to P must not cross a closed leaf (HE-19), so no strike lands through it. Counterplay by design (HE-26); the price is the task and the ship under STRESS.
- **If the highest-`lux_pm` seat is in a room with no open route from the horror (E-HE-20)**: HE-13 still names that seat the target. The goal room is chosen over the full adjacency (closed leaves counted for distance), the movement uses the open-leaf graph, and the horror stops in the last reachable room at the closed leaf and begins no REACH (HE-14 condition (f) fails: `connected(horror foot, P)` is false across a closed leaf, and the distance test (d) alone would not stop it) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier text cited a "segment test" that was not among conditions (a) to (e))*. It does not switch to a reachable but dimmer seat, because that would be a stored preference *(orchestrator ruling: HE-25 is silent on an unreachable target)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **It camps at the sill:** on the run it first becomes stationary there it lays one HAIRLINES mark on the leaf face (`held_at_leaf`, HE-26; no M17 call, no hull damage) and stays while that seat is the target and the phase is BREACH; the door is a visible, priced refuge, not a free one (delegated ruling G2, Open Question 33). A sealed seat is immune to the body; the Graph's own clocks and Attention's STRESS keep running. The share of told breaches with zero harm to a seat that stayed in the locus body is a playtest metric (30 to 60 % is the band; above 60 %, hull and ship consequences rise before any harm number does).
- **If the seat goes dark and stands still (E-HE-21)**: the target is the anchor on the next run (HE-13). The anchor REACH lays hairlines and, by default, one hull GOUGE under `GOUGES_PER_EPOCH_MAX` (HE-16 (3)); it never calls `apply_suit_damage` and never issues RUPTURE. Going dark spares the body and costs the ship, and every lit-only channelled verb is unavailable for the breach and the subside (about `BREACH_MIN_TICKS` + `SUBSIDE_TICKS` = 2100 ticks, 35 s). The anchor must lie within `HULL_CONTACT_PX` of a hull face for the GOUGE; if it does not, hairlines only.
- **If a dark seat stands within `R_SNAP_PX` of the anchor point at an anchor REACH's resolution (E-HE-22)**: no `apply_suit_damage` (an anchor REACH never harms a body, HE-16 (3)); the limb rests beside the seat and the hull takes the GOUGE. The seat has not been hunted, but a limb on its boots is not a safe place; whether a dark seat parked on the anchor should become targetable is an Open Question (the game-designer's `TOUCH_PX` guard was not adopted).
- **If the seat switches its lamp off after a REACH has begun (E-HE-23)**: P is already locked and stored; the strike resolves at P and hits every living seat in the disc, whether or not it was the target (HE-15). The lamp-off only changes the *next* run's target; nothing is remembered. Flapping the lamp to steer the horror costs the player the re-approach and cannot move a locked point.
- **If a seat stands in a thrown Chemlight pool (E-HE-24)**: the pool lights the seat, its `lux_pm` reaches `LIT_ON_PM` (a fresh Chemlight pool reads 700, so a seat standing in it is lit even with its own lamp off), and the seat is a candidate like any lit seat (HE-F8) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. The pool is never itself a target (a pool is not a seat). The glowstick works as a *work-light*: light the job, then step out of the pool dark; it does not pull the horror (HE-26).
- **If a seat walks through the horror (no collision) (E-HE-25)**: nothing happens in CALM, TELL or between REACHes; a rooted horror in REACH can be walked through, which may be an escape route out of a dead end. Accepted as a playtest item (Body's BDY-17: actors do not collide; the draw order decides how the overlap reads).
- **If a seat is in a dead-end room whose floor span is below `2 × R_SNAP_PX` (144 px) with a single exit (E-HE-26)**: it can be struck without a legal walk-out. This is an authoring defect, not a Horror rule: Derelict Authoring owes a lint (no dead-end room with floor span below 144 px and a single exit) and DA-29's wording note; Horror does not special-case it.
- **If a seat carries a module and a REACH begins (E-HE-27)**: the carrier needs 72 ticks (73 diagonal) against 40 available and is caught unless it drops the cargo within the reaction budget or `UNFOLD_TICKS ≥ 130` (11 of 18 draws), so a carrier who keeps the cargo is struck on 7 of 18 draws (38.9 %). Intended: hauling is where the horror bites and **dropping the cargo is the counterplay** (HE-24, C-HE-8); if the drop returns `OBSTRUCTED` (no free cell) the carrier is caught like a stationary seat, a lint case for Derelict Authoring and a question to Interaction (Open Question 7); the intended-price framing is a user ruling and the ruled lever is `R_SNAP_PX` 48 (HE-F5) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.
- **If a horror is in derelict A's `a_spine` during a carry (E-HE-59)** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R21)*: it is legal; nothing excludes a horror from the spine at the MVP (HE-26, answering Boarding ASK-BS-H1). The spine is a single 3-wide corridor with no side step, so the carrier's exits are the ruled ones and no new one is added: drop the cargo and walk back the way it came (a walker outpaces `V_HUNT_MPX`, HE-24; a horror ahead in the corridor is walked away from, not past, unless it is rooted in REACH, E-HE-25), stand still and go dark if no other light reaches the seat (HE-13), or shut a door behind it (HE-26). A carrier who keeps the cargo is caught as in E-HE-27 (7 of 18 draws). Horror adds no bypass and does not ask for a wider spine; if a spine end is a dead end that fails the E-HE-26 lint, that is Derelict Authoring's defect, not a Horror rule. Boarding's BS-13 precondition is answered "it may" (Interactions row Boarding & Salvage).
- **If a seat uses a tool on the horror, including a `tool_suppressant` placed by a test or debug fixture (E-HE-28; the item is not in the MVP start kit, user ruling X6)**: the verb fails `INVALID_TARGET` (IA-39); the item has no effect at MVP (AT-19) and `repel(origin, T)` is never called; the horror's state does not change; nothing is rewarded and nothing drops. Its acceptance criteria read NOT ASSESSED, never PASS (HE-39). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

#### Layout, weld and cut

- **If a weld joins two bodies during a TELL or BREACH (E-HE-29)**: `locus_body` is the body in force at the call tick: recorded at TELL entry and replaced only by an AT-14 fallback (LOCUS_FALLBACK); a weld alone does not change it (AT-14); the horror's walkable graph does (recomputed on the `layout.state` change), so it may path across the new join. If the record's own section was in the absorbed body, its body-frame points (`pos_px`, `locked_pt`, `ingress_anchor_px`) are re-based by `frame_shift_px` on the next run and `body_id` becomes the survivor (HE-22, `offset_seen_px`) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. Strikes still require `exogenous_permitted(actor_section(seat))`, so a seat in a section outside `locus_body` is never hit.
- **If a cut removes the section the record stands in from its body (E-HE-30)**: `body_id` is re-derived from `body_of(section_id)` and the body-frame points are re-based by the new `section_offset(section_id) − offset_seen_px` (HE-22) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*; if the section is gone the record goes TRANSIT with an egress mark and re-enters its home body by the HE-20 rule. Horror has no cut verb and never interrupts a cut (MSA-22) *(provisional — Modular Ship Assembly designed in this run)*.
- **If a Layout read returns NONE or UNKNOWN mid-path (E-HE-31)**: the record holds its last waypoint for at most `HOLD_MAX_RUNS` 12, then goes to LURK in CALM or TELL, stays in STALK holding position in BREACH, and goes to RETREAT in SUBSIDING (HE-25; never LURK in BREACH) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. UNKNOWN is never read as "unlit" or as "open" (HE-25). A REACH already in flight is not cancelled by a Layout change; the locked point is a stored integer.
- **If a door closes on the cached path (E-HE-32)**: the edge is excluded, the cache is recomputed once (`version(section_id)` of the section changed, C-HE-13 closed), and if the step reports no movement for `BLOCKED_RUNS_MAX` consecutive runs the cached hop is discarded and the path recomputed once more; a record still without movement after a second `BLOCKED_RUNS_MAX` runs is wedged and goes to RETREAT (HE-25) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- **If no legal ingress entry exists (E-HE-33)**: the record stays in TRANSIT and retries every run; the diagnostic `horror_ingress_pending` is raised. A record still unregistered after the first Horror run at or after BREACH entry is a **cry-wolf defect** (the breach has no teeth; a record that registers on that run, at most 4 ticks after BREACH entry by run alignment, E-HE-17, is not) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*, an acceptance criterion and an authoring signal, not a rule; the harm gates still require a registered record in the locus body, so nothing is struck. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* **The rule relaxes before BREACH:** at `tell_enter_T + INGRESS_DELAY_TICKS + STALK_VISIBLE_FLOOR_TICKS` (540) the entry falls back to the nearest legal hull anchor (HE-F9) and the record registers at the first Horror run at or after 600 (exactly 600 when `tell_enter_T` mod 5 = 0, at most 604 otherwise) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*, so the cry-wolf case is reachable only when every hull room of the locus body holds a living seat (AC-HE-44).
- **If the Layout chosen entry becomes illegal during the `INGRESS_MARK_LEAD_TICKS` window (a seat enters that room) (E-HE-34)**: the entry is re-chosen by the same rule; the first hairline mark stays (it is a true mark of where the web touched). The arrival is delayed to the new mark time plus the lead.
- **If a seat stands in an adjacent room within 72 px of P across a one-tile wall (E-HE-35)**: not hit. The disc is 1.5 tiles wide and wider than a wall, so the same-room test (HE-15) is the rule that stops a strike through a wall (Pillar 1: no lie about the world), and HE-14 condition (f) stops the REACH from *starting* when the horror stands on the far side of that wall, so no limb is drawn through it *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* A seat in the adjacent room across an **open, passable sill** is not hit either: the strike is strictly same-room (HE-15) even though a REACH could begin toward it (HE-19); AC-HE-27 carries the fixture.
- **If a leaf closes while the horror's foot box overlaps it (E-HE-57)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: Layout's close-safety counts seats' foot boxes, not the horror's, so the close succeeds; on the next run guard 4 ejects the horror along `−last_octant` in 8 px steps (at most 32 px, at most four `solid_in` queries) to the first free position (`horror_door_ejected`), or wedges it to RETREAT when none is free (`horror_wedged`); it is never embedded in the leaf (HE-26, AC-HE-137).
- **If a REACH is resolving while the layout changes under a linked seat (E-HE-58)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: `linked` (HE-19) flips once as a discrete event when a door opens or closes and never from a pixel-level move, so the standoff and step-away rules cannot oscillate at a doorway; the REACH already begun is untouched (the locked point is a stored integer).

#### Save, restore and dormancy

- **If a restore lands INTO REACH and `exogenous_permitted` still holds (E-HE-36)**: the record keeps its stored `locked_pt`, its `"horror.tell"` counter and its rooted state, and the remaining telegraph is exactly `reach_end_T − T_restore`: restore neither shortens nor lengthens it and never rerolls the draw. A save taken inside a REACH is Save/Load's SL-5 deferral case *(provisional — Save/Load: autosave is a soft gate that defers during a told breach)*, so the unfair case (a reload into the last ticks of a telegraph) is not Horror's to repair. Lengthening the telegraph on restore was considered (the game-designer's GF10 floor) and **rejected**: it mutates the simulation at restore time, breaks save-equivalence (G4) and SL-15's idempotent, no-clamp restore, and would make `snapshot(restore(snapshot()))` differ.
- **If a restore lands INTO REACH and the predicate no longer holds (the autosave predates a pod-hop or the breach ended) (E-HE-37)**: RETREAT with no strike, no hairlines and no GOUGE or RUPTURE (HE-41); the touch-down smear already in the ring stays.
- **If a restore lands INTO STRIKE (E-HE-38)**: the strike's `apply_suit_damage` and M17 calls were made on the entry run and are part of Suit's and the Graph's restored state; STRIKE is only the held snap pose and restoring it issues no second hit. `strike_T`, `seats_hit[]` and `strike_hull_seg` are persisted while the record is in STRIKE (HE-41), so the hit cue is not replayed *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.
- **If a restore lands INTO TRANSIT with `transit_until_T` in the past (E-HE-39)**: legal; the record arrives at the next run with a legal entry. A TRANSIT record that also has a registered Emitter subject, or a non-TRANSIT record without a legal section, fails restore as `STATE_INCOHERENT`.
- **If any restored value is out of range (`strikes_epoch` above `STRIKES_EPOCH_MAX` 13, a mark ring longer than `MAX_MARKS`, a position outside its section, `acc_mpx` ≥ 1000) (E-HE-40)**: restore fails hard with the matching `HORROR_RESTORE_*` code (`RANGE`, `MARK_RING`, `SECTION_UNKNOWN`) and the Save/Load layer refuses the file; nothing is clamped (SL-15). A clamp would hide a corrupt save behind a plausible world.
- **If the player dies and the game restarts from the newest autosave (E-HE-41)**: `horror.state` restores with the WORLD blocks, including the `"horror.tell"` counter, exactly. The counter continues from its saved value, but the stream is re-keyed by the restore's `restore_epoch` through the kernel's `rng_derive_epoch` (HE-42, user ruling X8/D16, delegated ruling R1; *Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: kernel name*), so **the replay does not draw the same `UNFOLD_TICKS` sequence**: a death or loss of play is not a way to scout the telegraph lengths of the next breach, and (Save/Load's constraint) quit and death offer no reroll oracle (a Save & Quit then Continue of the newest `QUIT` generation carries the epoch and replays the same draws, so quitting cannot reroll; a death-stub restore advances it; delegated ruling R1 as amended by R13/R14) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R13/R14)*. The marks, positions and every physical fact are bit-identical to uninterrupted play given the pair (save, `restore_epoch`) (G4); the draws themselves stay inside the legal range, so the fairness bound of HE-F5 holds at every re-keyed draw; what the player learned stays in their head (Pillar 5) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.
- **If an autosave fires during CALM while a LURK stands at the standoff (E-HE-42)**: no Horror gate is needed; CALM is non-lethal by construction (HE-10), no lethal horror is ever adjacent to a seat in a CALM save, and SL-5 is unchanged. If the standoff rule is ever relaxed, SL-5 must be revisited.
- **If a record's body is unloaded (E-HE-43)**: the record is frozen, its marks do not age, its disturbances do not run and nothing fast-forwards on reload (HE-21, LS-17), **with one exception: a new `tell_epoch` whose locus body is not that body sends the record to TRANSIT at once (HE-20, guard 2), even though its own body is unloaded, with an egress mark laid in the unloaded section (inserted, not ageing)**. A record in TRANSIT whose destination unloads waits. If an unloaded record is somehow woken with a `last_seen_tell_epoch` that is two or more epochs old (a restored older save), the lazy reset zeroes its five epoch counters and empties `gouged_seg_ids` before any act, so its caps are never bound by a stale epoch *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.

#### Budgets, registration and determinism

- **If `register_subject` (E6) returns false on a first registration (not a duplicate) (E-HE-44)**: the subject reads dark (`lux` 0, `lit` false); that is a **budget defect**, a failing criterion and a lint, never a gameplay state. The horror still moves and strikes (its harm does not depend on being a subject); the defect is that nothing can light it.
- **If a stub requests more footprints than the window allows (E-HE-45)**: at most `HE_FOOTPRINT_BUDGET` 8 per window are made, at most `MAX_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN` 4 per run; the rest are not attempted (a trace is a moment, not a queue) and counted `horror_footprints_withheld`. In nominal play the counter is 0; a nonzero count in a build is a defect.
- **If a mark insert mutates a packed array element without declaring its dirty key (E-HE-46)**: Godot 4.7 packed-array element writes do not call the setter, so the kernel would miss the change and `state_hash` would differ after a restore. Every mutation of the ring, the disturbance list and the record declares explicit dirty keys; a lint and a save-equivalence criterion enforce it.
- **If the same `world_seed`, the same (save, `restore_epoch`) pair and the same inputs are replayed (E-HE-47)**: the strike lengths, positions, marks, disturbance timings and `state_hash` are identical (HE-42). Randomness is `rng_derive` for the REACH length only; mark seeds, LURK waypoints and L3 timing are pure integer hashes of ids, the run index and `HE_HASH_SEED`.

#### Hallucination and the Perception Filter

- **If the Filter hides a real horror's body from one seat (Vertical Slice class (a), "hidden real entity") (E-HE-48)**: the marks, L3 state, M17 writes, `apply_suit_damage` calls and `horror.view` are bit-identical with and without the hide (HE-32). The limb tip and body are undrawn for that seat, but the touch-down smear at P is a true mark the Filter may not edit, so "a smear appeared under my feet" falsifies the hide at a cost of looking at the floor. The hide collapses when `horror.view` reports a state outside {LURK, RETREAT} *(provisional — Sanity & Hallucination Rules designed in this run)*.
- **If a phantom horror co-occurs with a real one (E-HE-49)**: the phantom has no record, no mark, no footprint, no registration and no strike (HE-32); the real one proceeds on its own clock. A seat that flees the phantom can walk into the real disc; "is the floor marked?" is the check, and TellAudit records nothing for the phantom (AT E-53).
- **If the Filter shows a crewmate as a horror and a seat harms that crewmate (Vertical Slice class (c)) (E-HE-50)**: that is the friendly-harm act under Interaction and Sanity's gate, not Horror's; no Horror state changes. The rig presenter is only the substitute body; a real horror emits nothing and carries no beam, so the true beam remains a perfect discriminator (HE-2).
- **If a seat's own Sanity state makes it see violet fringes or false gauges (E-HE-51)**: Horror's marks, disturbances and positions are TRUTH channel and are not corruptible; the body can be omitted or wholly substituted only by the Filter, and marks never use flesh or violet (HE-31). Horror reads no `PERCEPTION_OUT` block (CR-12).

#### Co-op hooks (Vertical Slice and later)

- **If two lit seats are in the locus body (E-HE-52)**: the higher `lux_pm` wins; a tie goes to the lower seat id; a stateless argmax can alternate as lamps change, and a hysteresis would need a stored value (C-HE-19, an Open Question). A strike hits every living seat in the disc once, in ascending player id, each as its own `apply_suit_damage` call.
- **If a seat is DOWNED by a teammate (friendly harm) or by another cause during a REACH (E-HE-53)**: it is not "living, non-DOWNED" at resolution and is not hit; the REACH completes harmlessly and the record RETREATs. DOWNED and DEAD seats are never candidates (HE-18).
- **If a second record is requested while `HORROR_MAX_MVP` is 1 (E-HE-54)**: the request is refused by the data lint and the boot check; at runtime `S_h ≤ HORROR_SUBJECTS_MAX` 2 is a ceiling reserved for the transient during ingress. Rosters per ring and more types are Vertical Slice.
- **If a seat is in a different body from `locus_body` (E-HE-55)**: it is not a candidate in TELL, BREACH or SUBSIDING, is never hit, and is not the subject of the predicate (the predicate's argument is Body's `actor_section`, not the Graph's `section_of`). In CALM the candidates are the seats in the record's own body.

## Dependencies

Direction reads from Horror's side. "Depends on" means Horror cannot run without it; "named by" means the neighbour's own GDD already names Horror and its text is quoted in Interactions with Other Systems, so the edge is bidirectional. Hard means a missing neighbour stops the MVP loop; soft means a degraded but correct run. Status is the neighbour's at authoring time (2026-10-26/27): **designed**, **in flight** (a draft on disk), **undesigned-provisional** (no GDD; every assumption is `[PROVISIONAL]` and registered with the owner named).

**Provisional registrations (reflexion rule: a placeholder from an undesigned neighbour is marked and registered so the later value propagates).** The values Horror takes from or hands to neighbours, each owed to the registry with the owner named. *Refresh 2026-10-27 (design review):* the `loco_state` read (Controller, PCI:58 and :241), `hull_segments_near` and the door-state answer (`version(section_id)`; Layout), `emitters_near` (Emitter), the five-tick interpolation alpha (Pipeline line 204), the audio fact names (Audio AA-28) and the horror-subject budget number (Interaction and Derelict Authoring quote Horror's) are **published and no longer provisional**; still open: the registry spellings of `emitters_near` ("name provisional" in the Emitter's table), the `leak` input (Filter) and the `horror.view.debug` rows (Debug Overlay) (`door_passage` left this list when Layout published it; *Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix*). Registry candidates for Horror's own constants are written from the final GDD text, after the final revision, and are not appended here.

**Bidirectionality audit.** Every neighbour in the table above that names Horror (kernel, Graph, Emitter, Attention, Lighting, Failure & Trail, Suit, Interaction, Body, Layout, Save/Load, Derelict Authoring, Pipeline, Controller, Item DB, Assembly, Sanity, Web Map) appears with its counterpart text; the neighbours that do not yet name Horror (Boarding, HUD, Instruments, Debug Overlay, Depth Rings, Codex, Co-op, Chart Table, and the Controller beyond PCI row 16) get rows from this side only, and the missing counterpart is listed as an owed row in Cross-References.

## Tuning Knobs

Every Horror-owned value lives in the per-type data file keyed by `type_id` (HE-5, R26); no behavioural constant lives in code. Every default is a **`[PLACEHOLDER]`** pending playtest, and every row is a candidate for the registry, written from this final text by a later stage. **★ marks a value that a neighbour quotes or that a registered relation binds** (a cross-system fact: another GDD, the registry or an acceptance criterion outside this document reads it); an unstarred knob is Horror-internal. Durations are authored as integer counts of runs (`HORROR_RUN_TICKS` 5) and published in ticks; a duration that is not a multiple of 5 is a data-load failure (HE-4, E-HE-4). "Interacts with" names the formula or neighbour that binds the row; the formula, not the row, is the source of truth for any two-bound.

**The dials that move lethality are not in this table.** `STRIKE_BP` cannot change the hits-to-FAILED count inside its registered interval (HE-F3), so "make it more lethal" is a request to change the per-attempt miss rate `p`, the attempts per breach, the breaches per session (Attention's) or repair availability (Suit's). The first of these is telegraph legibility (HE-14 and the blind-legibility criteria), not a number.

#### Speeds and ranges

| Knob | Default | Unit | Safe range | Too high | Too low | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `V_CREEP_MPX` ★ | 1000 | milli-px/tick | 1000..1400 | STALK loses its dread and nears `V_HUNT_MPX`; the standoff is hard to hold | below 1000 a tick can have `v_eff` 0 and Body resets sub-pixel residue on a zero tick (BDY-12), so the floor stays 1000 until verified | HE-F6, HE-F7 (trail length), HE-F9, HE-F15 |
| `V_HUNT_MPX` ★ | 1500 | milli-px/tick | `V_CREEP_MPX`+1..1999 | the walker's margin shrinks toward 1 milli-px/tick at 1999 and cry-wolf flips to a chase; at 2000 the invariant `V_HUNT_MPX < V_WALK × 1000` and DA-29 break | the horror rarely reaches a lit idle seat inside a minimum breach; fewer attempts | HE-F6 invariant (`< V_WALK × 1000`), HE-F13, HE-F15, `V_CARRY` |
| `R_SNAP_PX` ★ | 72 | px | 48..96, and below `REACH_RANGE_PX` (HE-F14 chain, asserted at load) | corridors fully covered; dodging needs room; carrier arithmetic worsens | one-tile disc, very dodgeable, tile-aligned | HE-F2, HE-F5, Interaction `REACH_PX` 72 (kept equal on purpose), E-HE-26; 48 is the ruled lever (user ruling on the 'drop the cargo' price; the default stays 72); at 48 a carrier who keeps the cargo is struck on 2 of 18 draws, not 7 (HE-F5) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| `REACH_RANGE_PX` ★ | 96 | px | `R_SNAP_PX + 1` to `min(LURK_STANDOFF_PX − 1, 3 × H_crew)` (73..159 at the placeholders) | limb off-screen at high zoom and the standoff gap shrinks | the horror must be nearly adjacent; feels timid | HE-F14 (`R_SNAP_PX < · < LURK_STANDOFF_PX`, `≤ 3 × H_crew`; asserted at load for every combination), HE-F13 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| `LURK_STANDOFF_PX` ★ | 192 | px | 144..288, and above `REACH_RANGE_PX` (HE-F14 chain) | never visible at `k_auto + 2` | the CALM presence reads as a stalker | HE-F14, HE-F9 (`D_entry_max`), L3 radius; Sanity `HIDE_SAFE_R` 288 (SN-22 boot assertion `HIDE_SAFE_R > LURK_STANDOFF_PX`, SANITY_BOOT_HIDE_STANDOFF): at 288 Sanity refuses to boot; retune together |
| `HULL_CONTACT_PX` | 24 | px | 12..48 | every wall-side strike gouges | hull acts almost never accompany strikes | HE-F17, `ANCHOR_REACH_GOUGES` |
